# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

Please choose versions by [Semantic Versioning](http://semver.org/).

* MAJOR version when you make incompatible API changes,
* MINOR version when you add functionality in a backwards-compatible manner, and
* PATCH version when you make backwards-compatible bug fixes.

## Unreleased

- fix: **the per-bucket classification is now written by the sweep reader that computed it, instead of retyped by the manager from the table it rendered.** `manager-loop`, `manager-status` and `manager-drive` each staged `{"<bucket>": [...]}` through a caller-typed heredoc, because the classification existed only as a rendered table whose names elide to `…` for width — so the artifact the drive leg's clause (0) gates on reached the store through a transcription, and the read-only `supervisor:manager-sweep-reader` could not write it at all. The agent now persists it via `--write-buckets` in the same step it writes the payload, and `--save` finds that file the same way it already found the payload: no path named, no set typed. ⚠️ **Measured, not inferred:** a caller-typed set in a synonym vocabulary (`hold` / `orphan` / `ready`, against the runbook's `blocked/hold` / `problem` / `ready-to-start`) is accepted **verbatim** by `bucket_shape_error`, which validates *shape only* — so a hand-typed set could land a second vocabulary in the record, which is the drift already on disk. ⚠️ **The `--write-buckets` call itself is unchanged and still refuses a malformed set** — the empty-list hole (`{"done": []}` passing a check whose message says "non-empty") is confirmed still closed at 0.92.1. ⚠️ **`--save` now defaults `--buckets` to the gate's own bucket path when the caller names none**, matching how it already finds the payload; an explicitly named path keeps the strict refusal, because a caller that names one is asserting it exists. ⚠️ **The *"this sweep's own sweep-reader computed"* clause is deliberately kept** in all three commands: it no longer accompanies a hand-typed set, and it is now purely the rule that the caller must not re-derive the classification — removing it to satisfy a grep would delete the prohibition the fix depends on.

## v0.93.0

- feat: **every fleet and manager round now prints how many approved tasks have not started and how long the oldest has waited, so approved rows stop sitting unopened unseen.** The sweep classifies *live sessions*, so it is structurally blind to a row nobody ever opened — there is no session to sweep from, and no channel reported it. Measured 2026-10-01 21:00–21:40: approved rows sat unopened (`Build claude-interactive` approved 14:06 with no session at all, Manager Layer's four answered approvals) and no round showed it; the operator found it only by asking *"we have still not more task open"*. Live run of the new script (2026-10-01 22:08): `approved, not started: 16 · oldest 1d14h (Check the ORB DE40 AUM 6-Month Cap Before It Passes)` — and the head count moved 12 → 16 across ten minutes as sessions started and stopped, which is the metric working rather than noise. `scripts/approved-not-started.py` is the reverse index for that gap, in the shape `orphan-candidates.py` already uses: it walks the task files, and reads frontmatter only (task bodies quote these keys in prose, so a whole-file scan approves a task on the strength of a sentence *about* approval). A row counts when its frontmatter carries `approved_at` — the field `vault-cli task approve` writes and nothing else does, so it is the vault's own record of the approval rather than a proxy inferred from `status`/`phase` — with a non-terminal `status` and no **live** `claude_session_id`. ⚠️ **The tick is a 30-minute script constant, never a per-call flag:** both callers must agree on it, and a per-call override is how two rounds start disagreeing about the same row. Rows past the tick are listed with their owning manager (the registry name matching a goal title, a `<X> Manager` suffix allowed — the same convention `fleet-board.py` uses to place a session under its parent), and `unmanaged` is reported as a fact about the fleet, never as a failure. ⚠️ **The "approved" predicate here is deliberately NOT the runbook's "approved and unowned" clause** — `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` forbids hard-coding *that* one because the two vaults' runbooks disagree about it, and it decides which rows a manager may **spawn** on. This script answers a different question from the approval's own record, so the disagreement does not reach it. ⚠️ **Liveness is delegated, never re-derived:** one `session-liveness.py --list --json` call and a set membership test, because two readers over one registry directory is the defect that file's own header records. ⚠️ **A failed read is `unknown`, never `0`** (`docs/pane-reads.md` § The rule) — an unreadable registry prints `approved, not started: unknown` on **both** streams and exits non-zero — stdout so a caller that captured only stdout renders a failed read rather than a clean round, and because *"no approved rows are waiting"* and *"I could not ask"* must not render the same. `commands/fleet-loop.md` gains § The approved-not-started reading (printed with the marker line, beside the colour census and the saturation ratio) and `commands/manager-loop.md` prints the same line with its table — deliberately **wider than the topic**, since the gap it closes is a row no topic manager owns. `scripts/tests/test_approved_not_started.py` covers the 29/31-minute threshold in both directions, the `unknown`-on-unreadable-registry path, frontmatter-only reading, and the manager join.
- fix: **the fleet-wide worker target counted the wrong population — it now counts worker sessions.** `spawn.maxConcurrent` and the manager ticks read `live-workers.py`, which is stamped only for **headless in-process** workers (`server/supervisor.mjs` writes `mode: 'headless'`, from `server/agent-loop.mjs`) — so it answered **0 while 11 interactive worker tabs were live** (measured 2026-10-01), with the four stamps on disk 13 h–4 days stale. A target that reads 0 on a busy fleet is **inert**: managers always see "below target" and always propose, and the cap never binds, so the feature would have done nothing on the fleet it was written for. ⚠️ **The unit is now pinned: a worker session is one present in BOTH the live registry (`~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json` — the liveness authority, its entry deleted on exit) AND the spawn ledger, which is what makes it a session the supervisor opened rather than a manager or one of the operator's own.** Measured: 26 live registry sessions, **18 worker sessions**, all four managers absent from the ledger. `scripts/worker-sessions.py --count` and `server/worker-sessions.mjs` carry the one definition, and `supervisor.mjs`'s cap uses it in place of `listLive()` — so the cap and the target cannot disagree, which was the whole point of making them one number. ⚠️ **Two limits, stated rather than hidden:** the ledger's own `status` is not a liveness source (824 of its 1075 entries read `running` against 26 live sessions), and a manager ever opened through `spawn_agent` would be counted, since it would hold a ledger record like any worker. ⚠️ **The worker-target task's live e2e would have passed vacuously on the old instrument** — `0 < 20` is trivially true on a fleet the operator would not call below target. Found by the Fleet Manager relaying Attention Routing's report; the same blindness is in the shipped cap, so part of this predates the target.

## v0.92.1

- fix: **`live-workers.py` gains `--count`, because the probe the worker target was wired to answered `1` for an idle fleet.** An empty heartbeat store prints `no live headless workers in <dir>` — to **stdout**, like every other line the script emits — so `--list | wc -l` counts that sentence and reports **one worker live** on a machine with none. Measured 2026-10-01 against the real store while preparing the worker target's live verification. ⚠️ **The error is invisible because 1 is a plausible count**, and it fails in the direction that makes the fleet look *busier* than it is — so a manager comparing it against the target would suppress a card it should have posted, which is exactly the silence the worker target exists to remove. `--count` writes the integer and nothing else; a non-zero exit still means the store could not be read, with its message on **stderr** where `$(...)` cannot capture it, so "could not check" never degrades into "zero workers". The three call sites that carried the wrong probe are corrected — `commands/manager-loop.md` (both the spawn-time cap check and the under-target trigger), `commands/fleet-loop.md`, and `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 5 — each naming `--count` and warning against `--list | wc -l` at the point of use. `scripts/tests/test_live_workers.py` adds six cases, including `test_list_line_count_is_wrong_for_an_empty_store`, which asserts the miscount **deliberately** so the reason the counting mode exists cannot be quietly forgotten.

## v0.92.0

- feat: **the fleet gains a worker target, and a manager whose tick finds nothing ready now proposes the next rows to approve while the fleet is under it.** `spawn.maxConcurrent` gains a **default of 20**: an absent key resolves to `DEFAULT_MAX_CONCURRENT`, `0` stays the off switch for unlimited, and an explicit integer is that integer. ⚠️ **The same number is both the hard cap `spawnAgent` enforces and the target the manager ticks read, and that is deliberate** — two keys (a cap and a target) were considered and rejected because they can disagree, and a fleet capped at 20 whose managers propose against 30 is a defect with no error on it. ⚠️ **This reverses the operator's 2026-09-27 ruling** (*"These limits are artificial and should be removed … For now there is no global limit"*), under which the key shipped unset and unset meant unlimited; the reversal is recorded at the constant's single home (`docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 5) rather than left with both statements standing, because a reader who finds only the earlier note concludes the fleet is unbounded, which is no longer true. The count is live **workers** from the heartbeat store (`scripts/live-workers.py`) — **not** `~/.claude/sessions/*.json`, whose registry also holds the operator's own sessions and the managers and would make a limit of 20 unusable (measured 2026-10-01: registry 16 sessions, heartbeat store 0 live workers). A manager tick **below** the target with no ready row posts ONE card naming the next most important goal-advancing unapproved rows, **ranked by `agents/manager-drive.md` clause (7)** — in that clause's order and within its cap, which stays its single home and is deliberately not restated here — with candidates claimed through `asked-ledger` so no row lands on two managers' cards; **at or above** the target it posts no card and opens nothing, and prints `at target: no card` so a suppressed tick is distinguishable from a tick that simply found nothing. ⚠️ **Ranked, never bulk** — operator ruling 2026-10-01: *"We have topics with 100 tasks and approving and starting all of them is not a good idea. So we should always look at the next important tasks we should approve and open."* Goal-level bulk approval is rejected. `commands/fleet-loop.md` prints `workers: <live>/<target>` on its marker line every round, and its saturation section's Open Questions 1–2 — which asked whether a *ratio* threshold should trigger a spawn — are now answered: the target replaces the ratio as the capacity signal and the ratio stays read-only, because a low ratio is a **human-queue** signal (at the 42% reading, 14 of 24 sessions were idle or waiting, most on the operator) while the target is a **load** signal. That earlier caution is about a target that *spawns*; this one *proposes* — the operator still answers, so the human queue is not lengthened by the rule.
- test: **the worker target's single home is now enforced, not merely asserted.** `scripts/check-worker-target.py`, wired into `precommit`, asserts three things: the home section exists at `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 5, every `commands/`/`agents/` file that names `maxConcurrent` carries a **pointer** to it rather than its own copy of the number, and the prose default and `DEFAULT_MAX_CONCURRENT` in `server/spawn-mode.mjs` **agree**. ⚠️ **The third is the half no amount of prose can satisfy** — moving the code constant without the doc, or the doc without the code, fails the check, and both surfaces stay internally consistent while they disagree, so nothing a reader sees is wrong. This repo has shipped exactly that failure: the cap was restated in `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-verify.md` and the manager runbook's Guardrail 2 until 2026-09-24, so one cap became four counters the day one home was edited and the others were not. ⚠️ **The home is identified by its heading and item, never by the `Spawn a worker item 5` pointer string** — measured while writing this: that string appears at two *other* places in the same file (lines 35 and 70, each citing the rule from a different topic), so a home check keyed on it passes even after the section it points at has been renamed or renumbered away. `scripts/tests/test_check_worker_target.py` pins both drift directions, the renamed-section and renumbered-item cases, and the negative control that a file not naming the constant is not required to point at it.

## v0.91.0

- feat: **one readiness ladder replaces the flat 9/10 spawn bar — a below-bar task is improved once and opens at 7 or better.** The bar was a single number with a single outcome: a row scoring 8 with zero hard-gate failures was held at `⛔ NOT READY` on the single-name path (`commands/open.md:342`) and carded by the drive leg, so an approved agent-filed row scoring 7–8 had no path to a session — the `--flagged` batch was the only carve-out (`commands/open.md:279`). Operator ruling 2026-10-01: *"< 9/10 => try to improve ... >= 7/10 => allow to approve and open after improvement"*. The four branches — **≥ 9 opens directly; < 9 runs one improvement pass then re-audits; ≥ 7 after the pass opens; < 7 posts one card and holds** — have **one home**, `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 2, referenced by `commands/open.md` § Step 1.5 Gate 2 and § Step 0.6, `agents/manager-drive.md` clause (1), `commands/ready.md` step 4 and the vault's `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md`. ⚠️ **Every branch is conditional on ZERO hard-gate failures** — the score decides *which* branch, the hard gates decide *whether the ladder runs at all*; the two conditions are joint, so a 9/10 carrying a failing hard gate is carded and held, not opened. ⚠️ **The improvement pass IS the existing bounded repair loop, not a second one** — its rounds, its stop condition (zero hard-gate failures) and its early-stop rule are unchanged, and the ladder changes only *which score opens a row*. The loop asks *"is there more structural repair to do?"*; the ladder asks *"may this row open?"* — merging them would spend rounds patching prose, which the measured evidence says converges on nothing (four audits of one unchanged row returned 6 → 7 → 8 → 8 with zero hard-gate failures at every round). ⚠️ **`UNFIXABLE:` is inverted.** It used to stop the loop and reach the operator verbatim; operator decision 2026-10-01 makes it the improvement pass's **first act** — **rewrite the unmeetable criterion into a falsifiable form, or split it into its own follow-up task**, then re-audit. The card is now the fallback, firing only when neither rewriting nor splitting works, or when the criterion is an operator-only decision. Two worked examples of the treatment were already on disk and are the pattern to match (`Build claude-interactive` SC4, rewritten from a precondition into a deliverable after an `UNFIXABLE` audit; `A Board Click on a Permission Card Bricks It` SC1 + SC3). ⚠️ **The probe stays read-only** — the improvement pass is the caller's edit *between* two probes, never the sub-agent's, so the ban on a mutating gate is preserved rather than lifted. ⚠️ **`phase: todo` rows are untouched**: refused at Gate 1 before any audit runs, never opened and never flipped. ⚠️ **The `--flagged` carve-out survives** — a flagged row below the threshold still opens with its gaps carried, because the operator's flag is already the answer the card would ask for; the improvement pass now runs first, so the flagged row opens *improved* rather than raw.
## v0.90.1

- feat: **a board answer now wakes the manager that posted the card, instead of waiting for its next tick.** `scripts/answered-watch.py` arms as a second, distinct `Monitor` and emits exactly `ANSWERED <item-id>` when the operator answers a card this session posted; on that line `manager-loop` and `fleet-loop` poll and act immediately rather than at the tick — up to 15 min later, and unbounded for a manager idle at its prompt. The signal is the store's own change stream (`GET /api/1.0/attention/stream`), which fires on the answer path: `pkg/attention-store-notifying.go` calls `notifier.Notify()` from `Answer` as well as from `Push`, so the latency is a round trip rather than a poll interval. ⚠️ **Two obvious sources are wrong, and both were measured before the design settled.** `GET /api/1.0/attention` — what `who-needs-me.py` reads — returns **open** items only and prunes dead askers, so an answered item is absent from it by construction and the line cannot be produced from that read path. Polling `GET /api/1.0/attention/history` costs **16,358,494 bytes / 19,167 items** per call: fine once per connect as a baseline, not viable as a poll. ⚠️ **`state == "answered"` is deliberately not the predicate** — the arm calls `answered-attribution.py`'s `operator_answered()`, so `NOT_OPERATOR_ANSWERED` fires nothing here exactly as it releases nothing in `attention-ask.py poll`, and the rule keeps its one home. Keying is on `producer_id`, so a card posted by a **different** session cannot wake this one. `scripts/tests/test_answered_watch.py` pins that as the negative probe, alongside the no-wake-for-a-pre-existing-answer property that keeps a manager restart from producing a burst of stale wakes — verified live against the real store, where a session with 170 answered items produced **zero** lines.
- fix: **the pre-dispatch gate no longer starves the act leg while rows sit ready-to-start or waiting-approval.** `evaluate()` decided `changed` from the digest alone, and `digest_of()` hashes status, phase, the Progress hash, session, liveness and stuck — none of which moves when a row merely sits approved and unstarted. Ready-to-start is therefore a **steady state**: the gate returned NO-CHANGE on every tick and the act leg dispatched 0 agents, so the rows never moved and the missing recommendation block never appeared. Measured 2026-10-01 on `Managers Spawn Interactive Claude Workers in the Cluster`: NO-CHANGE since 18:04 with 2 ready-to-start and 1 waiting-approval row on the board (durable record: `~/.claude/state/manager-predispatch/managers-spawn-interactive-claude-workers-in-the-cluster.json`). The verdict gains a third, independent reason to sweep: the caller's own `bucket_sets` — the only place a bucket exists, since the digest covers the tracked set and the snapshot schema has no bucket concept — now exits 10 when it places any row in `ACTIONABLE_BUCKETS` (`ready-to-start`, `waiting-approval`). ⚠️ **It is deliberately not folded into `digest_of`:** that function's contract is "what the sweep would render", a pure function of the tracked set, and a caller-supplied classification has no business moving it. ⚠️ **The saving returns on its own** — a row the act leg actually opened reclassifies as progressing on the next sweep, so the clause is neither a latch nor a permanent cost. A missing or malformed half reads as no actionable rows and changes no verdict, which is the safe direction: an absent classification costs a replay, never a wrong dispatch. `scripts/tests/test_manager_predispatch.py` gains `TestActionablePassThrough` — both pass-through directions, the negative control (an unconditional `changed = True` would satisfy the positive alone while disabling the gate), the absent half, a malformed half, and the recovery.
- fix: **`manager-drive` reports one ranked `Next` block and posts exactly one approve-card per run.** `agents/manager-drive.md` clause (7) ranked only the decision set and rendered it under a `Card` block, so the rows the operator actually acts on — `🚀 Ready` (ready-to-start) alongside `✅ Ready for approval` (waiting-approval) — were never ranked together; and `commands/manager-drive.md` never posted the card at all, which replaced a silent hold with an invisible one. The report now ends with the round's **one** ranked rendering, `<n>. <task name> — <bucket> — <score>/10 — <one-line why>`, capped at 5, ordered by clause (7)'s three ranks, with `<why>` naming the goal SC or the literal `no goal SC` and the grouped counts line below; the `Card` block carries the posting payload only, never a second copy of the rows, because two renderings of one decision set is the second counter a `grep` cannot tell from a real one. The command posts exactly ONE `post-batch` card over the round's waiting-approval row set — zero and one-per-row both fail — and opens `To open` rows via `/supervisor:ready`, one invocation per row, which re-audits idempotently and puts its verdict line into the run's output. ⚠️ **The mode binding stays stated at that site** even though the open is delegated — that is what `check-spawn-mode` asserts and what `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 6 requires.

## v0.90.0

- feat: **the fleet board gains an `Unblocks` column, and its transcript-age column is named `Inactive`.** Each live session now reads one of four values — `operator-keystroke` (it ended its turn on a closer only the operator can clear, taken from `who-needs-me.py`'s own `rendered_panels()` so the board's set and the operator's `Needs you` list are the same set *by construction* rather than by agreement), `reap/close` (its anchored task is `completed`), `nudge` (inactive past the threshold, in `execution`, holding an open box), or `working` (the default — a session with no stamped task cannot be shown to be idle, and a guessed nudge is worse than an honest default). `Project` is dropped to pay for it: the box was already 112 of the operator's 119 columns, `Unblocks` lands at 18 and the renamed `Inactive` keeps its 9, for **exactly 119**. ⚠️ **The threshold is aligned with `manager-predispatch.py`'s `STUCK_SECONDS` by value only** — that rule measures the *task file's* mtime, this one the *transcript's* age, so the two classify different populations despite sharing the number. Sibling groups now order longest-inactive first within each role run (managers still lead — the pre-existing hoist is what makes the tree's role read at a glance), compared on raw seconds rather than the 9-char cell, and every session row carries its `jump-link.py` link via a new optional `urls` array that `box-table.py` renders.
- fix: **name the third board-handoff exception** — a class-C gate that **cannot be carded at all** keeps its `jump-link.py` handover. `manager-loop`, `fleet-loop` and `fleet-drive` enumerated two exceptions (class D/E, selection modals); the round that closed the board-only handoff's own task showed a third, measured: three cardable class-C gates printed no link, and one closer-line `pick` kept its link because closer-line waits are not board cards at all (the open Stop-panel defect), so there is nothing to defer them to. The exception is structural and self-closing — when those waits become cards, the bullet stops applying.
- fix: **the sweep gate now keys ownership on the whole session-id set, with the roster name as the fallback for an empty set.** `scripts/manager-predispatch.py` read `claude_session_id` alone, so a row whose worker never stamped an id had **no input at all** and rendered `none` — indistinguishable from *nobody owns this* — and the standing spawn mandate acts on that reading without a human in the loop. Measured 2026-09-30: two workers spawned at 12:39 parked on their first tool call, so neither task file ever took an id; at the 13:00 sweep both rows rendered ready-to-start while each held a **live** roster entry whose label exactly matched the task title. `read_task` now extracts the whole set (`claude_session_id` plus every `metrics_sessions` id, matched **unanchored** — the entries are indented, and a line-anchored `^session_id:` match collects only the frontmatter id and silently reproduces this very bug), `liveness_of` probes every id and takes the first non-`none` verdict, and only a genuinely **EMPTY** set reaches `roster_owner()`, which matches the row's title against the live registry's `name` (glyph-stripped and casefolded — a roster label carries the `⚙` marker a task title does not, so raw equality never matches). ⚠️ **The fallback is one-directional by construction, and that is what keeps it safe:** a roster is empty-not-absence, so a **miss** proves nothing and the row stays ready-to-start exactly as it does today — it can only ever **remove** a row from the offer, never add one, since a rule that could also mark a never-started task owned would block every legitimate spawn. Measured 2026-10-01 in the Brogrammers vault: of the 124 tasks carrying a `metrics_sessions` block, **64 carry no `claude_session_id` at all**, so the one-field read left all 64 reading `none` while a live id sat in the next key.

## v0.89.1

- fix: **the PIDs-only caution now names `ps -o comm=`, which is not the safe spelling it looks like.** The caution in `commands/worker-drive.md` § 0b enumerated `pgrep -fl` and `ps … -o command=` and stopped there, so the one `ps` spelling that *reads* safe went unmentioned — and on macOS `ps -o comm=` prints the full argv, not the bare binary name, leaking exactly what `-o command=` leaks (observed 2026-10-01: `ps -o pid=,ppid=,comm= -p <pids>` returned `npm exec mcp-remote https://mcp.atlassian.com/v1/mcp` and a `tsx …/tts-mcp.ts` invocation). `commands/worker-restart.md` § Argument gains the same clause: no `ps` column is safe for identity, and a target resolves by PID through `~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json`.

## v0.89.0

- feat: **the manager layer gains a detection rule and a named recovery path for a worker parked on a gate that never reached the attention store.** A visible park emits a feed row — the watcher fires, `notify-gate.py` publishes it to the phone. An *invisible* park emits **nothing on any channel the manager is told to read**, so the manager's only honest report is *"no gate is open"*, indistinguishable from a healthy worker. Measured 2026-09-26 on topic `Unattended Execution`: a worker sat parked **~4 hours** while `who-needs-me.py`'s `Needs you` read **0** and the task file's mtime stayed **frozen at 10:35:11**; one `SendMessage` check-in recovered it in about two minutes. `commands/manager-loop.md`'s sweep step now carries the signature — *a tracked task that is non-terminal, whose file mtime has not moved for ≥2 cadence intervals, and for which the feed reports no gate* — together with the three-way discriminator (**working** ← the task-file mtime delta against the cadence interval; **genuinely gated** ← the feed's `Needs you` section, whose row IS the report; **parked on an unregistered gate** ← the live-owner verdict), and `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` gains the `⌛ parked-on-unregistered-gate` cell, which is evaluated before `stuck` because `stuck`'s predicate holds **no feed input at all** and cannot tell a wedged worker from one parked on a gate the feed cannot carry. ⚠️ **The `waiting-on-human` bullet now names the absence-claim distinction:** *"no mechanism of mine can see this gate"* is true of every **feed-reading** mechanism and false of the session-to-session channel, so a `SendMessage` check-in is the recovery path for that class. ⚠️ **The executable half lands in the vault's model-free gate** (`_classify` + `sweep-gate-fixture.py`), which reads the feed, the mtimes and the registry; the sweep reader cannot evaluate the cell on its own — the feed is not among its declared inputs — so it renders the cell only when the caller passes the verdict, and reports the row UNVERIFIED otherwise.

## v0.88.0

- feat: **managers hand an answerable gate to the operator through the attention board, not through a chat jump link.** `scripts/attention-board.py` prints the board's own open-card count and its URL, read from the board's markup — not from `GET /api/1.0/attention`, which is a superset (measured 2026-10-01: 4 API items against 2 rendered cards, stable across repeated fetches, so a count taken from the API would disagree with the page the operator is looking at). `manager-loop`, `fleet-loop` and `fleet-drive` print that one line in place of the per-gate jump links for answerable (class C) gates. ⚠️ **Class D/E gates and selection modals keep their `jump-link.py` handover** — a board button cannot release a gate. ⚠️ **Store down is one branch:** `attention-board.py` exits 3 having printed `board unavailable`, and the round falls back to the chat jump links for every gate.

## v0.87.9

- fix: **the `--flagged` batch's approve pass left two different `status` values for the same outcome — `vault-cli task approve` writes `status: next`, while the empty-`phase` branch writes through `task set` and never touches status, so a `todo`-approved row and an empty-phase row in the same batch ended up disagreeing.** Measured on a live run 2026-10-01: the sub-9 fixture read `status: next` with its worker already running in a pane, the empty-phase fixture read `status: in_progress`. The pass now writes `status: in_progress` after approving, so both branches agree. ⚠️ **`next` was the anomaly, not merely a cosmetic mismatch**: every row reaching the pass came from the § Step 0.5 selector, whose own condition is `status: in_progress` — so `next` is a status the pass itself introduced and never one it inherited — and a row left at `next` drops out of every *later* run's selector, since the selector tests that same field. A row holding a live worker is not queued. Operator ruling 2026-10-01; the divergence was found by the live verification of the empty-phase fix, not by its tests.

## v0.87.8

- fix: **the recording step now sits in the command body of all three manager commands, so a manager that resolved a subject can no longer silently skip its own registration — the miss that made it read itself as a worker and refuse to arm its own loop.** `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-status.md` and `commands/manager-drive.md` each delegated the recording contract to `docs/subject-resolution.md` with *"Read it there; **it is not restated here.**"* — and that document was already correct and already unread: `commands/manager-loop.md` carried **0** occurrences of `worker-manager`, so the one write a manager must perform lived exactly one hop away from the command it was reading. Measured on two sessions in one day (2026-09-27): `be22f6` and `ca3ea901` each ran manager-tier verbs with an explicit, resolving argument and were absent from `~/.claude/state/worker-manager/`; with no registration the tier test reads a session as a worker, the global rule *"Workers Don't Open Sessions"* then forbids it to arm `/supervisor:manager-loop`, and each stopped its own loop and doorbell and asked the operator what it should be — a question it could not answer, because nothing in its own state told it. Each command now carries an **actionable** recording step (run the block at `docs/subject-resolution.md` § Recording, naming the file it writes), a read-back line that says which happened (`✅ Registered: <subject>` / `⚠️ Not registered — the recording step above did not run`), and the tier statement that an absent registration is **not** a role verdict — the tier is recognised from the subject page and the pane title, and the recovery is the recording step run now, with no operator keystroke and no second session. ⚠️ **The rules are not restated:** which files on which resolution stays in `docs/subject-resolution.md`, because any site that requires reading a second file reproduces the one-hop failure this fixes. `commands/manager-verify.md` keeps its documented carve-out — its trimmed `allowed-tools` cannot run the block, so it records nothing by design.
- chore: **`scripts/check-recording-step.py` guards that step against silent omission or drift, wired into `precommit` as `check-recording-step`.** Three command bodies now carry the same step, and this repo's own history is that a copy with no mechanical guard drifts — `docs/subject-resolution.md` records that the *copy* was the liability when four commands each carried the recording contract, which is why the rules live in one file now. Same shape as `check-spawn-mode.py`: a contract with several call sites gets a check, not a keep-in-sync sentence. It asserts each of `manager-loop` / `manager-status` / `manager-drive` carries the step and names the file it writes, that each references the home for the rules rather than restating them, and that the three blocks match **outside three enumerated slots** — the point in each command's own flow where the step runs, the verb in the read-back line, and the moment the read-back names (`arm-time` for the command that arms a loop, `read-time` for the two that do not) — all three command-specific by design, with everything else required to match so a fourth divergence cannot appear silently. `manager-verify` is asserted **not** to carry the step, since its trimmed `allowed-tools` cannot run the block.

## v0.87.7

- fix: **the manager drive-leg's verdicts cache had no writer in the shipped tree, so a subject without one re-audited its whole set cold on every tick.** Clause (1) of `agents/manager-drive.md` names the cache's home (`~/.claude/state/manager-predispatch/<slug>.verdicts.json`) and assigns the write to the *caller* — *"The write is the caller's … let the caller persist them"* — while the leg itself holds no write tool; but no caller-side instruction to perform that write existed, and the only tool the manager commands are granted is `Bash(python3:*)`, with no `Write` and no shell redirect. The file therefore appeared only where a manager improvised it: the two sibling subjects' caches are one-off writes whose mtimes never move while their subject ticks daily, and `attention-routing` had none at all. Measured 2026-09-30 22:58 — a drive leg ran clause (1)'s audit **cold: 27 `task-auditor` dispatches, 0 cache hits, ~246k subagent tokens, 29 minutes**, and its own return named the remedy. `scripts/manager-predispatch.py` gains `verdicts_path()` and a `--write-verdicts` mode, the counterpart of `--write-buckets`, staged through the script so the first token stays `python3` and a 15-minute loop writes the cache with no permission prompt; `commands/manager-loop.md`'s act leg gains the persist step. ⚠️ **`content_key` is required on every entry** — clause (1) re-audits a row when its file's *content* changes (never its mtime), so an entry stored without a key can never be told from a stale one and would read back as a hit forever; `score` is allowed to be null because `blocked` rows carry none. ⚠️ **A malformed stage is refused and leaves the previous cache untouched** — it is replaced atomically, because a cache that reads back as present-but-unusable is indistinguishable from one never written.

## v0.87.6

- fix: **the manager attention watcher is scoped by pane ownership — `commands/manager-loop.md` step 7 and `commands/fleet-loop.md` armed a `Monitor` over the raw feed, so every manager paid a full turn for every other manager's gate.** Measured 2026-10-01 on the `Manager Layer` topic: **7 wakes in 16 minutes, every one a pane outside the subject's tracked set**, each a full manager turn; the same topic armed scoped fired **once, and it was that topic's own gate**. The runbook § Cadence mechanics already carried the fix — `gate-owner-filter.py --feed --self "<this-session-id>"` is *"the recommended scoping for a manager's attention watcher"* — but step 7 never pointed at it, so a manager reading the instruction literally armed the feed-wide watcher. Both bullets now name the filter. ⚠️ **Not by an allowlist** — the `--only-file` doorbell id list keeps **only** the sessions named and so drops unowned panes a manager should see; the ownership filter keeps those and drops only peer-manager-owned ones.

- fix: **`gate-owner-filter.py --feed` read zero panes from the live feed — the row marker is right-aligned inside its brackets and the parser matched unpadded ids only.** `panes_from_feed` used `re.match(r"\s*\[(\d+)\]", line)`, but a live `who-needs-me.py` render carries `[ 417]`, `[  90]`, `[   0]` — the id padded to width 4 — so the match failed on **every** row, `panes` stayed empty, and the caller exited `pass --pane, --feed, or both` **against a feed holding eight panes**. Measured 2026-10-01: exit 2 with no output before the fix; `gates: 7  emit: 4  dropped(peer-manager): 3` after it. ⚠️ **It failed loudly rather than silently** — the direction that matters — but it made the scoping the manager loop now names unusable on invocation. ⚠️ The fixtures used unpadded ids (`[7]`, `[1892]`), so every test passed against a shape the real render never emits; a right-aligned case is now covered.

## v0.87.5

- fix: **`/supervisor:open --flagged` held operator-flagged rows at Gate 2, and its approve pass could not reach a row with an empty `phase` — the operator's own flag now opens the row, and the empty-phase class is approved before its phase is set.** Three changes on one code path. (1) The readiness dispatch held **every** sub-9 row, so a row the operator had flagged for today was silently withheld and they learned of it only from the footer count. The 9/10 bar is built for agent-filed tasks and must not override the operator's stated intent, so in the batch an operator-flagged row scoring below 9 now **opens**, and the auditor's gap bullets travel to the worker as its first step, ahead of the work command. ⚠️ **Three things deliberately do not change**: `UNFIXABLE:` rows stay held on both paths, the single-name path keeps the bar, and Gate 1 is untouched. The transport is a new optional `--first-step <text>` argument — § Step 0.6 passes it, § Step 3 prepends it to the spawn prompt, and the batch still delegates through `/supervisor:open` rather than reimplementing spawn. (2) The approve pass left alone every row not at `phase: todo`, so a row with an **empty** `phase` reached its worker unapproved and stalled on the phase question — `task approve` refuses anything but `todo`. The pass now writes `approved_by`/`approved_at` and **then** sets `phase: planning`, in that order: the reverse leaves the row permanently unapprovable, because `task approve` will not accept it once it is past `todo`. ⚠️ **The generator half of this class was fixed separately** — the recurring templates now write `phase: todo` — so this path serves legacy rows, other creators, and any row a claim knocks back. (3) The batch keeps a TodoWrite checklist, one item per selected row, closed only when that row's outcome is settled and naming the outcome it earned, so a checklist ticked without opening anything is visible rather than green.

## v0.87.4

- fix: **`/supervisor:open` refuses when the task's session id could not be read — a failed read was indistinguishable from a task with no session, and the difference decides CREATE.** Step 2A read `claude_session_id` with `2>/dev/null` and never looked at the exit status, so a wrong `--vault` value (it takes a **name**, not a path — a path exits 1 with `vault not found`) produced an **empty `PRIOR_SID`** that read exactly like a task that has never been worked. The liveness guard is bounded by `PRIOR_SID` non-empty, so the empty id **skipped it entirely** and reached CREATE — the duplicate spawn the cluster-worker handling exists to prevent. The read's exit status is now captured and a non-zero one refuses with `❓ SESSION UNREADABLE`, before any branch. ⚠️ **Measured 2026-10-01, reported by a peer session that hit it**: `session-liveness.py --check ""` also returns **exit 0 listing every session on the box**, so an unbounded empty id reads as a confident "everything is live" wherever it reaches a probe. ⚠️ **The failure is silent in the direction that matters** — a wrong vault argument produces a plausible wrong answer, not an error you notice.

## v0.87.3

- fix: **unfold the `pod-attention.py` bullet from `v0.87.1`, which does not contain it.** The merge of #64 landed after `v0.87.1` was cut for #63, so the bullet was folded into an already-released section and the Changelog Fold Guard caught it on the merge commit. Direction is decided by the tag, not by where the merge put the entry: `git tag --contains 461b7a2e` returned **nothing** when the repair branch was cut, and `v0.87.2` by the time it was pushed — so the bullet moved **into `## v0.87.2`**, the release whose tag actually carries the work, rather than to a fresh `## Unreleased`, which would have announced already-shipped work as pending. `## v0.87.1` is now byte-identical to `v0.87.1:CHANGELOG.md`. ⚠️ The direction was re-established between the two commits on this branch rather than assumed at the start — the first commit moved it to `## Unreleased`, correctly for the tag state it was written against and wrongly by the time it landed.

## v0.87.2

- fix: **`/supervisor:open` refuses a session that is live but holds no pane on this Mac — a cluster worker's task was resumed, not refused, putting a second writer on a conversation running in a pod.** Step 2A decided JUMP / RESUME / CREATE from `wezterm cli list` and a `pgrep` on the task's `claude_session_id` alone, and a worker running as a pod in nuke holds **neither** a pane nor a local process — so both probes read "not running here" for a session that is working right now, and the `PRIOR_SID`-present branch resumed it. The branch now probes the plugin's single reader (`session-liveness.py --check`) and refuses, naming the session and the reason, unless the verdict is `ABSENT`. ⚠️ **`UNKNOWN` and `AMBIGUOUS` refuse too** — an unreadable source never authorises a second writer, the same rule `session-liveness.py`, `live-workers.py` and the manager commands already carry. ⚠️ **Not a blanket refusal**: a healthy session in a pane still jumps and a live headless pid still waits, so the guard fires only on *live, and reachable from nowhere here* — the one state where every branch below it is wrong.

- fix: **`pod-attention.py` posts a `producer_kind` and a `liveness_ref` the store actually accepts — as shipped, every push failed with a 400, and the unit tests could not see it.** The script hardcoded `producer_kind: "pod"` and `liveness_ref: "pod:<id>"`, inventing a member of each enum. The store validates both against a closed set — `attention-controller` `pkg/producer-kind.go` (`session`/`agent`/`cron`/`dark-factory`) and `pkg/liveness-ref.go` (`session`/`heartbeat`) — and rejects an unknown value with a `400` naming the field, so **every** `ask` and `gate` failed on the first real push. A pod is a long-lived Claude Code session, so `session` is correct for both. ⚠️ **The 28 unit tests could not have caught this**: they mock `urlopen`, so no real validation ever runs — which is exactly why the end-to-end step against the live store exists, and it found it on the first call. Two `WireContractTest` cases now assert the posted values against the enums, so a regression to an invented kind fails in the suite rather than at the next e2e. ⚠️ **`--liveness-ref` is now passable, because the ref decides whether the card *survives* rather than merely how it is tagged.** The store prunes an open item whose producer is not live and that *asked* rather than reported (`attention-store-impl.go` `classifyForRead`: `isAsked` + `!live` → `remove`), so a card posted by a producer the store cannot resolve is created with a `201` and removed on the very first board read — it never reaches the operator. A pod that the local session registry cannot resolve must declare its own ref. ⚠️ **The failure path now carries the store's own explanation.** It printed a bare `FAILED: store returned 400 for the push`, which names no field; the store's body names the offending one, and that message is what would have pointed straight at the cause instead of costing a round of probing against the live store.

## v0.87.1

- fix: **`test_restart_worker.py`'s too-loose case now asserts against a near-miss id, not an empty store.** It is one of five named both-direction pairs guarding the session-hold suppression, and it was the only one seeding `write_holds()` with **no arguments**. An empty store cannot fail for a prefix match, a substring match or a flag-everything build — so the inlined reader this test guards, a copy that does **not** inherit `session-holds.py`'s own near-miss coverage, was asserted only against the weakest possible input. Measured, not argued: with the reader inverted to a 7-character prefix match, the **old** empty-store test passes (48 tests OK) while the new one fails on `test_does_not_fire_on_a_clean_session`. The other four pairs (`test_session_holds.py`, `test_notify_gate.py`, `test_manager_predispatch.py`, `test_orphan_candidates.py`) already seed a populated store, and `test_session_holds.py` already used a near-miss id. The rule: a guard tested with an empty stream and a guard tested with a realistic stream containing near-misses are different tests, and only the second one exercises the boundary.

## v0.87.0

- feat: **`scripts/pod-attention.py` gives a cluster pod the operator channel it has neither a pane nor a spawner for.** A `claude-interactive` pod reaches the operator through neither existing carrier: `attention-ask.py` cannot post a gate (it refuses the permission class by design, so a manager cannot post one it cannot legally answer), a `message` answer routes by `SendMessage` to a name resolved from the local `~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json` — which a pod has no entry in — and a `permission` answer routes by the *spawning server* polling the store, which a pod does not have. So this arm does both halves itself: `ask` posts a `message` card, `gate` posts a `permission` card and blocks on its own item, `poll` prints the terminal. ⚠️ **The gate verdict is `permission-answer-poll.py`'s three conditions verbatim — `state == answered`, `decision ∈ {allow,deny}`, and a non-empty `resolved_by` — and that third one is the whole legitimacy basis for releasing a gate without a pane.** The board's JavaScript sends neither `decision` nor `resolved_by`, so no board click can release a gate; only an arm answer can. **Fail-open, never fail-allow:** an error, a malformed response or the deadline prints `NO_DECISION:` and exits non-zero *without* a verdict, because an implicit allow would release a gate nobody approved and an implicit deny would refuse one nobody refused — the same rule `attention-answer.py` applies to `--decision`. ⚠️ **The auth layer is a deliberate seam, not a stub.** The store carries no credentials and defaults to `localhost:18080`, which is safe only because it is localhost; a pod needs a base URL and a token injected at runtime. Both are read from the environment (`POD_ATTENTION_STORE_URL`/`ATTENTION_STORE_URL`, `POD_ATTENTION_TOKEN`) so the mechanism drops in unchanged — and a **non-local** base URL with **no** token is REFUSED rather than attempted, because an unauthenticated remote store lets anything that can reach the port read every card and release every gate. The refusal is what stops an unauthenticated localhost default being read later as the shipped shape.

## v0.86.3

- fix: **`manager-predispatch.py --save` bounds its stdin read, so a caller whose stdin never EOFs exits 3 instead of stalling the tick.** The read at the top of the `--save` branch was a bare `sys.stdin.read()`, which blocks until EOF — and the Bash tool's stdin is an open socket that may never reach one. Measured 2026-09-30 against the live Manager Layer store: **45 013 ms and killed**, versus **654 ms** for the same call with `< /dev/null`, with the store left byte-for-byte unchanged because the save never ran — a stale `recorded_at`, and the next drive leg holding all its ready-to-start rows at clause (0) with nothing reporting it. The read is now a `select` loop under a monotonic deadline (`read_stdin_bounded`), abandoned after 5 s, reported on stderr with the payload path, and returning the new **`EXIT_STDIN_TIMEOUT = 3`** — distinct from both `EXIT_USAGE` (2) and the exit-10 success code, so a caller can tell "nothing was read" apart from "nothing changed". ⚠️ **`select`-then-`read` would not have fixed it:** a caller that writes part of a table and holds the pipe open passes one readability check and then blocks *inside* `read()` — the same stall one layer down, hence the loop. ⚠️ **The caller cannot be asked to redirect its way out.** Measured in one session, minutes apart: `os.fstat(0)` reported `sock` with a bare `sys.stdin.read()` blocking past a 3 s cap, then the *same* read returned 0 bytes immediately — the harness's stdin wiring varies per call, so the bound has to live in the script. `commands/manager-loop.md`, `manager-status.md` and `manager-drive.md` carry the contract in lockstep (it is one block across the three, and a copy that drifts is indistinguishable from the real one at the point of use) and gain the consequence a reader needs: **a save that did not complete leaves `recorded_at` stale, and the next drive leg holds its batch rather than acting.** `scripts/tests/test_manager_predispatch.py` gains a `TestBoundedStdin` class driving a **real pipe** — the suite's own `io.StringIO` stdin always EOFs and is structurally unable to reproduce the stall — with the held-open case and a closed-pipe negative control, so the guard cannot be satisfied by refusing every save.

## v0.86.2

- fix: **the prior snapshot is frozen before it is passed, and the reader asserts the `recorded_at` it actually read — the v0.86.1 clause named which snapshot to pass but not how to stop it moving.** The canonical snapshot file is the **gate's own output**, rewritten every tick, so passing its *path* is passing a moving target. Measured 2026-09-30: a tick began 23:43 and passed the canonical path, read at dispatch as `recorded_at 23:35:32` — genuinely preceding the tick — and the gate rewrote that same file to `23:53:50` while the reader was still running, a ~13-minute read window. A reader that reads late compares against a snapshot post-dating its own tick, and it reports that as a **plausible delta rather than `uncomputable`** — the silent direction, and exactly the "confident number that is not a measurement" the v0.86.1 entry names as worse than the `uncomputable` it replaced. ⚠️ **Both halves are required and neither alone is sufficient.** The caller **freezes a tick-scoped copy** of the chosen snapshot and passes *that* path — a copy cannot move; and it **passes the `recorded_at` it chose as an expected value**, because the gate can still rewrite between the choose-read and the copy. The reader (`agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` step 1 item 3) checks the `recorded_at` it actually read against that value and **refuses with a named error, rendering no delta**, on a mismatch. A refused tick loses one delta; an unfrozen one silently reports a number nobody measured. Found by the Manager Layer manager against the shipped clause rather than by the review loop — the review had no way to see a race that only exists across a live read window.

## v0.86.1

- fix: **the sweep reader is handed a prior snapshot, so its delta test can fire instead of reporting `uncomputable` on every tick.** `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` has declared the input since v0.34.0 — *"A prior snapshot (optional) — the last report, for computing the delta"* — but no dispatch ever passed it, so the reader's own `progressing` / `stuck` buckets fell back to phase/status/liveness on every tick and `stuck` rendered as an **empty section**, which is indistinguishable from a genuine *nothing is stuck* result. Measured 2026-09-30, tick #43: the reader named the omission itself and stated *"a row could be stalled and I could not say so."* The caller omitted an input the callee declares; the fix is the dispatch alone. ⚠️ **The path is resolved, never hand-typed** — `~/.claude/state/sweep-gate-loop/<basename(realpath(vault-root)).lower()>/<slug(subject)>.snapshot.json`, the same derivation `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`'s `loop_snapshot_path` already performs. ⚠️ **`realpath` first is load-bearing:** this vault is reachable as `…/Obsidian/private-personal`, `…/personal` and `…/Personal` (the last two symlinks to the first), and the slug is the basename lowercased — so the *same* vault yields two different state directories depending on the spelling the caller passed, and both were live on 2026-09-30 (`personal/` 119 files, `private-personal/` 84, each written within the minute). A delta off the wrong snapshot is worse than the `uncomputable` it replaces: it renders a confident number that is not a measurement. `commands/manager-status.md` carries the same input in lockstep — it shares the agent and renders a delta too, so amending one alone would create the divergence those two files exist to prevent.

- fix: **the drive leg is handed the task-file mtimes and the verdicts-cache path, the same declared-but-omitted input class.** Both were reported by the leg against the caller's own dispatch on 2026-09-30. Without the **mtimes**, clause (1)'s freshness check cannot run for any candidate — *"had a row been `stuck`-classified it would have been un-checkable — report it, never nudge it as though the check had passed."* Without the **verdicts-cache path** (`~/.claude/state/manager-predispatch/<slug>.verdicts.json` — the store path is **not** it), clause (1)'s audit runs cold against a matching content key already on disk; naming it is the whole fix, and a later tick that named it reported **0 audits dispatched, 1 cache hit**.

## v0.86.0

- feat: **`scripts/session-liveness.py` reads a second source, so a session with no process of its own is finally answerable as live.** The registry `~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json` is the liveness authority for every session that holds a socket, and it is structurally blind to the ones that do not: a headless worker is an in-process `query()` holding no pid entry, and a cluster worker runs as a pod on another machine. Those are the heartbeat store's half (`scripts/live-workers.py`, written by `server/heartbeat.mjs`; the store is also where a cluster worker's registration lands). This reader now consults both and composes them the way `server/liveness.mjs:checkLiveness()` already does — **a positive from either source is an answer** (a fresh heartbeat is decisive on its own, reported even when the registry could not be read, because "could not tell" from one channel is not a refutation by the other), while **a negative needs BOTH sources readable**, since with one unreadable "no match" cannot rule out a match in the half that could not be read. Only a readable-and-empty pair licenses `ABSENT`, which is the one answer that permits a caller to resume onto the session. ⚠️ **The heartbeat half is delegated to `live-workers.py`, never re-globbed here.** Two readers over one directory is precisely the defect this file's own header records — on 2026-09-26 a hand-rolled second instrument read an 8-char prefix as `ABSENT` for two live sessions and published it as a confirmed verdict, at a 100% near-miss rate — and it does not become acceptable by being written twice in one repo instead of twice in one week. ⚠️ **`--list` returns `UNKNOWN` when EITHER source is unreadable** rather than a partial list: a list missing a whole half reads as "not live" for everything in the half that failed, which is the dangerous direction. `--heartbeat-dir` and `--ttl` are added, and the suite passes an isolated store — without it a headless worker running on this machine would turn an `ABSENT` assertion into `LIVE`, making the suite's result depend on the fleet's mood rather than on the code. 7 new tests, pinning the prefix contract on the new source and the unreadable-store → `UNKNOWN` direction.
- fix: **`commands/open.md` stops opening the session registry itself, so `/supervisor:open` is no longer a second reader over `~/.claude/sessions/`.** Step 2C globbed the directory inline to resolve a topic's manager — the same class of defect `session-liveness.py`'s header records, where two readers over one directory disagreed about what an id argument means and one published `ABSENT` as a *confirmed verdict* for two live sessions. It now reads `session-liveness.py --list --json`. ⚠️ **That required the shared reader to carry two fields it did not have.** The step matches the topic against the name a session holds *now* **and every name it has held** (`formerNames`) — a long-lived manager gets renamed, and a name-only match concludes there is none and spawns a **second** manager onto a live topic (observed 2026-09-18) — and it spawns the resume into the record's `cwd`, because the new tab otherwise inherits wezterm's working directory and Claude Code stops on the folder-trust prompt before registering a pid, so a resume that worked reads as a no-op. Neither field feeds the liveness verdict; both are read through the one reader so no caller opens the directory again — and so a cluster worker's registration, which the registry cannot hold at all, reaches this step too.
- fix: **`resolve()` returned `ABSENT` for a stale registry record even when the heartbeat store could not be read — the dangerous direction.** A stale record (an id registered against a pid that is gone) is a *negative*, and the composition rule above licenses a negative only when **both** sources were readable: with one unreadable, a fresh stamp in that half would be silently outvoted, and `ABSENT` is the one answer that permits a caller to resume onto a session. It now returns `UNKNOWN` there. Caught by the local `/coding:pr-review` pass rather than by CI, and the test added beside it is the regression.
- fix: **the direct-registry-read class in `commands/open.md` is swept, not patched.** Step 2C was the surface this change had already found; two more inline `glob('$HOME/.claude/sessions/*.json')` blocks sat in Step 4 and § 4.1, doing the spawn-verification `nameSource` check and the `-n`-does-not-self-link uuid lookup. Both now read `session-liveness.py --list --json`, and the reader carries `nameSource` for the first of them. ⚠️ **Fixing the instance would have left the class one surface further out** — the failure the Development Guide's second-sighting rule names. Two prose surfaces remain and are deliberately not changed here: `commands/worker-restart.md:23` and `commands/manager-loop.md:247,262` *describe* the registry read to a human rather than opening it in code.
- feat: **`scripts/cluster-heartbeat.py` reads the cluster heartbeat store — the cluster half's single reader.** A cluster worker runs as a pod on another machine: it holds no registry entry (that directory is pid-keyed and local-only) and no process on this host, so every local probe finds the same nothing for a worker mid-turn and for one that finished an hour ago. The worker publishes its own entry in a ConfigMap instead, and this reads it — one instrument per store, the same rule that keeps `live-workers.py` the only reader of the local heartbeat dir. ⚠️ **An unreachable cluster is `UNKNOWN`; an ABSENT ConfigMap is a readable-and-empty `[]`, and the two must not collapse.** The read passes `--ignore-not-found` for exactly that reason: without it both are a non-zero exit, and a network fault would read as "no cluster worker is live" for every worker in the half that failed — the dangerous direction. ⚠️ **The verdict is the stamp's age, never the key's existence** — a pod killed with no chance to clean up leaves its entry behind, so existence reports exactly the wrong answer for the case this store exists to catch. ⚠️ **The age comes from the record's own `refreshedAt`, not from a Kubernetes timestamp**, because a ConfigMap has no mtime and `metadata.creationTimestamp` is fixed at creation — a long-lived worker would read older the longer it lived. That is the one place this departs from `server/heartbeat.mjs`'s *"mtime is the age authority, never a field inside it"* rule, and the departure is forced by the store rather than preferred: the rule's purpose is that the age cannot disagree with the fact that a write happened, and on a ConfigMap the writer's own timestamp is the only thing that does. The local mirror keeps the stronger property on this side of the boundary. The wrapper, namespace and ConfigMap are configuration (`SUPERVISOR_CLUSTER_CMD` / `_NAMESPACE` / `_CONFIGMAP`), never literals — this plugin does not use `--context`. 12 tests, including a fake wrapper on `PATH` exercising the real `--cmd` path end to end.
- feat: **the cluster heartbeat store is bridged into the local one, so a cluster worker is visible to every local liveness reader.** `server/cluster-heartbeat.mjs` polls the cluster (through `scripts/cluster-heartbeat.py`) on the heartbeat interval, writes each fresh stamp into `config.heartbeatDir` carrying `source: cluster`, and refreshes a reachability marker. `live-workers.py` then reads a stale *cluster* stamp as `UNKNOWN` when the marker is also stale, and as `STALE` when the marker is fresh. ⚠️ **The marker is the whole reason the bridge exists, and refreshing it on a failed poll would invert the failure.** The mirror stops refreshing a cluster worker's stamp when the cluster cannot be read, so that stamp goes stale — and a stale stamp is indistinguishable from a dead worker. Without the marker the reader answers `STALE`, which permits a resume onto a worker that may be alive behind a network fault; with it, the two are finally separable. A test asserts the marker's mtime does not move across a failed poll, because that is the property that is invisible when it works. ⚠️ **`stampRecord` carries `source` only when the writer sets it**, so a headless worker's stamp keeps exactly the shape it had — and a stamp with no `source` reads as live, which is what keeps every pre-existing stamp working. `resolve()` gains the matching rule: a match in the unresolved set is `UNKNOWN`, while a *stale non-cluster* stamp stays `ABSENT` even during a cluster outage, because the rule keys on the stamp's own source rather than on the marker alone.
- feat: **the server polls the cluster heartbeat store, so a cluster worker now reaches the local readers.** `server/supervisor.mjs` runs `pollCluster()` once for the whole process, on the existing 30 s heartbeat interval — one timer rather than one per worker, because a single poll answers for every cluster session at once and a per-worker timer would run the same query N times for one answer. ⚠️ **A failed poll is reported on TRANSITION, never per tick, and never as an error.** The cluster being unreachable is a state the readers already handle — the reachability marker goes stale, every cluster stamp reads `UNKNOWN`, and no resume is authorised — so logging it every 30 s would bury the signal, and logging it as a failure would invite a reader to fold "could not tell" into "not live". That inversion is the one this whole store exists to prevent, and the log line says so.
- fix: **`scripts/fleet-board.py` stops opening the session registry itself — the last duplicate reader of that directory.** Its `registry_records()` globbed `~/.claude/sessions/*.json` directly, on the reasoning recorded in its own docstring: the shared reader *"returns only the id -> name map, and this table needs `status` as well."* That reasoning expired when the shared reader grew `status`, `cwd`, `formerNames` and `nameSource`, so the board now reads through it — and the fleet sweep's liveness authority (`agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`) and its board description (`commands/fleet-status.md`) name the shared reader rather than the directory. ⚠️ **The row set is deliberately unchanged: every registry entry, not only the `alive` ones.** Filtering on the shared reader's pid check here would silently shrink the row set and turn `coverage_errors()` into a tautology — the assertion would still pass while covering fewer rows, which is the exact false-clean the board's coverage check exists to prevent.
- feat: **the fleet board's row set is now registry ∪ heartbeat, so a headless or cluster worker is a row rather than an omission.** The board's rows were one per registry entry, which by construction omits every session that holds no entry — a headless worker is an in-process SDK query, and a cluster worker runs as a pod on another machine — so both were invisible in the one table whose stated purpose is that *"a table that renders correctly and drops a row is the exact failure this board exists to prevent."* Live heartbeat stamps are rows now, carrying `busy` because the store's assertion **is** "still being worked", which is precisely what the registry's `busy` means. ⚠️ **`coverage_errors()`'s `extra` assertion is widened with the row set, never dropped.** It read `rows - registry`, which was right while the registry was the only source; left unchanged it would fire on every heartbeat row, and relaxed to `rows - (registry | heartbeat)` it stays a real control — a row invented by neither source is still caught. Deleting it because it "now fails" would have been the same false-clean this function exists to catch, one level up. ⚠️ **A stamp in the `unknown` state is deliberately NOT a row**: that state means the cluster could not be read, so neither `running` nor `idle` is true of the worker and every bucket would assert something false about it. It is surfaced where it belongs — `session-liveness.py --check` returns `UNKNOWN` for it, and `UNKNOWN` is what SC4 tests. 4 new tests, including the control that a row from neither source is still caught.
- fix: **`scripts/restart-worker.py` reads the registry through the shared reader — the last direct reader — and the reader gains a second accessor so that it can.** `registry_entries()` globbed `~/.claude/sessions/*.json` itself, with its own `SUPERVISOR_SESSIONS_DIR` fallback; it now delegates, and `restart-precheck.py:173` inherits the change because it already called through. ⚠️ **It could not simply take `read_registry()`'s dict, and the existing tests caught it.** That dict is keyed by session id, so two files claiming one id **collapse into one entry** — deleting the ambiguity this path refuses on, and resuming onto the wrong claimant of two is exactly the double-writer this plugin guards against. Three tests that assert that refusal failed loudly under the dict version. The reader therefore gains `read_registry_entries()`, the **list** shape: one file, one glob, one unreadable rule, two views — because "which sessions are there" and "what is on disk" are different questions, and only the second preserves the collision. ⚠️ **The raw record is the other half**: this path's consumers read `sessionId`, `startedAt`/`procStart`, `kind` and `name`, none of which the normalised shape carries, and dropping `kind` would have silently removed the check that refuses a headless worker.

## v0.85.1

- fix: **the `HELD` cell renders the hold's age, not only its reason — `⏸️ HELD — <reason> · <age>`.** `session-holds.py` has stamped `held_at` since the store landed and renders the age in its own `--list`, but every consumer rendered the reason alone, so a hold set three weeks ago and one set this morning were byte-identical on every sweep — the one distinction a person reading their own holds is actually looking for. The four consumer surfaces now render the canonical cell: `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` and `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` (the two sweep renderers, which must agree), `commands/manager-loop.md` (the offer exclusion), and `commands/open.md` (the refusal print, whose second slot carried the task name in the age's position — the same shape, a different meaning, so a reader who knew one would misread the other). Units are the writer's own — `12m` / `4h` / `3d` — and an entry whose `held_at` is missing or malformed renders `?`, never `0m`: reporting an old hold as one set seconds ago is the reading that would invite a manager to treat it as fresh.

## v0.85.0

- feat: **`/supervisor:inbox` gives the operator one read-only view of the approval queue, and the three verbs that move a row across it.** `phase: todo` is the approval boundary — agents file there and stop, and the `todo → planning` flip *is* the approval — but nothing listed it, so the queue could only be walked one task at a time from the full vault. `commands/inbox.md` renders every live `todo` row across every vault declaring a `tasks_dir`, grouped by topic where the vault declares one and by vault otherwise, each line naming why it matters from the task's `# Impact`; `scripts/inbox.py` does the read and writes nothing at all. Three verbs, and no fourth: **approve** through `vault-cli task approve` (the only approval — `task set <task> phase planning` writes the phase and nothing else, so the row would move with `approved_by` empty and the approval recorded as never given), **reject** through `task set status aborted --reason … --gate-successor …` (the CLI requires both, and `none` is a claim the operator makes rather than a default), **later** through `task set status backlog` (status, never phase — `backlog` leaves the boundary intact). ⚠️ **The command never picks a row; the operator names it.** Every verb resolves its vault through `inbox.py --resolve`, which refuses on ambiguity rather than guessing: titles are not namespaced across vaults, so a same-named live row in two vaults is a real state, and a verb taking the first match would move the wrong row in a vault the operator never named. Measured 2026-09-30: **609 live rows** over 6 vaults (private-personal 441, seibert-brogrammers 151, seibert-personal 7, seibert-agent 5, private-boss 4, private-agent 1) — the queue had been accumulating since the gate shipped, and no view had ever listed it. ⚠️ **The recurring exclusion keys on an ISO-WEEK token (`2026W35`), never on a date.** `Fix Failed CI Build claude-supervisor 2026-09-30` is a one-off whose title happens to be dated, and a date-shaped matcher would drop every such row silently — the loss shows up as a shorter list and no error. A named negative-control test holds that line. `docs/session-tiers.md` gains its row (any tier) in the same change, keeping the lockstep `diff` clean.

- fix: **`commands/ready.md`'s frontmatter did not parse as YAML, so the command's `description`, `allowed-tools` and `argument-hint` were all at risk of being lost at dispatch.** Its unquoted `description` scalar contains `verdict: open`, and YAML reads a colon-followed-by-space as a mapping separator — `yaml.safe_load` on the block fails with `ScannerError: mapping values are not allowed here`. Found while adding `/supervisor:inbox`, whose own `description` carried the identical defect (`phase: todo`) and was fixed in this same PR; the class was then **swept rather than patched**, per the Development Guide's second-sighting rule. A `yaml.safe_load` over all 23 `commands/*.md` frontmatter blocks now passes, and that one-liner is the cheap way to catch the next occurrence. ⚠️ Nothing reported this — the command still appeared installed and enabled; the failure mode is a silently dropped dispatch hint, not an error.

## v0.84.1

- fix: **`agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` gains a `held` class, so a held session renders `⏸️ HELD — <reason>` instead of `stalled`.** The fleet's renderer classifies **sessions** and renders `[<session id 8>]` — the exact key a hold is stored against — but its class table had no held case, so a held session fell through to `stalled`, which is a **⚠️ problem row**. ⚠️ **This is a rendering fix, not an act fix, and that is the whole point.** `agents/fleet-drive.md` already refuses to nudge, reap or escalate a held session, so the loop was never going to act — but a row rendered `stalled` invites a manager to nudge **by hand**, and that is the act the drive leg's refusal cannot reach. A row that is **mislabelled** is worse than one that is missing, because it reads as work. ⚠️ **The hold row sits first because it wins over every other, and it is the fourth WAITING carrier:** the rule that a row held from outside is never `stalled` already names a parked gate, an unmet `blocked_by` and a future `defer_date` — a hold is the fourth and the strongest, because the other three say *not yet* while a hold says *not by you*. ⚠️ **Keyed on the session's FULL id, never the 8-char prefix this file renders in its digest** — the store is keyed on the whole id, and a prefix lookup misses **every** entry while looking exactly like a session nobody held. Caught in draft rather than in review.

## v0.84.0

- feat: **`commands/manager-answer.md` refuses to answer a held session's prompt when the answer is the manager's own — and delivers it when the answer is the operator's.** A hold is operator *policy* about a **session**, and answering a prompt unblocks that session to keep working. ⚠️ **The split is provenance, not mechanism, and it is the whole point.** An answer given under `Manager answer, via supervisor:` is the manager's **own decision**, and deciding to unblock a parked session is exactly what a hold forbids. An answer relayed under `Operator answer, via supervisor:` is **the operator's own**, given in the current session — refusing it would drop a decision they actually made. *The hold constrains agents, not the operator*, which is the same reading `agents/gate-relay-send.md` carries as a non-site. ⚠️ **The join was verified in source before the clause was written, not assumed.** `mcp__supervisor__list_agents` builds each row with `session_id` (`server/supervisor.mjs:1054`) and `pending_permissions` (`:1068`) side by side, so `request_id → row.pending_permissions → row.session_id` is direct. ⚠️ **That check is the point of this entry.** Two earlier consumers this session shipped clauses that read correctly and **could not execute** — a dict key that never exists, and a tool the agent did not have. A third would have been written here had the join not been verified first; `list_agents`' habit of returning `[]` against a 28-session fleet makes the schema description look unreliable, and the answer was in the source rather than the docs. ⚠️ **An unresolvable request id fails closed** — no row carrying it means stop, never fall through to answering, because an unresolvable request and an absent hold are indistinguishable downstream. `list_agents` and `Read` were added to this command's `allowed-tools` for this check.

## v0.83.0

- feat: **`agents/manager-wrangler.md` never auto-answers a held session's permission prompt — it escalates instead.** The wrangler exists to answer headless workers' prompts by policy, which is unblocking a worker so it keeps working; a session the operator has marked *do not work on this* must not be unblocked by a policy loop. ⚠️ **Escalation is the point, not a compromise, and it is why *deny* is the wrong verb here.** The wrangler's own escalate path already means *"do NOT call `answer_permission`; leave the request pending"* — so the request stays **visible** to the operator while only the act stops. That is this feature's load-bearing constraint almost verbatim (*"the row stays visible; only the message stops"*), and denying would instead act on the operator's behalf: a hold says the operator is the one who decides. ⚠️ **Keyed on the worker's `session_id`, never its `agent_id`** — the latter is a supervisor handle and not a session id, so step 1 now carries both. ⚠️ **`Read` was added to this agent's tool list for this check and nothing else** — the agent previously ran on the `mcp__supervisor__*` tools alone, so the first draft of this clause instructed a read it had **no tool to perform**. That failure is silent by construction: the read does not happen, the hold is not seen, and the prompt is answered exactly as if the session had never been held. Caught by reading the frontmatter rather than trusting the review — the bot approved that draft, as it must, since nothing in the diff distinguishes an instruction an agent can carry out from one it cannot. ⚠️ **A hold outranks the worker's brief**, and a held session may carry no task at all, so an absent task is no reason to skip the read. The canonical rule lives in `skills/hold/SKILL.md` and is referenced, not restated.

## v0.82.4

- fix: **`agents/manager-drive.md` no longer reaps a session that has already run its close.** The reap test read three disk facts (`status: completed`, `phase: done`, zero open boxes) and nothing about the session. A worker that had finished its close and was merely still open therefore looked exactly like one parked on the close gate. Measured 2026-09-27: one Manager Layer tick reaped both. One session had closed clean at 08:07:23Z, so its notice was stale; the other still held `pick — 1. /vault-cli:session-close`, so its notice was correct. ⚠️ **A fourth read, run only after the three pass**, runs the new `scripts/reap-closer.py <session-id>`, which returns `CLOSED` (exit 0), `OPEN` (1) or `UNREADABLE` (2). It takes the last `👤 You:` line from the session's latest assistant message, never from user turns, which carry injected command templates. It resolves the transcript by session id and dedupes by realpath, because a symlinked project dir lists one file twice and would otherwise always read as unreadable. Covered by `scripts/tests/test_reap_closer.py`. `nothing — session closed` means no message is sent, and the row goes under a new `Closed clean` section with a `closed clean <n>` count on the `Drive:` line. Any other closer, and any unreadable read, reaps exactly as before. ⚠️ **This is deliberately not a repeat-count**: an open gate keeps getting notices however many it has had, since a count would silence the very neighbour this read protects. Replayed against both transcripts cut at the 08:15:56Z tick: the closed session reads `nothing — session closed`, so it is not reaped; the open one reads its `pick`, so it is reaped.

## v0.82.3

- fix: **the Session cell renders three states — `[<sid8>]` alive, `(<sid8>)` not alive, `—` no id recorded — and both renderers now agree.** `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` carried *"the sessionId prefix alone: `[<sid8>]`, or `—`"* while the vault gate's `session_cell()` returned `—` on a **not-live** probe, so the same recorded id rendered `[<sid8>]` from the reader and `—` from the gate — the divergence this repo's own two-renderer rule forbids, and in the silent direction, because the gate is what decides a wake. Operator-visible symptom, 2026-09-27: *"Here are most sessions done but still have a session.
