This, I can't believe that all of them have an open tab in wezterm or?"* — measured 2026-09-30, **10 of 10 sampled ids probed `ABSENT`** and only **3 of the topic's 82 recorded ids were live**, so the column rendered 82 identical-shaped cells describing 79 dead sessions. ⚠️ **The delimiter carries the state, so no word returns and the cell stays 10 cells exactly** — the `live` / `parked` retirement of 2026-09-20 stands, and `[<sid8>]` / `(<sid8>)` are byte-different at equal width. ⚠️ **`parked` is ALIVE** — the session holds a socket and is waiting on a gate — so it takes the live-shaped cell; only a not-alive verdict takes `(<sid8>)`. ⚠️ **The verdict stays the caller's** — the reader may not probe, so input 10 now covers **every id-carrying row** rather than the ownership-dependent rows alone, and a verdict that is missing or `UNKNOWN` keeps the live-shaped cell rather than claiming a state nothing established. ⚠️ **`manager-loop` and `manager-status` already probed the whole tracked id set** — only the reader's *description* of that input was narrower than what they do, which is why the divergence survived; both callers now name the Session cell as a second consumer of the same map. The vault halves of this change — `sweep-gate.py`'s `session_cell()` and its module docstring, `sweep-gate-fixture.py`'s `cell_cases`, and § Sweep output's Session rule — land outside this repo.
- fix: **`scripts/manager-predispatch.py` implements branch (b) of the runbook's `stuck` rule — an idle worker holding open boxes is now flagged rather than silently passed.** `apply_stuck()` computed `busy = bool(rec and rec["alive"] and rec["status"] == "busy")` and set `stuck = False` for every row that was not busy, so the rule's second limb had **no implementation at all** and a stalled-but-idle worker was flagged by nothing — the coverage gap the runbook's own note recorded as open. ⚠️ **The idle duration is the task file's mtime, read in `read_task()` via `os.fstat` on the descriptor it already holds** rather than a second `stat(path)`: the path could be replaced between the two calls, and the clock has to describe the file whose contents were hashed. That is the rule's own *"task file unchanged"* measure, and the same signal the reader's shipped branch (b) derives its duration from. ⚠️ **Three qualifiers ride along and each one is load-bearing.** `phase: execution` and `>=1 open box` are the runbook's own; the **parked** guard is not optional — the runbook states that a row demonstrably WAITING is not `stuck` and that the file-unchanged proxy cannot tell the two apart, so a branch (b) ignoring `LIVENESS_PARKED` would flag exactly the workers the rule exists to leave alone. ⚠️ **The digest's deliberate mtime exclusion still holds, and now says why.** mtime reaches `digest_of()` through `stuck` alone: for a row the rule measures a rewrite is not unrelated — it resets the clock the rule reads — and for every other row `stuck` is `False`, so mtime never enters the digest at all. Branch (b) carries no persisted map, because an mtime is absolute and there is no clock to observe across runs; the two branches are disjoint, so no row can be flagged by both.

## v0.82.2

- fix: **the `HELD` cell now exists in both sweep renderers, not one — closing a divergence this repo's own two-renderer rule forbids.** The vault's model-free gate was given the cell first (`bucket()` returning `⏸️ HELD — <reason>`, `action_lines()` skipping the row), while `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` — the renderer that produces **the table the manager actually reads** — had **zero** references to it. ⚠️ **That is the failure the runbook measured on 2026-09-26 in the mirror direction**: `blocked-upstream` was added to the runbook and the agent while the gate had no `blocked_by` at all, and *"the tightening changed what this runbook said and nothing about what the loop did."* ⚠️ **And the gate's own half was inert when this was written** — its overlay read `task.get("claude_session_id")` against a dict `read_task()` keys as `session`, so it compiled, ran, and never fired. The honest statement of the defect is therefore that **neither** renderer suppressed: the gate silently did nothing, and the agent had no cell at all. Measured 2026-09-30 by probing `bucket()` with a task in the gate's own shape — the fixed build returns `⏸️ HELD`, the unfixed one returned `🔄 progressing`. The vault halves of this four-surface change fix the gate and pin the cell in `sweep-gate-fixture.py`; **the missing pin is why this shipped**, since `mk()` builds exactly the task shape that would have failed and no other check in either repo asserts on it. ⚠️ **The cell wins over every other, with no exemption** — unlike the `deferred` overlay it does not yield to `done`, `hold` or `backlog`, because `✅ done` on a held row is an invitation to reap it. ⚠️ **And the exclusion is a separate act from the repaint**, exactly as `waiting-approval` and `deferred` each record: a `next` task keeps the `ready` key, so the cell alone would leave the line beneath it still offering `/supervisor:open`. Both surfaces now carry both halves. ⚠️ **Keyed on the session id alone, deliberately not the whole id set** — a wider key in one renderer than the other is the same divergence in a new place. ⚠️ **The two renderers spell that key differently, and unifying them is the bug, not the fix:** this agent reads task frontmatter, where the field is `claude_session_id`; the gate reads a dict `read_task()` has already normalised, where it is `session`. The other three surfaces of this four-way change are vault-local and land outside this repo — the gate's key fix, `sweep-gate-fixture.py`'s pin, and the runbook's `Held` bullet.

## v0.82.1

- fix: **`manager-loop` arms its tick at the head, not the tail — the re-arm can no longer be lost to a long tick.** `ScheduleWakeup` is one-shot per call, so a re-arm written into the tick's last step is dropped by exactly the tick that runs long: one that dispatches a sweep, then takes an inbound peer question and answers it, ended **unarmed** — no error, no marker, no stale-loop signal — and the `manager-liveness.py --arm` record was skipped in the same motion, so the one instrument that could have caught it was fed by the same skipped call. The `ScheduleWakeup` + `--arm` pair is now **step 3**, ahead of the sweep and after subject resolution (which `--arm --topic` needs); the Procedure renumbers to 1–9, and the two references that named the old numbers (`fleet-loop.md`'s waiting-on-human pointer, `manager-drive.md`'s extraction note) move with it. The same ordering lands in `commands/fleet-loop.md` § Cadence — its `ScheduleWakeup` moves to the round's head and its bullet carries the why. ⚠️ **The fleet side has no `manager-liveness.py --arm` record to move**, and none was added to force symmetry: the fleet loop carries no liveness store and nothing there keys on `noop`, so inventing either would be new behaviour rather than this ordering. ⚠️ **`noop` is why the old cadence step survives in reduced form.** The field is required and the runbook makes it the topic-page write-back trigger, but a tick armed at its head has not run and cannot report change — so the head arms with `noop: false` and a **no-change** tick corrects it at the tail, which keeps a quiet tick collapsing and keeps the write-back keyed on the tick's real result. A second call **supersedes** the first rather than stacking (each call cancels every pending dynamic-loop wakeup before scheduling its own), so the correction is never a second loop; a change tick keeps the single arm it already made. Verified against the shipped binary — two `ScheduleWakeup` calls in one turn leave exactly one wakeup pending, and `noop` carries no scheduling semantics.

## v0.82.0

- feat: **`/supervisor:open` refuses a session carrying an operator hold — before any branch, JUMP included.** The check sits at Step 2A rather than in the `--flagged` selector because that is the choke point: Step 0.6 delegates every batch row to this command's own single-task path rather than reimplementing it, so one gate covers both the batch and a hand-typed `/supervisor:open "<task>"`. A held session necessarily carries a `claude_session_id`, which already excludes it from Step 0.6's approve pass — so no `approved_by` is written onto a held row before the refusal is reached. ⚠️ **The refusal is loud and names the release path, never a silent skip** — a dropped row is indistinguishable from a task that was never opened, so the operator would have no way to learn their own hold had been read. ⚠️ **Three different things in this command are called a hold and only one is read here:** the operator's session hold, the `hold` *task status*, and the `HOLD` marker the selector prints for a `blocked` row. The canonical rule lives in `skills/hold/SKILL.md` and is referenced, not restated.

## v0.81.0

- feat: **`agents/fleet-drive.md` never acts on a session carrying an operator hold.** A `NEVER` clause alongside the existing ones: no nudge draft, no reap, no escalation. ⚠️ **The row is still rendered** — only the act stops, and because this leg returns *drafts* rather than sending, that means emitting **no draft at all** for a held row rather than one the caller is trusted to drop. A hold is operator *policy* about a **session**, orthogonal to any task's status; a held session may carry no task. The canonical rule lives in `skills/hold/SKILL.md` and is referenced, not restated.

- fix: **`commands/manager-loop.md` classifies a relay's gate class *before* dispatching, so a relay its own contract forbids is never sent — and a bare affirmative against a handover line is named as not-an-answer.** The path-B relay rules already forbade relaying a production-touching or irreversible approval, but the check only ever reached `gate-relay-send` *after* the dispatch: the agent is dispatched first and refuses second, so the refusal spent the subagent it existed to save. Measured 2026-09-30 in one manager session, twice within an hour: the manager emitted `👤 You: you run: tell pane 212 the obsidian-personal PR is merged and to proceed to the deploy step`, the operator replied `y`, the manager dispatched `gate-relay-send`, and the agent refused with `not sent (gate cleared)` plus a class-D/E rationale — zero keystrokes sent, pane untouched, ~50k subagent tokens spent to produce a refusal. ⚠️ **Neither source said what a bare affirmative against a handover line *is*.** `commands/manager-loop.md` carries no `👤 You:` line and no `you run:` definition in any released version, and the global closer rule reaches the case only when the line's action is itself irreversible or production-touching — so the incidental-verb variant (a `you run:` line whose stated action sits adjacent to a class-D step) was unwritten in both. The rule now runs the check in the manager before the dispatch, classifies **the action the line names rather than the line's verb**, and requires the manager to say so once and hand over the `jump-link.py` line verb-free instead of dispatching.

## v0.80.2

- fix: **`--save` refuses a table on stdin, so a record can no longer be stamped with save time while the payload it describes is older.** `--save` reads the payload the render wrote and dates the record from *its* mtime; a caller that hands the table over itself — a `< <payload>.table` redirect, or the `<<'TABLE'` heredoc the three commands dropped at 0.63.0 — moved that stamp to `datetime.now()`, so a stale table read as fresh and the next tick replayed it. Measured 2026-09-30 on the `MDM Bugs` record: `recorded_at` `11:41:29` against a payload written `11:35:22`, written at `09:41:25Z` by a `--save … < …/mdm-bugs.table` redirect in the Brogrammers vault. The stdin read is kept deliberately — that read is the separate hang defect — but a non-empty one is now a usage error naming `--write-payload`. The CLI's own help and docstring had advertised the piped shape; both now describe the payload path. Reproduced in a temp state dir: a piped save stamps `now()` while the payload mtime stays earlier, and a no-stdin save still dates from the payload.

## v0.80.1

- fix: **`agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` names the ownership key once, with the roster name as the fallback when a task's id set is empty.** The ready-to-start bucket and the vault runbook's auto-resume clause 6 previously read as two independent ownership tests — the first keyed on the task's session-id set, the second on the roster name — so a task whose worker never stamped an id read **unowned** to one and **owned** to the other, and the standing spawn mandate **opens** ready-to-start rows rather than merely listing them. Measured 2026-09-30 on a live manager loop: two workers spawned at 12:39 parked on their very first tool call, so neither task file ever took a `claude_session_id` or a `metrics_sessions` entry; at the 13:00 sweep both rows rendered `🚀 ready-to-start` while each held a **live** roster entry whose label exactly matched the task title — one sweep away from a second session being spawned onto work a live session already held. ⚠️ **The fallback is one-directional by construction, and that is what keeps it safe:** a roster is empty-not-absence, so a *miss* proves nothing and the row stays ready-to-start exactly as it does today. It can only ever **remove** a row from the offer, never add one — a rule that could also mark a never-started task owned would block every legitimate spawn. The lockstep edit to the runbook's ready-to-start clause lands in the operator's vault, not in this repo.

- docs: **`docs/fleet-surface.md` § Session roles now names the three-way exclusion — the shape where no role may run an acceptance check.** Some checks need one session that **both** spawns a tab worker **and** then reads it back (`agent_status` resolves only the workers its own server spawned, `agents.set()` inside `spawn_agent`), and no role holds both halves: a worker may not spawn, a manager may not verify, so only a session the operator starts can close it. Each leg is a deliberate contract and none is individually wrong; their intersection is empty, and no source named it until 2026-09-27 (session `cd816ba7`). ⚠️ Stated as **policy, not mechanism** — nothing in the harness blocks `spawn_agent` from a worker, so a reader told a worker *cannot* spawn goes hunting for an enforcement that does not exist; the operator's authorization is the bridge. The same statement lands in the vault's `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` § Guardrails item 2 (the manager's side) and in the KB page `A Worker Cannot Validate a Manager Command` § Additional Insights (the case beside its two-way Corollary).

## v0.80.0

- feat: **`agents/manager-drive.md` never acts on a session carrying an operator hold.** A `NEVER` clause alongside the existing ones: no nudge, no reap, no auto-resume, no escalation. ⚠️ **The row is still rendered** — only the act stops. The clause also draws the distinction that matters and is easy to miss: a hold is operator *policy* about a **session**, orthogonal to the `hold` *task status* the neighbouring clause names. A task can be `in_progress` while its session is held, and a session can be held with no task at all. The canonical rule lives in `skills/hold/SKILL.md` and is referenced, not restated, per the repo's single-home convention.

## v0.79.1

- fix: **no manager may write a reading into the `manager-sweep-reader` dispatch that it has not taken this tick — and the read and the dispatch that consumes it are never emitted in one message.** A **stale** carry is worse than an omitted field: an omission is visibly missing and the reader reports it as a caller bug, while a stale value has the same shape, position and confidence as a fresh one, so it silently disables the check it feeds. Measured 2026-09-27 (session `c9f59bee`, tick 20): the feed read (`who-needs-me.py`) and the reader dispatch sat in **one assistant message** — one `message.id`, two parallel tool calls — and the prompt asserted `Needs you (0)`, tick 19's value, while the feed's own output for that tick read **`Needs you (5)`**. The clause lands verbatim at **all three** dispatch sites — `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-status.md`, `commands/manager-drive.md` — because the defect is **mode-dependent** and a fix in one command says nothing about the others; each points at the rule's single home, `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` § Step 4. The reader's other half lands in `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` `<error_handling>`: a caller-supplied **reading** it could not corroborate must be **named**, never passed over in silence, because a reading it could not corroborate and did not name reads in the report exactly like one it verified — *consistent* is all the tracked set can ever give you, and *consistent* is not *fresh*. Structurally blind inputs (liveness verdicts, the clock) are named once as a group, not per fact. Both directions measured 2026-09-30 on scratch fixtures: against a set with no contradiction the agent correctly reported the figure consistent **and** named the limit; against a set holding two `phase: human_review` rows facing a claimed `Needs you (0)` it flagged the divergence **unprompted** and named the caller's emission ordering as what would make it detectable.

## v0.79.0

- feat: **`restart-worker.py` refuses a held session — an EIGHTH refusal.** ⚠️ Checked **before** the status and kind gates, because a hold is the stronger statement: `busy` says the moment is wrong, a hold says the session is not yours to touch at all. Restarting is exactly the act the operator held the session to prevent. The refusal carries the stable token `held-session` and names the release path (`/supervisor:hold`), like the seven refusals the module already documents. The reader is inlined (the plugin's scripts do not import each other; a subprocess dependency on a plugin path is a fail-open) and reads are lock-free because the writer lands through `os.replace`; a missing or corrupt store reads as *nothing held*, so an unreadable file cannot refuse a restart the operator asked for. Paired test, both directions named: `test_fires_on_a_held_session` (too-tight) and `test_does_not_fire_on_a_clean_session` (too-loose — the SAME session, unheld), plus `test_a_corrupt_store_reads_as_nothing_held`. **Both inversions verified:** disabling the guard fails 1 test; making it refuse unconditionally fails 16; restoring passes 48.

## v0.78.1

- fix: **`agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`'s ready-to-start bullet stops hard-coding one vault's predicate, and stops citing a match that does not exist.** The bullet read *"the task is `next`"* and claimed *"it no longer varies by vault"*, citing *"`65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md:134` now carries the predicate above verbatim, matching another vault `70 Runbooks/Manager Session.md:155`"*. Measured 2026-09-30, **both halves of that citation were false**: the primary vault's bullet sits at `:147` and still reads `status: next`, while `70 Runbooks/Manager Session.md:155` was corrected on 2026-09-29 to *"its `phase` is `planning` or later and its `status` is non-terminal"* — so the two copies diverge, and this file matched the **primary** one while citing the other as agreeing. The bullet now states the clause as **vault-relative**, names both copies with their real line numbers, records that the `70 Runbooks` correction is **not yet ported to the primary copy**, and forbids hard-coding either clause as a default — a row at `next` + `planning` satisfies both, but one at `in_progress` + `planning` satisfies only the `70 Runbooks` clause, so a hard-coded `next` silently drops every row that correction was written to admit. ⚠️ **Behaviour is unchanged** — the file's `<constraints>` already make the runbook canonical per vault and the sweep-reader resolves the conflict by rule; what was wrong was the text a future implementer builds from. ⚠️ **The blocker half of the clause is untouched** — the bare `next`-with-a-free-slot arm stays removed, and the surviving divergence is on the status half alone. ⚠️ **Unfolded from `## v0.77.0`, where the merge placed it** — that release was cut at `5f612b8` *before* this change merged (`c94cd91`), and `git tag --contains c94cd91` is empty, so the tag proves the work is not in it. Repair per the changelog fold guard.

## v0.78.0
- fix: **`session-holds.py`'s pane read was missing from the transport-read enumeration, so the check failed on a clean `master`.** `pane_title` reads `wezterm cli list` and returns `None` on failure — compliant with `docs/pane-reads.md`, and its caller `cmd_hold` refuses non-zero — but `SITE_LIST` never recorded the site, so `transport-read-check.py` failed its own completeness assertion (`derived [('session-holds.py', 'pane_title')]`). The enumeration is the check's spec, so the omission is the defect, not the site. Note this is the *local* failure only: the check has further, environment-coupled failures that appear on a clean runner and are owned separately.

- feat: **`manager-predispatch.py` includes the session hold in its digest, so adding or releasing a hold wakes the loop.** ⚠️ **This is the one consumer where the hold is a digest INPUT and deliberately NOT a suppression.** The gate decides whether the sweep runs at all, so suppressing it for a held session would stop the sweep and the held row would never render -- the exact failure the hold design forbids, where a row that disappears from a sweep is indistinguishable from a row that got fixed. Including it as an input is what makes a hold being added or released move the digest. The reader is inlined (the plugin's scripts do not import each other; a subprocess dependency on a plugin path is a fail-open) and reads are lock-free because the writer lands through `os.replace`. Paired test, both directions named: `test_fires_on_a_held_session` (too-tight) and `test_does_not_fire_on_a_clean_session` (too-loose -- a hold on a session NOT in the tracked set leaves the digest alone), plus `test_releasing_a_hold_moves_the_digest` and `test_a_corrupt_store_reads_as_nothing_held`. Verified by neutralising the input: fails 2 tests; restoring passes 49.

## v0.77.0
- feat: **`notify-gate.py` never publishes a gate whose blocked session is held.** The reader is inlined (the plugin's scripts do not import each other; a subprocess dependency on a plugin path is a fail-open) and reads are lock-free because the writer lands through `os.replace`. The filter runs **before** the store read and independently of it, because a hold is local operator policy and must apply even when the store is unreachable -- unlike the cross-layer de-dup, which needs the store and degrades without it. ⚠️ **The gate leaves the publish set but its store item is left alone**, the same shape the existing `suppressed` path uses: pruning the item would make a RELEASED hold re-notify as though the gate were new, inverting the design -- a hold suppresses the message, never the record. A held gate is printed, not silently skipped. Paired test, both directions named: `test_fires_on_a_held_session` (too-tight) and `test_does_not_fire_on_a_clean_session` (too-loose, an unheld gate in the SAME round), plus `test_the_control_fires_when_no_hold_is_set` and `test_a_corrupt_store_reads_as_nothing_held`. `publish()` is mocked rather than the live notification core reached. Verified by inverting the guard: the held filter disabled fails 2 tests; restoring passes 65.


- fix: **`agents/manager-drive.md` clause (1) names the verdict cache's store and the cost model that lets the widened input actually execute.** Two defects found by the first live tick on v0.76.0. **(1)** The cache paragraph said to persist a verdict and score *keyed to the row's content* but named no store — and the drive agent is a fresh subagent each tick holding no write tool, so it correctly refused to invent a path and a second tick could never be warm. The cache now has a named home beside the caller's own classification — `~/.claude/state/manager-predispatch/<slug>.verdicts.json`, next to the `<slug>.buckets.json` carrying `bucket_sets` — read from the path the caller passes and **never** self-chosen, because a leg that picks its own path writes a cache no later tick reads. **(2)** Widening the input from one bucket to every non-terminal row left no cost model: the leg dispatched **0** audits across 29 `todo` rows, called one `task-auditor` per row too costly for a single pass, and asked for a budget. The clause now states the model — **a cold tick audits the full set, and the cache is what makes every later tick free** — forbids inventing a budget, batch size or sampling rule, and requires any early stop to name clause (5)'s bound and its value, since *"too costly for one pass"* is not a bound. ⚠️ **A partially-audited set cannot be ranked at all**, because the ranking compares rows against each other — which is why the cold cost is the design rather than a defect to trim. Clause (5)'s budget wording moves from *per ready row* to *per row in the set*, which is the population it now covers.

## v0.76.0



- feat: **`agents/manager-drive.md` audits every non-terminal row, not only the `ready-to-start` bucket, so a `todo` row finally reaches the approval card with a readiness verdict.** Clause (1) scored the rows a caller had already bucketed `ready-to-start`; on Manager Layer that bucket held 0–1 rows while 28 of 32 non-terminal tasks sat at `phase: todo` and were never audited, so the whole approvable backlog went out as one unranked card (`e93f31de03495789b294f50b6cf05d72`, 31 rows) that came back `ANSWERED: skip` — nothing flowed. Step 4's input is now every non-terminal row with no live worker, and each leaves clause (1) with exactly one of six verdicts: 🚀 Ready, ✅ Ready for approval, 🔧 Repairable, ❓ Needs you, 🗑 Stale, ⏸ Blocked. ⚠️ **The approval boundary is unchanged** — a `todo` row is audited, ranked and named on the card, and is still never opened and never flipped `todo → planning`; clause (6)'s hold now says *never a dispatch candidate for **opening*** rather than *never a dispatch candidate*, which is the reading `commands/open.md` § Step 1.5 already draws (*"report `todo` rows instead of acting on them"*). Three additions carry it: a **premise check** feeding 🗑 Stale (structure is not truth — `task-auditor` scores shape, and 3 of a 5-row batch opened 2026-09-30 were UNFIXABLE on premise, already fixed at the load path), a **ranked round card** (clause (7), batch-level, ≤5 recommended, each with score and WHY, ranked by unserved goal criterion → unblocks others → score, with grouped counts and the stale rows as a retirement question), and a **content-keyed verdict cache** so a second tick on an unchanged tree re-audits nothing. ⚠️ **The cache key is content, not mtime** — a rebase or an obsidian-git autocommit moves an mtime without changing a word. ⚠️ **`commands/manager-loop.md`'s waiting-approval bullet carries the same rule** — the row is audited and ranked, and still never offered — so the family closes across the agent, the command and the vault runbook together.


## v0.75.1

- fix: **the manager sweep's `stuck` and `orphan` predicates stop acting on rows that are not in the state they name — the row stays visible and only the nudge / candidacy is suppressed.** Each read a cheap proxy that cannot separate two states. `stuck` (`agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`) reads the task file's mtime, satisfied identically by a wedged worker (**STALLED**) and by one correctly parked on a human or a dependency (**WAITING**); the discriminating input is whether anything outside the worker is holding it — the parked verdict (`liveness_of` / `is_open_gate` / the `later (on <trigger>)` closer), an unmet `blocked_by`, or a future `defer_date`, all three already computed — and the row now falls through to `🔄 progressing` instead of `⚠️ problem`. `orphan` (step 5) reads an empty id set, satisfied identically by a task whose worker died and by one whose planning ran **manager-side** as sub-agents, so no worker ever existed and the probe matched it permanently; the discriminating input is **`metrics_sessions` read against `claude_session_id`** — a *current* id can be absent for two unrelated reasons, a *historical* one cannot, because the history is written by the session that did the work and never cleared. ⚠️ **The two fixes have different blast radii and that is recorded rather than smoothed over:** `orphan` is reader-only, because the vault gate has no orphan bucket at all (`_classify` returns `🔄 progressing` for every `in_progress` row and `action_lines` declines the ORPHANED verdict outright), while `stuck` is a **two-renderer change** whose same test must also land in the vault gate's `_classify` / `apply_stuck`. ⚠️ **Neither fix implements branch (b) of the `stuck` rule** — the gate's `apply_stuck` requires the registry to report the session continuously `busy`, so an idle-but-stalled worker is flagged by nothing; that coverage gap is separately owned and folding it in would close it in the wrong direction. Both fixes keep the row in the table and in the tally, per *"a row that disappears from a sweep is indistinguishable from a row that got fixed."*

## v0.75.0

- feat: **`orphan-candidates.py` reads the session-hold store, so a held session is never an auto-resume candidate.** The reader is inlined — the plugin's scripts do not import each other, and a subprocess dependency on a plugin path is a fail-open — and reads are lock-free because the writer lands every change through `os.replace`. ⚠️ A missing or corrupt store reads as *nothing held*, and that direction is chosen: inventing a hold would silently drop a real orphan candidate, while reading a real store as empty merely fails to honour a hold. The hold suppresses the **act** only — the row still renders, which is what keeps a hold distinguishable from a session that got fixed. Paired test: with the hold set, the held task is excluded **and** an unheld task in the same run is still a candidate; with the hold removed, both are — the control is what makes the exclusion able to fail. First of the consumer wirings.

## v0.74.1

- fix: **`agents/manager-drive.md` clause (1) step 2 routed a below-bar row to the wrong terminal branch.** The loop has exactly **two** terminal branches — *below the bar after 2 rounds* and *the early stop on repetition* — and both end in an attention-board card plus a hold, but only the early stop's card carries *"the claim, not the probe, is wrong"*, because the operator's act there is a **reframe**. Step 2 read *"a row that clears the loop and fails Gate 2 … escalates through the reframe branch below"*, sending a reader to the early-stop branch for a case whose operator act is a raise, not a reframe. Step 2 now names the **below-bar terminal branch** directly — post the card naming the score against the bar, then hold the row — and the phrase *reframe branch* is named only by step 4, where the early stop is defined. ⚠️ **`commands/ready.md` § 4 carried the same gap in weaker form** — it said only that a below-bar row "asks the operator", naming neither the card nor the hold — and now names the same act as clause (1): post the card, then hold, with the card's fields and the hold line read from clause (1)'s `<error_handling>` rather than restated.

## v0.74.0

- feat: **`/supervisor:open --flagged` refuses a flag that was not operator-set.** The selector now reads `flag_set_by` and admits only `operator` or `legacy`, printing a named `REFUSED | <vault> | <task> | flag_set_by=<who>` line for everything else — absent included, because after the 2026-09-30 disposition no flagged row should carry an absent value, so one means a writer that did not declare itself, which is the bypass. The filter sits in the **selector**, not at Gate 1: the batch's opening move is `vault-cli task approve` on every row the selector returned, *before* Gate 1 runs, so a refusal there would land after `approved_by: operator` had already been written onto the agent-set row. Requires `vault-cli` ≥ v0.156.0, which is where the field itself comes from.

## v0.73.0

- feat: **`/supervisor:hold` — the skill over the session-hold store.** `skills/hold/SKILL.md` is the single home of the hold rules: a hold is keyed on the **session**, never on a task; ⚠️ **the row stays visible and only the message stops**, so a consumer must still render `⏸️ HELD — <reason> · <age>` while suppressing every act; a hold is removed **only** by `--release`; and reads are lock-free with each consumer inlining its own reader rather than shelling out, because a subprocess dependency on a plugin path is a fail-open. `scripts/session-holds.py` now takes the flag-first shape the skill documents — a bare `<session|pane>` means hold, `--release <id>` and `--list` are the verbs — so the skill passes `$ARGUMENTS` through verbatim and the shape has exactly one home. The subcommand form still works. No new behaviour beyond the CLI shape; the store itself shipped in v0.72.0.

## v0.72.0

- feat: **session holds — a durable, machine-readable operator mark, stored at `~/.claude/state/session-holds.json`.** Adds `scripts/session-holds.py` (`hold` / `release` / `list` / `is-held`), keyed on **session id**, carrying `reason` / `held_at` / `held_by`. Storage copies `asked-ledger.py`'s discipline verbatim: a **sidecar** lock (`session-holds.json.lock`, never the data file), then an atomic `tmp.<pid>` + `os.replace` write. ⚠️ **A hold is removed only by `release`** — pruning is by age and liveness, never by sweep-absence, because "absent from my sweep" is a claim about the sweep's own partial view. A hold whose session has died is *surfaced* by `list` as a release candidate rather than silently dropped. ⚠️ **Reads are lock-free by design:** every write lands through `os.replace`, so a reader sees the whole old file or the whole new one and never a partial one. Consumers therefore inline their own reader rather than shelling out to this script — a subprocess dependency on a plugin path is a fail-open. ⚠️ **The row stays visible; only the message stops.** This commit lands the store and its CLI; wiring the consumers that must *render* a held row while suppressing every act on it is the next step. Tests assert both directions by name — `test_fires_on_a_held_session` (too-tight) and `test_does_not_fire_on_a_clean_session` (too-loose, written against a store carrying a near-miss id, not an empty file), plus the release-candidate pair (`list` flags a dead session, does **not** flag a live one) read off a single `list` run. Each fails when its guard branch is inverted.

## v0.71.5

- fix: the manager sweep's `ready-to-start` and `blocked-upstream` buckets now decide ownership by **liveness**, not by the presence of a recorded session id, so a `next` row whose session has died is offered instead of silently dropped. The reader (`agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`) may not probe liveness — its `<constraints>` forbid `pgrep`/`ps`, transcript recency and the clock, and a subagent inherits the caller's ancestor chain, so its own probe reads a false empty for the caller's own session — so it had used id presence as a proxy, while the canonical runbook states the clause as *"no live session owning it"* and the model-free gate implements liveness (`liveness_of()` → `enrich_liveness()` → `bucket()`). The two renderers therefore disagreed on exactly one row shape, in the direction the runbook's two-renderer rule forbids: a `next` task carrying a `claude_session_id` whose session has ended rendered `🚀 ready-to-start` in the gate and was excluded by the reader — a consequence this file's own step 3 already names (*"reads as owned and silently drops out of the ready-to-start set, which is a missed dispatch rather than a false alarm"*). Measured 2026-09-30: 127 of the primary vault's 557 `phase: todo` rows carry a `claude_session_id` whose session has ended, so each was excluded from the offer from the moment it was approved, for the rest of its life. Both caller commands now probe the tracked set with `session-liveness.py` **before** dispatching the reader and pass the verdicts in as a new input (item 10); `UNKNOWN` is not `ABSENT`, so an unreadable registry yields UNVERIFIED rather than death, and the probe is bounded by the tracked set rather than the vault. Fixed in both commands — the defect is mode-dependent, so a fix in one says nothing about the other.

## v0.71.4

- fix: **`commands/open.md` § Step 1.5 Gate 2 gains the `UNFIXABLE:` producer, so the token its reader has been waiting for can actually be emitted.** v0.71.2 shipped clause (1) step 0 — the token's reader — but nothing told a sub-agent to produce it: every dispatch site asked only for a `READINESS:` line, and the prompt named no class of gap the token applies to. The probe prompt now instructs the auditor to check, before scoring, for the one class of gap that is not its to close — a Success Criterion depending on a page, artifact or credential that does not exist, a scope question the task file does not answer, a target system or account named nowhere — and to end with `UNFIXABLE: <reason>` as its **only** final line instead of a score, spending no repair round and opening nothing. ⚠️ **Measured, and it is why this is a producer fix rather than a wording tweak:** a probe whose prompt *did* carry the rule still emitted no token, because the audit had never raised the gap as a finding at all — so the instruction names the class to look for, not just the output token. ⚠️ **The reader is not restated here.** `agents/manager-drive.md` clause (1) step 0 owns the branch; this block documents that it is the producer and points there, and `commands/ready.md` step 3 continues to reference the clause rather than re-implement it.

## v0.71.3

- fix: **only the server holding the producing session records a delivery attempt, so the fleet stops outvoting the real carrier with false `failed` records.** Both store-polling arms — `server/message-delivery.mjs` and `server/attention-poll.mjs` — recorded `failed` whenever they could not settle an answered item, including when the item belonged to a session **another process** held. But every supervisor server watches every open item, so one answer is read by the whole fleet: at most one server holds the park, and every other one wrote *"a carrier tried and could not deliver"* for an attempt it never made. ⚠️ **Measured live 2026-09-30 against the running store:** after the fleet restarted onto the trail-writing code, **19 carrier-written records, every one `failed`, and 0 `delivered`** across 15,906 items — and because the trail keeps one record per item, whichever server wrote last won, so a real `delivered` from the owner could be overwritten by a non-owner's `failed`. That is a confident wrong verdict, the failure direction the trail exists to prevent. `selectMessagePark` and `selectParked` now mark each refusal with `owner` — `false` when the item names no producer or no agent of this server holds the session, `true` when this server holds it and the park is gone, ambiguous or stale — and `failed` is recorded **only** when `owner` is true. ⚠️ **The permission arm's own spec asserted the bug as correct:** it fed an item for `sess-2`, a session the server does not hold, and expected `failed`. It now expects nothing, and a new spec pins `failed` to the owned case. Specs: two new negative cases in `server/message-delivery.test.mjs` (another server's session; no producer) and the corrected pair in `server/attention-poll.test.mjs`, all three mutation-verified to fail with the gate removed. ⚠️ **Records already written are not repaired by this change** — the 19 `failed` records stay in the store and read as they did.

## v0.71.2

- fix: **`agents/manager-drive.md` clause (1) gains the `UNFIXABLE:` reader, so the loop that stops on the token is the loop that owns it.** `commands/ready.md` dispatches into clause (1)'s bounded repair-then-open loop, and a sub-agent that cannot resolve a gap — because its resolution needs an **operator decision** — returns `UNFIXABLE: <reason>` **instead of** a `READINESS:` line. That token previously had no reader in the loop: the new command mentioned it, but the clause it delegates to had no branch for it, so a sub-agent's verdict would have reached a loop that could not act on it. ⚠️ **The branch is clause (1)'s step 0, read before the audit** — a token that stops the loop must be read before anything else runs — and it spends **no round** and opens nothing, exactly as a below-bar row does: no repair the sub-agent can perform would resolve a decision that was never its to make. ⚠️ **One loop, one reader.** `commands/ready.md` now *references* the clause instead of re-implementing the branch, because a caller-side reader leaves the loop's own stop rule unenforced for every other entry into it — the failure the single-home rule exists to prevent.

## v0.71.1

- docs: **`docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker names `/supervisor:ready` as the one-verb path for its readiness precondition.** The verb shipped in v0.71.0; the canonical spawn doc still described the manual audit → repair → re-audit → open sequence without saying it is now one command. The addition names the verb, its call surface and its single output line, and states the two properties a reader needs before trusting it: it **re-surfaces** the loop in `agents/manager-drive.md` clause (1) rather than adding a second one, and it **references** that section for the bar rather than restating the number — the same single-home rule the authoritative-home block already states for all three constants. It also records that a `phase: todo` row is refused *before* any audit runs, so the verb readies a task and never approves one.

## v0.71.0

- feat: **`/supervisor:ready` readies ONE named task and opens it, in one verb.** The sequence a manager previously ran by hand — dispatch `vault-cli:task-auditor`, read the gaps, edit the task file, re-audit, then `/supervisor:open` — now runs inside a sub-agent against a single named task, and the calling session prints one verdict line (`READY: <task> — <before>/10 → <after>/10 — opened | escalated: <gap> | refused: not approved`). ⚠️ **It re-surfaces the existing loop rather than adding one.** `agents/manager-drive.md` clause (1) already owns the bounded repair-then-open loop for the ready-to-start bucket of a swept set; a second loop would be a second counter — the day one is edited and the other is not, nothing errors and no diff catches it — so this verb runs that loop for one named row and references its stop rule, round cap and early-stop rather than restating them. ⚠️ **The bar has one home too** — `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 2 — and is referenced, never restated. ⚠️ **It readies; it never approves.** A `phase: todo` row is refused with `commands/open.md` § Step 1.5 Gate 1's block verbatim, *before* any audit runs, so a readying verb cannot quietly become an approval path. ⚠️ **Cross-tier.** The tier table gains its row, and the two prose counts that had drifted from it are corrected in the same change: `docs/session-tiers.md` said *"Two bare verbs are cross-tier"* and `CLAUDE.md` named three, while the table already carried four — a count restated beside the list it summarises is a second counter, so the prose now points at the table instead of counting it.

## v0.70.0

- fix: **the `message` class's board carrier records its own delivery attempt, so a delivered card stops reading `never_attempted`.** `server/message-delivery.mjs` settles a board-answered `message` card but wrote no trail record, so the query answered *"did my answer reach the session?"* with **`never_attempted`** — *"no arm ever picked its answer up"* — for cards a carrier had in fact delivered. ⚠️ **Measured live 2026-09-29 against the running store:** 39 board-answered `message` cards in a 30-minute window, **every one reading `never_attempted`**, one of them a delivery that visibly landed in a running session. That is the worst failure direction a trail can take — a confident wrong verdict, which is worse than having no trail at all. ⚠️ **This arm is the carrier for the board path.** `commands/attention-next.md` carries the conversational path and records nothing on its `DELIVERY: supervisor poll` branch, so instrumenting only the command left the common case — the operator clicking a card — unrecorded; the set of attempting arms is open, and a new carrier becomes one the day it ships. The write mirrors `server/attention-poll.mjs` exactly so the two cannot drift: `delivered` after the settle, `failed` when the park it was aimed at is gone (an attempt WAS made and could not be delivered), and **nothing** when the answer carries no content, because no delivery was attempted and recording `failed` would claim a carrier tried and could not deliver. ⚠️ Recorded **after** the settle, never before, and a write failure is logged rather than thrown — the park is already settled, and a missing trail entry must not be reported as a failed delivery. Specs: three cases in `server/message-delivery.test.mjs`, including the negative one asserting **nothing** is posted.
- feat: the answering arm records its own **delivery attempt**, so *"did my answer reach the session?"* stops being an inference. `scripts/attention-answer.py attempt ITEM_ID --outcome delivered|failed` posts what this arm observed to `POST /api/1.0/attention/{itemID}/attempt`, and `commands/attention-next.md` calls it **after** the delivery step, per branch. ⚠️ **Written by the arm that ATTEMPTS, never at answer time.** An attempt is a fact about the carrier: recording it where the *answer* is recorded would make every answer read as attempted — the exact defect the trail exists to close. ⚠️ **The outcome is refused rather than defaulted**, on the same reasoning `--decision` carries: a `delivered` default would record a success nobody observed and a `failed` default a failure nobody observed, and both would be indistinguishable, downstream, from a real measurement. ⚠️ **Two branches deliberately record nothing.** `DELIVERY: supervisor poll` — the supervisor server delivers the verdict and records its own attempt, so posting here would record an attempt by an arm that delivered nothing. And `LOST:` / `REFUSED:` / `FAILED:` — no attempt happened, so recording `failed` would claim a carrier tried and could not deliver, which is a different and false statement. ⚠️ **Requires attention-controller ≥ `v0.30.0`**, which adds the endpoint; against an older store the `attempt` call fails with `FAILED:`, which is a missing endpoint rather than a failed delivery — report it as such and never retry the delivery. Specs: `scripts/tests/test_attention_answer.py` gains `AttemptBodyTest` (the body names the carrier and the outcome; the POST targets the attempt endpoint; a failure is recorded as `failed` rather than dropped) and `AttemptOutcomeGateTest` (an empty or unknown outcome is refused **without posting** — asserted on the call, not on the message). ⚠️ **The permission class's carrier records its own attempt too**, in `server/attention-poll.mjs` — the class is delivered by the supervisor server polling the store, so it is that server, not the command, that must write the record. It posts `delivered` after the park settles, `failed` when the park it was aimed at is gone (an attempt WAS made and could not be delivered — not `never_attempted`), and **nothing** when the answer was not arm-delivered, because no delivery was attempted and recording `failed` would claim a carrier tried and could not deliver. ⚠️ Recorded **after** the settle, never before, and a write failure is logged rather than thrown: the gate has already been released, and a missing trail entry must not be reported as a failed delivery. Specs: three cases in `server/attention-poll.test.mjs`, including the negative one asserting **nothing** is posted.

## v0.69.0

- feat: **`supervisor:fleet-drive` renders a `Waiting on your keystroke` list, so one round hands the operator one to-do list.** `agents/fleet-drive.md` gains the section in its `<output_format>` — rendered immediately before the `LEDGER` line and never omitted, printing `(nothing waiting on you)` when empty — with one row per pane that needs the operator's own keystroke: the bold action verb first, then the task name, then the link the digest already carried (the agent renders no link of its own). Rows come from the digest's `CLOSERS` lines plus the drive leg's own `blocked` verdicts, so a pane listed under `BLOCKED` belongs there even with no closer line; a pane carrying two closer lines is **one** row holding both actions; quick actions (`approve` / `Shift+Tab`) sort above `review` / `pick`; and an `unverifiable` session goes on a separate `unconfirmed:` line rather than into the list, where the operator could not act on it. `commands/fleet-drive.md` step 5 and `commands/fleet-loop.md` § Output shape both name the section. ⚠️ **This removes a per-round hand step, not a channel.** The six rows the operator called "mega nice" on 2026-09-29 had been assembled by hand out of three sections across two sub-agent reports; the list now arrives by default, and the section sits beside `📋 Open with the operator` and `Needs-input` rather than superseding either. ⚠️ **It prints on a round whose drive leg returns no usable report, too.** A dead drive leg is not an empty fleet — that is the round the operator most needs the list — so the fallback builds it from the attention feed (`who-needs-me.py --all`), oldest wait first and numbered, one `jump-link.py <pane>` link per row, with rows whose session name marks them a fixture or self-test excluded. That fallback is the one path in `commands/fleet-loop.md` that renders a link itself; the keep-in-sync rule it sits beside governs the drive leg's own rows, which arrive already rendered because the sweep resolved them.

## v0.68.0

- feat: **A manager asks about its round's unapproved rows in one card, and acts only on the operator's words.** `commands/manager-loop.md`'s Act block gains a `waiting-approval →` line: the round's `⌛ waiting-approval` rows go to the operator as **ONE** card via `attention-ask.py post-batch` — never one card per row, which would reintroduce the per-item reporting this bucket exists to remove and would ask the same question N times — and the answer is acted on. ⚠️ **Poll before you post**, and the reason is the store's, not a convention: suppression is open-scoped, so once an item is answered the same `dedup_key` writes a **new** row, and a manager that posted first would read that fresh row as `OPEN` and lose an answer it already had. ⚠️ **The `--dedup-key` is the round's unapproved row set, not the tick** — which is what removes the need to carry an item id between ticks, since an unchanged set returns the same item to poll while a set that changed because rows were approved is a genuinely different question. ⚠️ **On `ANSWERED:` the manager runs `vault-cli task approve` per row the answer names, then compares the named set against the rows actually flipped** — an approve-on-inference build produces the same `phase: todo → planning` delta and the same timestamps, so the frontmatter delta alone cannot discriminate. Only then is each approved row opened via `/supervisor:open`. `NOT_OPERATOR_ANSWERED:` reads as `OPEN`: the gate is not released.

## v0.67.0

- feat: **A manager can batch its round's unapproved rows into ONE attention-board card.** `scripts/attention-ask.py` gains a `post-batch` arm: `--task` repeated once per unapproved ready row produces a **single** `message` item listing them, numbered, rather than one card per row. ⚠️ **The rows are printed verbatim because a task's name is the key `vault-cli task approve` takes** — a card that abbreviated one would be answerable but not actionable. ⚠️ **No options are offered, deliberately:** the answer is the operator's own words naming which rows to approve, and a fixed option list would make an all-or-nothing click the only reply to a question whose real answer is a *subset* — which is the thing the caller's set comparison measures. The producer gate and the post tail are extracted into shared helpers so `post` and `post-batch` cannot drift on the one rule that decides whether an item can ever be polled back; `post`'s behaviour and exit codes are unchanged. Tests: `scripts/tests/test_attention_ask.py` gains `BuildBatchPayloadTest` and `PostBatchTest`, the latter asserting **one** store call for a three-row batch.


## v0.66.1

- fix: **an all-empty bucket set can no longer be staged, and a corrected re-stage can no longer be silently discarded.** Two defects compounded: `--write-buckets`' shape check was `not all(isinstance(n, str) and n.strip() for n in names)`, and `all(...)` over an **empty list is vacuously True** — so `{"done": []}` passed the very check whose message reads *"must map to a non-empty list of names"*. Measured **2026-09-28** in manager session `b444543e`: the sweep render's two-space indent made the caller's column-0 bucket extraction match nothing, it emitted an all-empty set, `--write-buckets` accepted it at **exit 0**, and `--save` wrote it — leaving the drive leg holding its entire ready batch at clause (0). ⚠️ **The no-heal was the dangerous half, and it is a second, independent defect.** `digest_of()` hashes the tracked set only and `bucket_sets` is not in it, and `save_stored(...)` sits inside `if changed:` — so re-staging the corrected set against an unchanged tree returned `SAVED no-change (digest equal)` at **exit 0** and the bad record stood until the tree next moved. Measured again **2026-09-29** on the Attention Routing loop, where the route was the no-change branch rather than a mis-parse: a *correct* set (21 waiting-approval / 4 progressing / 89 done, including a task filed that minute) was staged, discarded, and the store kept the previous tick's sets. ⚠️ **The fix makes the per-bucket half a save input in its own right rather than folding it into the digest.** `digest_of()` stays a pure function of the tracked set — its docstring's contract is "what the sweep would render", and a caller-supplied argument has no business moving it — so `evaluate()` now carries `stored_bucket_sets` and `--save` writes when the staged set differs from it. `--write-payload`'s indent is deliberately **kept** and documented rather than stripped. ⚠️ **Both doors into the record are checked, by one shared helper.** Validating only `--write-buckets` left the `--save --buckets` read path a bare `json.load`, so a hand-written or stale staging file could still reach `save_stored` with a set that cannot gate — the same defect by the other route; `bucket_shape_error()` is now called from both. Change set: `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_predispatch.py`, `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-status.md`, `commands/manager-drive.md`.

## v0.66.0

- feat: **a `message` card answered on the board now settles the parked prompt that asked it — the board's answer reaches a worker instead of stopping at the store.** A new arm, `server/message-delivery.mjs`, started beside `startAttentionPoll` and watching `answer_mechanism: message`, settles a park with `deny` + `Operator answer, via supervisor: <the operator's words>` — the shape `llms.txt` § Install already prescribed by hand for an `AskUserQuestion`, applied from the board instead of from a manager session. ⚠️ **It is a SECOND ARM, not a wider watch set, and that distinction is the change's whole design.** `attention-poll.mjs`'s permission-only watch set (`:174`) is correct as written: it exists to settle a park from a store *verdict*, and a `message` card carries no `decision`, so `decisionOf` would refuse it and `selectParked` would refuse it again for having no park to join — widening that set would move the same refusal one step later, not make a message card work. The two differ in the rule they apply, so they are two modules. `armDelivery` is deliberately **not** applied here, per the operator's 2026-09-26 ruling that a scripted answer to a `message` card costs little while a permission approval EXECUTES a command: a board click sends no `resolved_by` and is a legal answer to this arm's cards and an illegal one to the permission arm's. ⚠️ **Measured basis for the class, read from `~/.claude/state/attention/*.events.jsonl`:** of 1258 `question`-kind open lines (`question` → `message` via `attention-watcher.py:93`), **919 are written on `Stop`** — a closer panel — against 257 on `PreToolUse` and 82 on `PermissionRequest`. The `Stop`-origin card is why this cannot be a hook: the session has ended its turn and is idle, no harness event follows it, and the permission carrier works only because `PermissionRequest` *blocks while the gate is up*. ⚠️ **The freshness guard is load-bearing, not defensive.** The park is joined by producer and one producer answers many cards over its life, so an item whose `created_at` predates the park's `requestedAt` is refused rather than allowed to settle a gate its answer does not belong to; ambiguity (two parks) and no-content answers (`skip`, empty, absent) are refused for the same reason `decisionOf` refuses a missing verdict. ⚠️ **Residual, named rather than implied:** a card whose producer has **no** parked prompt — the idle closer-panel session — is still not delivered, and this arm says so on its own log line instead of reaching for a park that is not there. Change set: `server/message-delivery.mjs`, `server/message-delivery.test.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`.

## v0.65.1

- fix: **The sweep payload file is written link-free and `0600`, so the jump token stops reaching disk world-readable.** `manager-predispatch.py`'s `--write-payload` branch — the writer `manager-sweep-reader` calls as its own last step, so every tick goes through it — wrote the table it was handed **verbatim, OSC 8 escape bytes included**, through a plain `open(tmp, "w")` that created the file **0644**. The escape carries the jump link, so `~/.claude/state/manager-predispatch/<slug>.table` held a live `http://127.0.0.1:1337/jump?pane=<n>&t=<token>` URI in a shared state directory, readable by any local process. Measured **2026-09-29**: `attention-routing.table` at mode `0644` with `grep -c 'jump?pane'` → **1** at 11:28; at 15:0x, **three of six** `.table` files carried the token (`manager-layer.table` ×2, `mdm-bugs.table` ×2, `bro-21546-automation-vulnerability-fix-agent.table` ×1), two of them written within the preceding 20 minutes. ⚠️ **The exposure is intermittent, and that is why it cannot be closed from the outside.** The same path measured 1 link at 11:28 and **0** at 11:56, because the variable is the *reader's* behaviour — the sweep reader may set `BOX_TABLE_NO_LINKS=1` itself — and never the writer's. A clean artifact therefore proves nothing about the writer, and a tick that happens to read clean must never close the issue; SC1's ≥2-write requirement with a stdout positive control is the guard, and it has to run against a subject that actually renders links. ⚠️ **The fix is reuse, not new code, and it belongs in the writer.** The same file already held both halves — `strip_links()` (`:654`) and `save_stored()`'s `os.open(tmp, …, 0o600)` (`:715`, called at `:693`) — and `--write-payload` was simply the branch that never got them. It now writes `strip_links(table)` through `os.open(…, 0o600)`, so the file is never world-readable even for the instant between the write and the `os.replace`. ⚠️ **Stripping at the caller is explicitly rejected, and the reason is structural rather than stylistic.** One `render_table()` frame serves both the tick file and the operator's printed output, so an exported `BOX_TABLE_NO_LINKS=1` — or any caller-side strip — takes the on-screen jump links with it; the narrowing has to sit at the sink that needs it. This is the second instance of *one rendered artifact, two destinations*, after the sibling tick-file leak closed 2026-09-25, and it is fixed the same way: strip at the write, mode at creation, caller's frame untouched. Verified: `--write-payload` handed a frame carrying a link writes a file with no token at mode `600`, while the same render's caller-facing stdout still carries its OSC 8 link.


## v0.65.0

- feat: **`/supervisor:open --flagged` approves every eligible row before it gates or spawns anything.** Step 0.6's opening move is now `vault-cli task approve` over each row the Step 0.5 selector returned that is **not** JUMP/RESUME-bound — `role: human` rows included, since they open into Direct as the operator's own session and only the agent plan path is skipped for them. The pass runs **before** the readiness dispatch, never after, so a row is approved whether or not it then clears Gate 2, and a held row that was approved opens on the next run without the operator touching it. One `approve <task>` line is printed per approved row — the run's output is the only record that the ordering held. ⚠️ **This turns the 2026-09-28 ruling from an exemption into a recorded approval, and the ruling is what makes it legal.** `flag: true` on a `todo` row *is* the approval for `--flagged` only; without that standing ruling the pass would be an approval read from task content, which `vault-cli`'s `docs/task-writing.md` § *Phase transitions* (the **"The approval may be delegated"** paragraph) forbids verbatim. The approval is the operator's, the keystroke is the batch's, and `approved_by: operator` records exactly that. Measured 2026-09-29: a `--flagged` batch opened 8 rows, 4 with an empty `phase`; within 30 min three workers had handed the operator an approval or phase question from their own panes. ⚠️ **Gate 1's note is rewritten, not deleted** — the batch no longer *bypasses* the gate, it *performs* the approval, and "the batch is exempt from Gate 1" would now describe a step that no longer happens. The four sweeps are unchanged.
## v0.64.1

- fix: **A manager reads the pane tail before publishing a gate, not only the attention feed.** `commands/manager-loop.md` gains the requirement at both sites that name the feed as the channel — the pre-classification note and the Sweep-1 `waiting-on-human` bullet. ⚠️ **The feed names a gate; it never proves the gate is still open**, and a feed row can outlive the question it describes — measured 2026-09-27, when two panes were published to the phone from the feed and were found already answered when their tails were finally read. The rule already lived in the vault's `Manager Session.md` § Gate triage; this is what makes the two meet at the point where the feed is named as the gate source.

## v0.64.0

- feat: **The manager's repair-then-open loop is bounded, and stops on repetition rather than on a round count.** `agents/manager-drive.md` clause (1) previously permitted **one** structural repair; it now runs **at most 2 rounds**, and stops early the moment the **same criterion draws findings in two consecutive rounds**. ⚠️ **Repetition is the diagnostic, and it is why this is not a plain round cap.** When round N and round N+1 both name one criterion, the next repair is a third patch to that criterion's *probe* — and the measured evidence is that the probe is not the defect. Four consecutive audits of one unchanged row returned **6 → 7 → 8 → 8 with zero hard-gate failures at every round**; rounds 2–4 each tightened the same probe, and only round 4 named the real defect — **the claim itself was unknowable from the evidence available**. That reframe fixed it in one edit where three probe patches had not. The repeat branch therefore escalates as a **reframe** (*"the claim, not the probe, is wrong"*, naming the criterion and both rounds) rather than spending round 3 on another patch. ⚠️ **The loop's acceptance and the spawn bar are different questions and are deliberately not merged.** The loop stops repairing once the audit returns **zero hard-gate failures** — a structurally sound row needs no further structural repair, and the score is reported, never the trigger for another round. The **spawn bar is unchanged**: `commands/open.md` § Step 1.5 Gate 2, *≥9 AND zero hard-gate failures*, single home `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 2. A row that clears the loop and fails Gate 2 is **not** re-repaired; it escalates through the reframe branch. ⚠️ **And the escalation fires once, never per sweep** — the card's `--dedup-key` is the task's `task_identifier`, so a later sweep re-posts nothing and quotes the existing `item-id`; the hold *line* repeating is correct and expected, the second *card* is the defect.

- feat: **The four sweeps stop treating `phase: todo` as ready-to-start — the manager half of the approval gate.** `todo` is the operator's approval boundary: agents file there and stop, and the operator's `todo → planning` flip *is* the approval. A `todo` row now renders **`⌛ waiting-approval`**, stays in the sweep and the tally, is **excluded from the offer set**, and emits **no action line** — no spawn, no resume, no nudge, no TTS. Changed: `commands/manager-loop.md` (the spawn-side approval check, and Guardrail 2's mandate narrowed to *approved* ready-to-start work), `commands/fleet-loop.md` (the standing spawn mandate), `agents/manager-drive.md` (the disk re-read that already holds `completed`/`aborted`/`parked` rows now holds `todo` rows too), and `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` (the Status cell and the offer subtraction). **Measured basis: 17 of the 18 rows in `ready-to-start` sat at `phase: todo`**, so the standing mandate was spawning work the operator had never approved. ⚠️ **It is a bucket of its own, not a sub-case of `waiting-on-human`** — both are waits on the operator, but `waiting-on-human` carries a ~15-min TTS rule because a genuinely blocked worker is urgent, while an unapproved row may sit for days *by design*; folding them together would page the operator about their own inbox on every tick. ⚠️ **The placement in the classification order is load-bearing in both directions** — it sits *after* the parked check (a live worker blocked on a gate is the sharper wait) and *before* the stuck check (an unstarted `todo` row is in its intended state; a `stuck` branch above it would re-file the entire unapproved backlog as `⚠️ problem` on every tick). `completed`, `aborted`, `hold` and `backlog` keep their own cells. ⚠️ **`/supervisor:open --flagged` is the one carve-out**, already shipped by the sibling change above; an agent-written `flag: true` is not an approval. This completes the half that change deferred — *"the four sweeps … remain the sibling task's"* — and the two land together in this release, so the earlier bullet's *"the four sweeps are unchanged"* describes that PR's scope, not this release's. ⚠️ **The vault's two renderers change with it and must not drift** — `sweep-gate.py`'s `bucket()` (plus its `GLYPHS`/`BUCKET_ORDER` entry, without which the row is counted and then silently dropped from the tick marker) and `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` § Step 4, pinned by `sweep-gate-fixture.py` with four **controls** (`parked`, `hold`, `completed`, `aborted` at `phase: todo` must each keep their own cell) — a branch placed anywhere in `_classify()` satisfies the positives, and only the controls distinguish "carve out unapproved work" from "swallow the bucket set".

- feat: **`/supervisor:open` refuses a `phase: todo` row before it spawns.** Step 1.5 now runs two gates in order. **Gate 1 (approval)** reads the resolved row's phase from the CLI and, on `phase: todo`, prints `⛔ NOT APPROVED` naming `vault-cli task approve "<task>"`, then stops — it spawns nothing, never runs the readiness probe, and writes nothing to the task. **Gate 2 (readiness)** is the existing auditor probe, unchanged. The order is the point: auditing an unapproved row spends work on a decision the operator has not made. Measured twice on 2026-09-28 — a worker spawned on an unapproved row reaches the approval question itself and hands the operator a pane-bound `you run: vault-cli task approve …`, reintroducing exactly the pane-bound approval the attention board exists to remove. ⚠️ **The `--flagged` batch is exempt from Gate 1**, by the operator's ruling of 2026-09-28: `flag: true` on a `todo` row **is** the approval for `--flagged` only, so the batch opens such a row rather than refusing it, and the batch section now states the exemption instead of leaving it implied. The four sweeps (`manager-loop`, `manager-drive`, `fleet-loop`, `fleet-drive`) are unchanged and remain the sibling task's. ⚠️ The ruling's other half — an agent-set flag is never an approval — is still enforced by convention, not by a field; the missing provenance field is tracked separately.

- docs: name the renamed launchers — `cc-personal-deepseek` → `cc-private-deepseek` and `cc-brogrammers-deepseek` → `cc-seibert-deepseek` — in `commands/open.md`'s per-vault `claude_script` example, the `CLAUDE.md` spawn-ledger sample and the `server/ledger.test.mjs` fixture. The launchers were renamed to the access-group prefix scheme in `bborbe/scripts#14`. The old names survive only as transitional wrappers that are due for removal. The dated 2026-09-13 observation in `open.md` keeps the name that was true then.

- fix: `who-needs-me.py` no longer re-judges a store-vouched row's liveness. A session blocked on a gate writes nothing, so its transcript aged past `LIVE_WINDOW`, so the reader called it `provably finished` and dropped the row the operator was being waited on — the ask vanishing *because* nobody answered it, and returning the moment the session wrote again. `store_items()` already resolves producer liveness server-side and drops dead askers as a side effect of the read; `needs_source()` now stamps those rows `store_vouched` and `quiet_session_ids()` declines to re-decide them. Rows the store did not vouch for (the log fallback, and the log-sourced `idle` promotions) keep the old rule unchanged, so the orphan protection is intact.

- docs: **README rewritten for a public audience, and the agent-operational half moved to a new `CLAUDE.md`.** The README gained the sections a stranger needs and did not have: a **Quick start** worked example whose invocation (`/supervisor:manager-spawn`, `/supervisor:manager-answer`) names commands the repo actually ships, a **Documentation** section linking `docs/`, a **`## License`** section, and CI + DeepWiki badges. It lost the sections a *user* does not need and an *agent* does — **Session tiers**, **Reaching a running tab worker** (with **Reading a worker**), **The spawn ledger**, **How it works**, **Layout** and **Status** — which moved verbatim to `CLAUDE.md`, along with a header stating the audience split. RULE `readme/user-facing-not-agent-context` (MUST) is the driver: agent-operational content in a README drowns the install steps. One stale path corrected in passing — the moved layout list said `commands/{spawn,workers,answer,drain}.md`, the real files are `manager-spawn` / `fleet-workers` / `manager-answer` / `manager-drain`. Markdown only: no script, no server module, no behaviour changed.

## v0.63.4

- fix: **clause (3b) of `agents/manager-drive.md` now names the source of each live worker's `cwd`, so its worktree probe resolves the worker's own directory instead of leaving the hop unnamed.** (3b) reads *"For each live worker, read that worker's worktree dirt"* and hands `<cwd>` to the shipped read (`scripts/restart-precheck.py:115`), but named no store to take it from — while the same file's resume path (`:143`) and five shipped readers (`restart-precheck.py:183`, `restart-worker.py:359`, `who-needs-me.py:1103`, `fleet-board.py:376`, `fleet-colours.py:215`) already read it from the session registry's `cwd` field. The clause now names that store, addresses it by **pid** (the registry directory is pid-keyed; `sessionId` is a field *inside* the record, not the filename), and **defers the resolution rule and its refusal semantics to their single home, `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker — cited, never restated**, per this file's own idiom (`:159`, `:179`). ⚠️ **It also names the store that must NOT be substituted: the supervisor spawn ledger.** The ledger's `cwd` is the directory the *caller asked for*, not the one the worker *got* — measured 2026-09-27, a worker requested into `/tmp/clause3b-fixture` ran in the spawner's vault (`lsof -p <pid> -a -d cwd`, the session's own transcript directory, and its wezterm pane all agree; the ledger row is the outlier) — and its record outlives the session, so resolving from it would name a **dead** worker's directory as though it were live. ⚠️ **Markdown only — no script changed and no behaviour moved.** The v0.52.3 inventory line recording that `agents/manager-drive.md` *"needed no edit"* was scoped to the resume argv and stays true; this is the other half of the same fact — the worktree probe needs the same `cwd`, from the same store.

## v0.63.3

- fix: a board answer releases a gate again, by withdrawing the `resolved_by` requirement — it refused the operator's own answer, which is the answer the board exists to take

## v0.63.2

- fix: **An operator-facing hard stop is handed over as the artifact they would actually use, never a scratch fixture.** `commands/worker-drive.md` named *when* to stop at the `Credential / console / click` row and said nothing about *what to hand over*, so the natural move — build a small fixture that isolates the criterion — produced a step the operator could not perform: a `/tmp` file is not a clickable surface, and a synthetic table is not the one they use. Verified 2026-09-24 — three fixtures were built for a "click the cell" criterion, each unable to exercise the predicate, and the session read the operator's failure to act as a pending wait rather than a broken hand-off. Change set: `commands/worker-drive.md`.

## v0.63.1

- fix: **The manager sweep reads session liveness through one instrument, so a prefix and the full id it abbreviates can no longer disagree.** `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-status.md`, `commands/manager-drive.md` and `agents/manager-drive.md` each restated the probe as `grep -l "<id>" ~/.claude/sessions/*.json` followed by `ps -p <pid>`. On 2026-09-26 a manager hand-rolled a **second** instrument over the same registry and the two disagreed about what an id argument means — `grep -l` is substring matching, so a prefix is legal, while the hand-rolled `reg.get(sid, [])` was an exact key lookup, so an 8-char prefix read as ABSENT. It published `registry ABSENT` for two live sessions as *confirmed verdicts*; the drive leg re-derived from disk and refused them, and had it applied them the auto-resume gate would have read as HOLDING and spawned two resumes onto two live conversations. ⚠️ **The rate is 100%, not the two-of-three the report recorded — measured 2026-09-27 against the live registry: 6 of 6 sampled sessions returned `LIVE` for their full id and `ABSENT` for their own 8-char prefix.** The defect is the second instrument, never the prefix. `scripts/session-liveness.py` is now the one reader and the four sites call it. ⚠️ **It answers four ways, not two:** `--check <id>` exits `0` LIVE / `1` ABSENT / `2` UNKNOWN / `3` AMBIGUOUS — `UNKNOWN` is an unreadable registry and is **never** death (folding an I/O error into "not live" is precisely what authorises a resume), and `AMBIGUOUS` means the argument matched two live sessions, so it resumes neither rather than guessing. ⚠️ **Neither existing registry reader satisfied that contract**, which is why a new file was written rather than an existing one adopted: `who-needs-me.py:read_registry()` has the `None`-on-unreadable rule but returns names only and no pid, so it cannot answer the runbook's `ps -p` limb; `manager-predispatch.py:read_registry()` carries `pid` and `alive` but returns `{}` when the registry cannot be read — "nothing is live", the dangerous direction. ⚠️ **Both existing registry readers now delegate to it** — `who-needs-me.py:read_registry()` and `manager-predispatch.py:read_registry()` no longer glob the directory themselves. ⚠️ **Their rules are preserved, not unified**, because changing either would be a rule change and is out of this task's scope: `who-needs-me.py` keeps **presence** ("an entry is deleted when its session exits") and deliberately ignores the reader's new `alive` field — applying the pid check there would sweep attention items for a session whose entry outlived it; `manager-predispatch.py` keeps its `{}`-on-unreadable flattening, which the shared reader refuses. Both are named as residuals rather than changed silently inside a conversion commit. ⚠️ **Scope: the liveness probe only.** The registry's other readers in `commands/open.md` are name→id joins (`formerNames`, `nameSource`, name→uuid) and are deliberately untouched — `--check` returns no such field, and replacing them would delete the renamed-manager lookup and the mis-named-spawn check they exist to perform. Change set: `scripts/session-liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_liveness.py`, `scripts/who-needs-me.py`, `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`, `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-status.md`, `commands/manager-drive.md`, `agents/manager-drive.md`.

## v0.63.0

- feat: replace the per-manager spawn cap with **one fleet-wide concurrent-worker limit**, read from the single config value `spawn.maxConcurrent` in `~/.config/claude-supervisor/config.json`. The cap's 2-per-sweep / 4-per-30-min budget is removed from every home it was restated in, and the print token becomes `⏸️ CONCURRENCY LIMIT: <n> running, <limit> allowed`. ⚠️ **The value ships unset**, and unset means unlimited — the correct shipped state per the operator's ruling of 2026-09-27 (*"These limits are artificial and should be removed … For now there is no global limit"*), not a gap awaiting input. Auto-resumes remain excluded, answering to the auto-resume gate's own 30-min crash-loop cap
- feat: the fleet-wide concurrent limit is now **consulted, not merely documented**. `spawn.maxConcurrent` (env `SUPERVISOR_MAX_CONCURRENT`) is resolved in `server/spawn-mode.mjs` and enforced in `spawnAgent` before any worker, tab or ledger record exists — a limit checked after the spawn has already spent the budget it protects. The count is live **workers**, read from the heartbeat store, so the limit bounds the fleet's open workers rather than every live session (the registry includes the operator's own and the manager's, which would make a small limit unusable). Unset or `0` means unlimited; an unusable value refuses the spawn and names the file; an unreadable heartbeat store refuses rather than opening past a limit it cannot count
- feat: **a task below the readiness bar now asks the operator instead of being held in silence.** `agents/manager-drive.md` error_handling previously read *"hold it, naming the score and the gate that failed"* — a hold the operator could not see, indistinguishable from a manager with nothing to do. The clause now posts an attention-board card carrying five fields (`--dedup-key` the task's `task_identifier` so a second sweep re-posts nothing, `--payload` the question with the score, `--context` the audit's **named gaps** plus the task link and the fact that the row stays held, `--option` the choices, `--recommend` the recommendation) and reports `Held (below bar <score>/10 — card <item-id>)`, so the hold line names the card that carries it. Written as an **application of the in-force board-only ruling**, not a new exception. Operator ruling 2026-09-27: *"If the quality of the task is too low, it should ask the user, human, me, to help to raise this"*
