- docs: **the readiness bar's home now states its interaction with the below-bar card**, so the interaction is written rather than implied. `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 2 names four points: what the card says when the audit returns a score (the score **against the bar**, never the score alone — otherwise the operator re-runs the audit to find out how far short it falls); which gaps it carries (the audit's **named gaps verbatim**, never a paraphrase and never a bare count); **who re-runs the audit** (the manager, on a later sweep — never the operator, who was asked to raise the task rather than score it, and never the worker, whose `plan-task` would park on a task the manager can still repair); and what happens when it passes **only after** the operator edits the task (the row opens on the next sweep through the normal path, the card closes as answered, and ⚠️ **the pass is re-scored, never assumed** — an operator edit is not itself a passing score, and a manager that opens on the edit alone has replaced the bar with its own judgement of the edit). Written as an **application of the in-force board-only ruling** (the manager page's § `## What a manager does`), not a new exception
- fix: **the below-bar card's task name and task link are now mandatory, and the clause requires reading the card back before reporting the row held.** Found by reading the rendered board rather than the template: a live sweep posted **21** cards whose payload read `held below bar 3/10` with **no task name and no task link** — the only identifier was the opaque `dedup_key` UUID — so an operator reading that board could not tell **which** task to raise. The card replaced an invisible hold with an **unactionable** one, which is worse, because it looks like the asking happened. A quoted template and a posted item id are both satisfied by a card nobody can act on; only the rendered board distinguishes them
- fix: **The manager sweep's snapshot no longer depends on the caller hand-piping the rendered table — the render writes it to a deterministic path and the gate reads it back by default, so the two halves of a record can no longer be stamped minutes apart.** `manager-predispatch.py` computed the digest at save time while reading the table from **stdin**, and stamped `recorded_at` with `datetime.now()` — nothing tied the table's render time to the digest's computation time, so any payload a caller held for a while landed as a fresh record. Measured **2026-09-27**: a manager piped the **10:21** tick's payload into `--save` at **10:44**, producing a record whose digest was computed against current task files while its table was **23 minutes stale** — and a no-change run would then have replayed that stale table with no marker saying so. The fix moves the hop into the machine, following the shape `--write-tracked` already established for exactly this reason (*"routing the write through this script keeps the first token `python3`, which matches the `Bash(python3:*)` grant all three manager commands already hold"*): a new `payload_path()` sibling of `state_path` / `tracked_path` / `buckets_path` at `~/.claude/state/manager-predispatch/<slug>.table`; a new **`--write-payload`** mode the renderer calls as its own last step; **`--save` now reads that file when stdin is empty** rather than refusing, with an absent payload still falling through to the existing empty-table guard; and `save_stored` dates the record from the **payload's mtime** whenever the table came back from that path, so a record can never postdate the render it stores. ⚠️ **`agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`'s write prohibition was prose, not a missing verb** — its `allowed-tools` already carried `Bash(python3:*)`, which reaches any path, and the renderer's own diagnosis was exact (*"My definition forbids me any write"*); the description, `<role>` and the `<error_handling>` escape clause now authorise **exactly one** write and say it is the only one. ⚠️ **Wiring the renderer's call surfaced an input defect:** the agent received the vault **name** while `--vault` requires a **root PATH** (a name fails open silently), so its first `--write-payload` call would have failed on every tick — input 4 now requires the root path. ⚠️ **SC5's probe is measured, not asserted:** `grep -c "<<'TABLE'"` returns **0** across all three commands. Verified end-to-end against a temp state dir: `--save < /dev/null` after `--write-payload` persists the render's table with `recorded_at == mtime`, and a payload **backdated 23 minutes** — failure mode 2's own shape — is stored carrying its **earlier** render time instead of reading as fresh. Change set: `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`, `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`, `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-status.md`, `commands/manager-drive.md`.

## v0.62.5

- fix: **A task carrying `mode: interactive` can no longer be handed a resume it cannot execute — the auto-resume gate gains an eleventh clause, and the drive leg reports `Not resumed` instead of stamping a `To resume` row.** The gate's resume path is **path A** (`spawn_agent(resume=…)`), which is headless-only by construction: `resumeSupportError` refuses `resume` alongside an interactive resolution, because the tool's tab path launches the `cc-*` launcher without handing it `--resume`. So `mode: interactive` silently made **every orphaned instance of that task un-resumable by the very mechanism the gate names** — while all ten existing clauses held on it. That is a gate reporting READY on an action that cannot execute. Measured **2026-09-26**: all ten clauses verified on disk, the drive leg stamped `mode=interactive` onto the row, and `spawn_agent` refused it; the refusal being terminal by the runbook's own rule, the task was left orphaned with **no resume path at all**. ⚠️ **The refusal fires on the *resolved* mode**, so omitting the argument does not dodge it — under a `spawn.mode: interactive` fleet config an omitted argument resolves interactive and is refused too. ⚠️ **Remedy (b), not (a), and the choice is recorded:** the alternative — teaching path A to carry a resume — is a `server/*.mjs` change that collides head-on with the standing ban in the very bullet it would have to amend (*"never fall back to the wezterm path — that path is a tab by construction, cannot produce a headless worker, and writes no ledger row"*), and it is the deferred work of its own task; this change fixes the gate **without touching the resume mechanism**. The escalation names the manual path-B recipe rather than falling back to it — a fallback is a second resume the gate never authorised. Change set: `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 6 (the single home of the mode rule), `commands/manager-loop.md` (the `To resume` branch reads the mode before executing), `agents/manager-drive.md` (clause 11, the `RESUME` row is now `mode=headless`), `commands/manager-drive.md` (the clause count).
- docs: **`docs/fleet-surface.md` § A plugin install does not reach a running session now reads the probe as the newest `.in_use` entry by mtime — the command it shipped returned several paths and could not be read after a reload.** The section shipped in `v0.62.4` with `ls -d …/*/.in_use/"$CLAUDE_PID"`, correct only for a session that has never reloaded: a reload **writes a new entry and never removes the old one**, so the pid sits in several version directories at once. Measured 2026-09-27 on one session — before `/reload-plugins` its pid `83726` was in `0.61.1/` alone (mtime 18:13); after, it was in **both** `0.61.1/` (18:13) and `0.62.4/` (20:15), with `0.62.4` installed. Multi-membership is the normal case, not an edge: the same inventory shows pid `56704` in nine version directories. The probe is now `ls -dt … | head -1`, and the section says why `-t` is load-bearing rather than style. ⚠️ **The same run settles what the section may claim about the lever:** the reload moved that entry to the installed version, so the probe reports **recovery** as well as staleness — the property that lets the section promise `/reload-plugins` as a remedy at all. ⚠️ **It also records a limit the operator should see:** `/reload-plugins` is a built-in CLI command, not a Skill, so no tool available to a session can invoke it — the recovery can only be triggered by the human in that session. Change set: `docs/fleet-surface.md`.

## v0.62.4

- docs: **`docs/fleet-surface.md` gains § A plugin install does not reach a running session — the probe and the lever, and `docs/session-tiers.md` stops claiming a restart is the only remedy.** `session-tiers.md` carried *"Restart the session to pick up new command text; do not count on `/reload-plugins` for this (measured 2026-09-22, see the vault's a Claude Code plugin guide)"* — a rule stale twice over: the 2026-09-22 run it cites tested **update, then invoke with no reload**, so it never exercised the lever it disclaims, and the vault page it names as its authority has since been corrected to say the opposite. ⚠️ **The replacement is not only a correction — it carries the probe, which did not exist before.** The harness writes `~/.claude/plugins/cache/<marketplace>/<plugin>/<version>/.in_use/<pid>` (`{"pid":<n>,"procStart":"<date>"}`) at session start, and `CLAUDE_PID` is exported into a session's own Bash, so `ls -d …/*/.in_use/"$CLAUDE_PID"` names the version that session is serving — measured 2026-09-27 on pid `83726`: recorded in `0.61.1/` while `installed_plugins.json` read `0.62.1`, four installs inside one session and nothing in the session saying so. ⚠️ **Two probes that look like checks and are not, both measured the same day and both now recorded so the next reader does not re-derive them:** the **skill listing** cannot discriminate (all 21 commands byte-identical across three consecutive versions, frontmatter descriptions included), and the **MCP server** is a different artifact class — this plugin's runs from a workspace checkout (`bun run --cwd …/claude-supervisor/server`), never the version-pinned cache, so probing it says nothing about `commands/*.md`. Change set: `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `docs/session-tiers.md`.
- fix: **the drive leg holds a file collision only on a live same-section rewrite or a large in-flight refactor — a shared declared path is now a candidate, never a hold.** Operator ruling, 2026-09-27: *"we don't start working in the fear of a merge conflict … normal little features are not that big of a deal to be merged."* Clauses (3)/(3a)/(3b) of `agents/manager-drive.md` previously held a row whenever its declared paths intersected those of **every other non-terminal task**, so two `next` tasks naming one file held each other permanently — neither could ever open, and no ordering of the topic's plan cleared the pair. ⚠️ **Measured 2026-09-27: clause (3a) alone held 17 of 26 ready rows in the Manager Layer topic**, because two hub files are declared by almost everything — `commands/manager-loop.md` by 10 tasks and `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` by 8. **The rule that exists to remove the deadlock was itself held by it.** (3a) now emits a `Shared path` **candidate** and holds only on a **large in-flight refactor of the same service or area**; (3b) holds only when **two live sessions rewrite the same section of one file at the same time**, since only worktree state can see a section. A file-level overlap with no section overlap is reported and the row **opens** — the merge resolves it. ⚠️ **The token rule is narrowed with it, and the narrowing is compensated rather than silent:** the literal rule collects git refs (`origin/master`) and slash-commands (`/supervisor:manager-loop`), neither of which can resolve to a file, so two rows that merely *mention* one produced a candidate on every sweep forever. Those two classes are now excluded, but an excluded span is **re-collected into a separate shared-token note instead of dropped**, so a collision hiding behind an exclusion still reaches a human — and collection stays the default for anything not positively matched to those two classes. That preserves clause (3a)'s own fail-closed rule at `:168` — *"let over-collection fail closed"* — which exists because a worktree probe once reported clean on a live two-writer collision. Change set: `agents/manager-drive.md`.

## v0.62.3

- fix: **The manager sweep no longer renders a deliberately-deferred task as a problem, and no longer advises the one disposition the vault forbids for it.** A task whose `defer_date` names a **future** date rendered `⚠️ problem` in the manager's table and drew an `⚠️ ORPHANED: … restart or mark hold` action line — advice the primary vault forbids on a task carrying a `defer_date` (`hold` means *no resume date*; a `defer_date` **is** a date), so following it would have replaced a deliberate defer with a mis-statused task, on every tick until the date arrived. `deferred` is now a declared **overlay**, the shape `aborted` already has on `done`: it renders the Status cell **`⏸️ deferred`** — the existing `⏸️` glyph discriminated by its label word, exactly as `blocked/hold` and `blocked/upstream` already are — and leaves the bucket set and the seven-icon legend unchanged. ⚠️ **Four sites, and they must agree** — `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` (Status cell + the orphan-candidate step, where the exclusion actually belongs), `commands/manager-loop.md` (the registry probe, which must short-circuit *before* the liveness read) and `commands/manager-status.md` (the confirm-before-printing contract), plus `sweep-gate.py`'s `bucket()` in the primary vault repo — the gate is the renderer that decides whether a wake happens, so a divergence is silent in exactly the direction that matters. ⚠️ **The overlay never overrides four cells**: `done` (a finished task is not waiting), `hold` and `backlog` (definitive dispositions), and **`⌛ waiting-on-human`** — that cell is how a raised gate reaches the operator, and a `defer_date` must never hide a worker blocked on a human. ⚠️ **And a deferred task emits no action line at all**, which is a *separate* act from repainting the cell: a `next` task keeps the `ready` key, so the cell alone would read `⏸️ deferred` while the line beneath still offered `🚀 READY TO START: … start a session` — inviting a spawn onto work deliberately parked. 34 `next` tasks in that vault carry a `defer_date`, so the case is populated, not hypothetical. ⚠️ **Both stored shapes parse** — the quoted `"YYYY-MM-DD"` and the unquoted RFC3339 datetime vault-cli writes when the scalar is left unquoted; an **unparseable** date does *not* take the overlay, since the auto-resume gate blocks on it and reports it by name rather than reading it as absent. ⚠️ **The classification is deliberately untouched**: the orphan predicate keeps no defer exemption, because what holds a deferred orphan is auto-resume **condition 10**, not the bucket. ⚠️ **The agent half is a two-step change, not one, and the second step is what makes it reachable:** step 2's frontmatter read list did not include `defer_date`, so an overlay written into the bucket list alone would have shipped **inert** — present, greppable, and unable to fire. That is the identical defect this repo's `v0.62.2` entry records for the `role:` carve-out, and it was repeated here; step 2 now reads `defer_date` in the same single pass that already pulls `status`/`phase`/`role`, with a note naming the parallel. ⚠️ **The "future" predicate also had no input to compute from** — the agent may not read the clock (`timestamp` is a time-of-day, and `snapshot` mode carries none at all), so a new caller input **`today`** (ISO `YYYY-MM-DD`) is now required, and its absence is **reported** rather than silently read as "not deferred" — the silent direction is the one that re-opens parked work. ⚠️ **And the offer half is deliberately on both sides:** the agent subtracts a deferred row from the `ready-to-start` offer exactly as it already subtracts a `role:` row, because a `next` task keeps the `ready` key and repainting the cell alone would leave the caller's standing spawn mandate opening parked work. ⚠️ **And declaring `today` on the agent is inert until every dispatcher passes it** — the same two-step shape one level up, and the first cut of this PR shipped exactly that: the input was documented on the agent while all three callers omitted it, so the overlay would have reported *uncomputable* on every run. ⚠️ **`commands/manager-drive.md` is a third dispatcher of this agent and was missed entirely** — the first cut named only the two sweep commands, and a repo-wide search for `subagent_type: "supervisor:manager-sweep-reader"` is what finds all three rather than the two the author happened to remember. Change set: `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`, `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-status.md`, `commands/manager-drive.md`.

## v0.62.2

- fix: **`agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` now carries the `role:` carve-out the runbook and the model-free gate both implement — it was the only one of the five carriers missing it, and the divergence was silent precisely because the agent followed the runbook and its output was therefore correct.** The runbook states the rule twice (its `👤 YOURS` legend in § Sweep output, and its `role: human` / `role: manager` spawn-exclusion guardrail), and `sweep-gate.py` implements it (`NON_SPAWNABLE_ROLES`, the `👤 YOURS` branch, and a roleless **positive control** in its fixture); the agent's own `<constraints>` makes the runbook canonical *and* obliges it to **report** a disagreement — so nothing failed loudly, and the only thing standing between a `role: manager` row and a spawn offer was the reader's choice of which file to believe. Measured 2026-09-27: **three such rows were live in the Manager Layer tracked set**, each a spawn candidate for an agent following its own definition. ⚠️ **The fix is two steps, not one, and the second is what makes it reachable:** step 2's frontmatter read list did not include `role` at all, so a clause written into step 4 alone would have shipped **inert** — present, greppable, and unable to fire. Step 2 now reads `role` in the same pass that already pulls `status`/`phase` (the runbook's own wording for when the check happens), and step 4 carries the subtraction: the row is removed from the **offer** while staying in the **sweep**, renders `👤 YOURS` in its Status cell, is never dropped from the frame, and is never handed to the act leg as a spawn target. ⚠️ **It is a subtraction over a field that is present, never a filter over `next`** — a task carrying no `role:` key is unaffected and still reads `🚀 ready-to-start`; treating an absent field as human would strip READY TO START from every task in the set, which is the inverse defect the gate's fixture pins as its positive control. ⚠️ **The disposition half is reconciled in the runbook, not here, and deliberately so:** `👤 YOURS` joins `hold` and `backlog` as a **third non-bucket disposition** in § Step 4 — the runbook is canonical, and its § Sweep output legend already cross-references *that* clause for the other two, so the omission left the canonical set incomplete and gave step 4 nothing to resolve against. This file reads the disposition from the runbook and carries only the operational half. Change set: `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`.

## v0.62.1

- docs: **`skills/asked-ledger/SKILL.md` now names the `--layer` value each caller tier passes, and its documented lines actually resolve.** The `## Invocation` block documented `--layer <fleet|worker>` and never said which of the two a **topic manager** claims as — so the one caller the block is written for had no stated value, and the token it would naturally derive from its own tier name, `manager`, is not in `LAYERS`: argparse exits **2** with `invalid choice: 'manager'`, at the exact moment the manager is trying to avoid a duplicate ask. ⚠️ **The tier count and the layer count differ, and that is the trap:** `docs/session-tiers.md` names three tiers (Fleet manager · Manager · Worker) while the ledger accepts two layers (`fleet`, `worker`), so a reader mapping tier-name → layer-name lands on a token the script refuses. The block now carries a tier → `--layer` table — fleet manager → `fleet`, goal/topic manager → `worker`, worker → never claims (a worker does not batch) — and names `worker` as the same token `commands/manager-loop.md` already passes to `scripts/notify-gate.py --layer` for that manager's own gate publishing. ⚠️ **All five documented lines carried a bare `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`, measured unset in a Bash tool call** (re-verified 2026-09-27: `[UNSET]`, so the bare form expands to `/scripts/asked-ledger.py` and `test -f` exits **1**); they now use the `:-$HOME/.claude/plugins/marketplaces/claude-supervisor` fallback the `commands/` files already carry, and the block says why it is load-bearing rather than style. ⚠️ **The frontmatter `allowed-tools` grant had to move with them** — it carried the same bare form, so it named a path the documented command no longer resolves to and the skill would have prompted for permission on its own claim, mid-sweep, at exactly the moment it exists to prevent an interruption; it now grants the fallback path (`commands/fleet-verify.md` already carries that form). Verified by executing the documented form: `…/asked-ledger.py list` → exit **0**. Change set: `skills/asked-ledger/SKILL.md`.
## v0.62.0

- fix: **the tracked set now travels as a file path at every dispatch site, never as an inline list — a recalled set is indistinguishable from a scanned one, and the measured cost was 46% of the topic.** All five dispatch sites across the four sources of truth (`commands/manager-loop.md` steps 2/3/4, `commands/manager-status.md` resolution + step 3, `commands/manager-drive.md` steps 3/4/5) now carry `<tracked-set path …>` in place of `<tracked set …>`; each caller writes the set its **own scan** resolved to `~/.claude/state/manager-predispatch/<slug>.tracked.txt` (the same subject key `manager-predispatch.py` uses) and passes that path, and `agents/manager-drive.md` reads the set off disk — treating an absent or unreadable path as an **absent set**, never reconstructing one from the roster, the classification or the snapshot. ⚠️ **The defect this closes is measured, not theoretical:** a 128-name list typed from memory omitted **59 of 128 genuinely-tracked tasks (46%)** and swept in **14 belonging to other topics**, and every probe passed while it did so, because the receiving agent had no way to tell a recalled list from a scanned one. The path removes the question by construction — the file is either there and scan-produced, or it is not. ⚠️ **The write is routed through the script, not a shell redirect, and that is load-bearing:** `scripts/manager-predispatch.py` gains a `--write-tracked` mode (names on stdin → `<slug>.tracked.txt`, atomic replace, refuses an empty stdin rather than clobbering a good set), so the call's first token is `python3` and matches the `Bash(python3:*)` grant all three commands already hold. **`Write` is granted by none of them**, so an unspecified "write the set to a file" would have raised a permission prompt on every tick of a 15-minute loop — the same reason the sibling `--save` block is written this way. ⚠️ **The `agents/` half is not decoration:** clause (3a)'s corpus is now explicitly "the tracked set read from the passed path", which is what makes that corpus the set the caller actually swept rather than a list the agent cannot check. Change set: `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-status.md`, `commands/manager-drive.md`, `agents/manager-drive.md`, `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`.
- feat: **the caller's per-bucket classification is persisted, so the drive leg's provenance half survives a compaction and a fresh manager.** Clause (0) requires the caller to name *two* halves, and the second — the per-bucket name sets — had no durable source at all: it lived only in the calling session's context, which is why a manager measured itself as *"one compaction away from being the caller who cannot satisfy it either."* `scripts/manager-predispatch.py` gains `--write-buckets` (JSON `{bucket: [names]}` on stdin → `<slug>.buckets.json`, atomic, shape-checked: a bucket mapped to a count or to nothing is refused, because both *look* like a classification and gate nothing) and `--save --buckets <path>` folds the staged sets into the record under **`bucket_sets`**. The three commands stage and save in one step, and the drive-leg dispatch now passes the **store path** as the half rather than quoting the sets — so the leg reads `bucket_sets` from the same record `recorded_at`'s snapshot sits beside, and a hand-named stand-in such as `/tmp/<slug>-buckets.json` is refused by `<constraints>` as it always was. ⚠️ **An unreadable `--buckets` path is a usage error (exit 2), deliberately NOT a fail-open** — the gate's fail-open rule exists so an unreadable *state* file re-sweeps rather than reporting a false "no change"; here the caller believes it supplied the half, and a record written without it would read back as persisted when it is not, holding an entire batch on a half that was never stored. Change set: `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-status.md`, `commands/manager-drive.md`, `agents/manager-drive.md`, `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`.
- feat: **a dispatch whose tracked set disagrees with the gate's own membership is now reported rather than accepted.** The two memberships are produced by different code from the same declarations, so each reads as self-consistent while they disagree — measured 2026-09-27: **154 declared against 153 in the snapshot**, differing by a single name whose only fault was a capital `The` against a case-sensitive membership compare. Nothing else could see it: APFS is case-insensitive, so `os.path.exists`, `open()`, Obsidian's link resolver and `ls` all succeed on the capitalised path, and no sweep notices because neither source is internally inconsistent. `scripts/manager-predispatch.py` gains `--compare-tracked`, which compares the staged set against the loop snapshot's `tasks` membership and prints **both counts** plus the symmetric difference under `⚠️ DIVERGENCE:`; the snapshot path is **derived** from the vault basename and subject slug (honouring `SWEEP_GATE_BASE`) rather than passed, because a caller that supplies the path can supply the wrong one, and a wrong path that reads clean is indistinguishable from agreement. All three commands run it on the dispatch path. ⚠️ **Exit 10 covers two states that are deliberately never collapsed** — a real divergence, and an unreadable snapshot — because "I could not check" reading as "they match" is the same defect one level down; the two print different lines. **The command reports a divergence and never reconciles it:** it holds no input for which source is right. Change set: `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-status.md`, `commands/manager-drive.md`, `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`.
## v0.61.3

- fix: **a gate releases only on an arm answer carrying `resolved_by`, and a close is a clear rather than an answer.** A real Playwright/CDP click on the deployed board records `answered_client: {user_agent: <the operator's own Chrome UA>, automation: false}` — byte-for-byte the operator's own click — so `operator_answered()` released the gate for a scripted answer, and **no field the store holds separates them**. Enforcement therefore rests on **provenance, not attribution**: `scripts/answered-attribution.py` returns `ATTRIBUTED` only when the item carries `resolved_by`, a field `scripts/attention-answer.py` sources from its own `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` and the board's JavaScript never sends. ⚠️ **The `message` and `question` classes join `permission`**, which the operator ruled on 2026-09-26. ⚠️ **A close stops being a release:** `classify` refuses `state: closed` with no `answered_at`, because a close records that a card was *cleared* — nothing routed back, the asking session still frozen — and the board's Acknowledge control plus the corner X that aliases it (88 of the 90 rendered rows) closed cards exactly that way. `answered_at` is the discriminator, so an item answered and closed *afterwards* still counts. `cmd_poll` keeps `OPEN` for a record with no actor at all, so a reaped close and an acknowledgement stay distinguishable in the terminal. Measured on a copy of the live store, 2026-09-21 → 2026-09-27: **151 items change reading, 147 board-caused** — 65 closes with an actor and no `answered_at` (63 `attention-board`) and 86 of 90 `answered` items lacking `resolved_by` (84 `attention-board`) — against 11,838 reaps that were already `NOTHING`. ⚠️ **The spec that pinned the defect is inverted, not deleted:** `test_a_scripted_browser_click_passes_the_predicate` becomes `..._no_longer_takes_the_gate`, keeping the falsified expectation on the record. Change set: `scripts/answered-attribution.py`, `scripts/attention-ask.py`, `scripts/tests/test_answered_attribution.py`.
## v0.61.2

- fix: **a replayed row now says so on the row itself, not only in a header printed once above it.** `scripts/who-needs-me.py` stamps every record the event-log fallback produces (`needs_source()`) and `row()` renders `⟳replay` in the scan position, so a manager reading the feed while the store is slow can tell a gate answered hours ago from a live one. Measured 2026-09-27: a store answering in 7.08 s against this reader's 3 s `STORE_TIMEOUT` rendered 18 rows where the live store held 2, and **no row said which read produced it** — the fallback announced itself in one line and then rendered identically, so every one of those rows was a candidate for action. The marker is empty when the store answers, so a responsive store's output is **byte-identical** to before (verified same-moment, patched vs unpatched: `Needs you (0)`, 11 lines, `diff` clean). Counts and classification are untouched — 17 rows before the change and 17 after on the same slow read. Four tests pin it: `test_fallback_rows_are_stamped_replayed`, `test_store_rows_are_not_stamped_replayed`, `test_a_replayed_row_says_so_on_the_row`, `test_a_live_row_carries_no_replay_marker` — all four fail against the unfixed script (verified by reverting it).

## v0.61.1

- fix: **the prose-blocker hold reads the condition the blocker names, not the blocker task's `status`.** `agents/manager-drive.md` clause (2) and a new pre-spawn check in `commands/manager-loop.md` replace the bare `grep -nE 'blocked on|depends on'` — which held **4 of 13** ready rows on ordinary English, one of them on a sentence stating the block was **cleared**, and one on the substring inside `blocked only` — with a **declared-dependency shape** (a wikilink or `blocked_by`-shaped construct; whole words; polarity read) whose condition is then checked against a named source: a **merge** (`gh pr view <n> --json mergedAt,mergeCommit`), a **release** (`git tag --list <tag>`), or a **deployed binary** (the running artifact's version). **Met → released; unmet → held.** A possible merge conflict is never a hold reason (operator ruling 2026-09-27). Measured in both directions: a false negative held a row ~11h while the merge it named had already shipped (`8a0b918`), and the false positives above held rows that had passed every hard gate. The `Held` line now renders `— prose blocker: [[X]] <condition> unmet (<source read>)`. Change set: `agents/manager-drive.md`, `commands/manager-loop.md`.
## v0.61.0

- feat: `agent_status` returns `open_cards` — the session's open attention-store cards, so a manager can see a tab worker's live permission prompt (absent from `pending_permissions` by construction) and the `item_id` to answer it with

## v0.60.0

- feat: add a plugin `PermissionRequest` hook (`hooks/hooks.json` → `scripts/permission-answer-poll.py`) that carries an attention-store answer into a live permission prompt, so a manager can relay the operator's decision to a tab worker without the operator opening its tab. Fail-open (an unanswered prompt stays for the human), binds only to the current gate's own card (refuses stale, message-class and ambiguous cards), exits once the gate closes, stands aside for headless supervisor workers (their server already parks the prompt), kill switch `SUPERVISOR_PERMISSION_POLL=off`.

## v0.59.19

- fix: **the roster move is reverted in both legs — a subagent cannot read the roster, and the tool the move used returns a different population.** `commands/manager-drive.md` steps 4 and 6 read `ListAgents` again and pass it verbatim, `ListAgents` is back in `allowed-tools`, and both `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` and `agents/manager-drive.md` drop `mcp__supervisor__list_agents` from `tools:`. The 2026-09-27 move rested on a probe showing that `mcp__supervisor__*` **binds** in a subagent — true, and beside the point: that tool lists *supervisor-spawned* agents rather than peer sessions, and returned `[]` against a 28-session fleet, so every roster join made against it matched nothing while reporting success. **The harness `ListAgents` is main-session-only** — measured, 4,867 calls across 714 main transcripts and **zero** across 16,027 subagent transcripts — so the sentence this repo "corrected" (*"You cannot call `ListAgents` yourself: … the roster is an input, never something you fetch"*) was **right**, and is restored with it. ⚠️ **The stated rule gains a third clause:** a read may move to the agent only if it is a *join*, **and** the agent holds a tool for it, **and** that tool returns the **same set** the caller would have read — **binding is not equivalence.** Change set: `commands/manager-drive.md`, `agents/manager-drive.md`, `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`.

## v0.59.18

- fix: **the drive leg stops paying for the `ListAgents` roster a second time — the manager no longer reads it at all.** `commands/manager-drive.md` step 4 stopped passing the roster to the sweep-reader on 2026-09-27, but **step 6 still passed it to the act agent**, so the roster kept entering the manager's own context once per round and the extraction was half done. Step 6 now passes no roster, `ListAgents` is gone from the command's `allowed-tools`, and `agents/manager-drive.md` declares `mcp__supervisor__list_agents` in its `tools:` and reads the roster itself — **honouring one the caller passes**, so `commands/manager-loop.md`'s verbatim roster is unaffected and the loop path lands once rather than once per command. ⚠️ **The tool is a read, not a capability**, and the file now says so explicitly: `mcp__supervisor__spawn_agent` stays out of `tools:` because binding it let this agent open a worker and skip the mode decision, while `list_agents` opens nothing and decides nothing. Change set: `commands/manager-drive.md`, `agents/manager-drive.md`.

## v0.59.17

- fix: **the `role: manager` spawn exclusion is now named in the prose that explains it, not only in the code.** `NON_SPAWNABLE_ROLES = frozenset({"human", "manager"})` has been correct since it was written, but the sites that *explain* the exclusion named `role: human` only — so a reader of any of them routed a `role: manager` row to the operator as human work rather than recognising it as a manager-tier act. `agents/manager-drive.md` clause (4) now reads *"A `role: human` or `role: manager` row is never dispatched … **neither role is a spawn target**"*, and its `Held` report shape reads `👤 YOURS (role: human|manager) — not dispatched`. `commands/manager-loop.md`'s guardrail block said *"Human-only work cannot be delegated"* one line above its own correct *"`role: manager` is likewise not a spawn target"* clause, so the block contradicted itself one indent level apart; that line now reads *"A `role: human` row cannot be delegated"*, which is true and no longer implies the exclusion is human-only. ⚠️ **`commands/manager-loop.md:173` / `:177` are deliberately untouched** — they already named both roles and are the model `agents/manager-drive.md` was brought to. Change set: `agents/manager-drive.md`, `commands/manager-loop.md`.

## v0.59.16

- fix: **The board's Jump button now brings WezTerm to the front.** `jump.py` `raise_pane()` only wrote the OSC `SetUserVar=raise` escape; the `wezterm.lua` hook's `pane:activate()` + `window:focus()` selects the pane but does not reliably activate the WezTerm app on macOS — four direct calls on 2026-09-27: Telegram frontmost ×2 stayed frontmost, Chrome / Firefox frontmost raised. Because `raise_pane()` reports the write, not the effect, the board still answered "Jumped.", so the button presented as silently inert. `raise_pane()` now runs `open -a WezTerm` after the escape (darwin only, best effort). Verified live: Telegram frontmost → `jump.py pane:1963` → frontmost `wezterm-gui`, focused pane 1963.

## v0.59.15

- fix: **the pre-dispatch gate's `--save` path honours the fail-open contract, and the goal branch stops requiring `page_type: goal`.** Two defects in one gate, both measured 2026-09-27 during a `/supervisor:manager-loop` run against `a trading goal`. ⚠️ **`--save` crashed where `--check` failed open.** `evaluate()` returns an empty payload on both of its fail-open paths (`resolve_subject` / `resolve_tracked`), but the `--save` branch read `payload["branch"]` unconditionally — so a subject the gate could not resolve produced `KeyError: 'branch'` and exit 1, while `--check` on the identical input reported `CHANGE fail-open: subject unresolvable (…)` and exit 10. The failure is silent in the direction that matters: the snapshot was never written, so `recorded_at` stayed empty and every subsequent tick re-swept the subject, and the caller read a crash rather than a verdict to gate on. The empty payload is now guarded before it is dereferenced: `CHANGE fail-open: …` on **stdout**, matching `--check`, exit **10**. The two pre-existing refusals (empty table, unrecordable state) stay on stderr, where they belong. ⚠️ **The goal branch was keyed on `page_type: goal`, which a third of the goal pages do not carry.** Measured over the primary vault's `24 Goals/`: **258 pages, 170 with the field, 88 without** — so `--check` on `a trading goal` returned `fail-open: subject unresolvable (no subject page for … in 24 Goals/ or 23 Topics/)` while that page sits on disk. The field's stated purpose is rejecting the convention guides that describe these page kinds (`Topic Writing Guide.md` carries no `page_type` and shows one inside its YAML template block, so an unscoped grep resolves the guide that *describes* topics as if it were one) — but those guides live in the vault's knowledge folder, **outside** both folders, and the exact `-iname "$SUBJECT.md"` match inside the folder already excludes them. The goal branch now keys on `24 Goals/<name>.md` existing; the topic branch keeps `page_type: topic`, and the asymmetry is measured rather than stylistic — **11 of 11** primary-vault topic pages carry that field, so requiring it costs nothing there. The page test now lives in `docs/subject-resolution.md` § *The page test* — the doc all four commands already point at as the rule's single home, and which previously carried **no page test at all**, leaving the test restated inline in three commands and the script while each command said the rule *"is not restated here"*. Change set: `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_predispatch.py`, `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-verify.md`, `commands/manager-status.md`, `docs/subject-resolution.md`. Verified: `make test` green (259 node tests, 705 python); `--save` on an unresolvable subject exits 10 with no traceback; a first `--save` on a well-formed goal writes `~/.claude/state/manager-predispatch/<slug>.json` with a non-empty `recorded_at`; the untagged goal now resolves while an absent subject still fails open.

## v0.59.14

- fix: **both dispatch templates now name the file `recorded_at` must come from, and name the wrong alternative explicitly.** `commands/manager-loop.md` said *"`recorded_at` from the snapshot record the sweep wrote"* with **no file named** — and the sweep does write a record (`manager-predispatch.py --save`), so that reading was defensible rather than careless. Measured **2026-09-27** on a live pass: the drive leg took `recorded_at=2026-09-27T12:09:12+02:00` from `~/.claude/state/manager-predispatch/manager-layer.json`, while the sweep-gate snapshot read `2026-09-27T12:44:18+02:00` — the same two-artifact divergence the bucket half was corrected for, one level out, and it is what a criterion requiring the snapshot's value caught. Both files now name `~/.claude/state/sweep-gate-loop/<vault>/<subject>.snapshot.json` and say **not** the manager-predispatch record, so the ambiguity is closed in the text rather than left to the reader. Change set: `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-drive.md`.

## v0.59.13

- fix: **`manager-drive`'s pre-open collision clause now intersects declared `# Tasks` paths against each other, so it catches two live workers on one shared non-worktree directory.** Clause (3) read the row's declared paths and intersected them with `git status --porcelain` **in each live worker's worktree** — a *state* probe answering a *plan* hazard. For an in-place editor of `~/.claude` that operand is empty: `~/.claude` is a single shared checkout on `master` with no per-task worktree (`git --git-dir=~/.claude/.git worktree list` returns the main checkout plus unrelated worktrees), so the check reported clean on a collision that was entirely live. ⚠️ **Measured 2026-09-26:** two sessions each declared `~/.claude/hooks/attention-log.py` and `~/.claude/scripts/attention-watcher.py`; neither had written yet; the leg reported *"all four live worker worktrees clean … No overlap, nothing held."* Every word was true, and the pair went unheld. Clause (3) is now two ordered intersections — **(3a)** declared paths × every other live task's declared paths, the half that can fire before anyone has written, and **(3b)** the pre-existing declared paths × worktree porcelain, unchanged in what it looks for — so a worker whose uncommitted edits already sit in a file another row declares is still caught — with its join rule now stated, since porcelain paths are **repository-root-relative** and a literal comparison against an absolute declared path matches nothing. Both halves canonicalize to **one path form** (`~`/`$HOME` expanded, a relative path resolved against the worktree root it was declared in) before intersecting, because the motivating case is a tilde path and an absolute-only reading would drop exactly it. ⚠️ The clause's own caveat is corrected too: an *undeclared* edit is a real and separate blind spot, not the shared-directory one (3a) closes — both halves take declared paths as input. Change set: `agents/manager-drive.md`.

## v0.59.12

- fix: **the drive leg's provenance names the caller's own classification as the per-bucket source, never `.snapshot.history.jsonl`.** The snapshot schema carries no bucket concept — its single home, `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Session end, says a per-bucket name set "is never obtainable from this file" — yet `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-drive.md` and `agents/manager-drive.md` all named it as the source, across the dispatch prompt template, clause (0), the `Held` reason, the report line and `<constraints>`. Measured **2026-09-27**: `.snapshot.history.jsonl` holds **195** records whose union of keys is `events · fail_open · recorded_at · tasks`, and **zero** carry a bucket key. A caller following the old wording read a plausible provenance from a file that is not this sweep's row source — the exact divergence a live pass reported on 2026-09-26. ⚠️ **The two halves genuinely come from different producers**, which is why the single-file framing was wrong: `recorded_at` from the snapshot record the sweep wrote, the per-bucket sets from the caller's own classification. `manager-drive.md`'s `--vault` note still refers to "the prior record the next sweep diffs against" — that is the **snapshot** history, which is real and append-only, and is untouched.
- fix: the manager no longer pays for the `ListAgents` roster **twice**, and `manager-sweep-reader`'s claim that it cannot read the roster itself is corrected — it was never true. `commands/manager-drive.md` step 4 read `ListAgents` and pasted the result into the sweep-reader's dispatch prompt verbatim, so the roster entered the manager's own context once as a **tool_result** and again as **dispatch text**. `mcp__supervisor__list_agents` is now declared in `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`'s `tools:`, and the agent reads it itself; a roster the caller still passes is honoured, so `/supervisor:manager-loop` and `/supervisor:manager-status` are untouched. ⚠️ **The barrier was this file's own frontmatter, not a property of subagents.** `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` § `<process>` step 1 stated, as a platform fact, *"You cannot call `ListAgents` yourself: a subagent runs in-process with no address of its own, so the roster is an input, never something you fetch."* Three pieces of evidence in this repo refute it: `agents/manager-drive.md` § `<process>` step 2 records the 2026-09-22 probe (*"`mcp__supervisor__*` **does** bind inside a subagent — all five names in that namespace were visible to the same probe, and two were called successfully"*), `agents/manager-wrangler.md` declares `mcp__supervisor__list_agents` in its own `tools:` and calls it, and the sweep-reader's own `allowed-tools` simply listed no such tool. A self-imposed constraint written as a limitation of subagents blocks the next reader exactly as it blocked this one, so the sentence is corrected alongside the change rather than left standing.
- fix: `commands/manager-drive.md` step 5 now carries the **reason** the liveness probes stay with the manager, as a clause rather than as prose. The verdict is **atomic** — death needs registry absence *plus* transcript staleness, life needs a registry entry against a running pid *or* an argv hit — so splitting those reads across two actors splits one verdict across two contexts, and a caller handed half a probe cannot tell a partial answer from a complete one. The argv half **inherits** the ancestor-chain blind spot rather than escaping it, because a subagent's ancestor chain contains the caller's (`pgrep -f` read 2 hits under `ps` and 0 under `pgrep`, measured 2026-09-15). And the `ps` half is not the agent's to run: `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` § `<constraints>` bans `pgrep`/`ps` outright. **The line is drawn per read, never per agent** — a read that is a *join* moves to the agent, a read that is a *verdict* does not.

## v0.59.11

- fix: **`manager-sweep-reader`'s step 4 no longer describes the primary vault's runbook as it read before 2026-09-26 — the stale copy made the agent assert a divergence that had been resolved, and instruct the reader to classify against the clause the runbook had already tightened.** Four claims were stale on `origin/master`, all verified live: `:85` said the two vaults *"currently differ by one bucket"* with the primary vault *"still declares **seven**"*; `:91` called **the primary vault's clause looser**, quoted it at `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md:131` as a *"genuine two-arm OR"*, and told the reader to **report the disagreement** rather than apply the tightened reading; `:92` said *"the primary vault's copy is at seven and declares no such bucket"* and deferred the fix to a *"reverse-port"* runbook edit; `:94` said *"the primary vault's copy still declares seven and does not include it, so **do not render it there**"*. ⚠️ **Both closure conditions the file was waiting on were already met:** `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md:122` enumerates the **eight** — including `blocked-upstream` — and `:136` records the bare `next`-with-a-free-slot arm **removed 2026-09-26**. ⚠️ **The defect was not cosmetic — it reached the standing spawn mandate.** An agent following `:91` literally classified a `next` task carrying an unmet `blocked_by` as `ready-to-start` and reported a vault divergence on every sweep, while `:94` suppressed the `⏸️ blocked/upstream` row entirely in the primary vault; the sweep-reader agent flagged its own definition as stale during a live sweep on 2026-09-26, which is how the drift was found. Change set: `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` — step 4 now states the set is **vault-relative, with no count authoritative and the count cited as evidence rather than carried as the definition**, records the ready-to-start clause as **a single vault-relative rule that no longer varies by vault** (keeping the follow-the-runbook-and-report discipline for any runbook that ever keeps a looser arm), and renders `blocked-upstream` in either vault. The reverse-port passages are removed, because the reverse-port happened. ⚠️ **The same premise also lived outside step 4** — `<success_criteria>` carried *"no count is carried in this file, because the two vaults differ"*, which the step-4 rewrite would otherwise have contradicted; it is corrected here too, since a rule stated in two places and fixed in one is precisely the drift this change is about. Two smaller consistency fixes ride along: step 4's ready-to-start sub-bullet no longer restates the not-declared fallback that the `blocked-upstream` bullet already owns, and the `<tasks_dir>` note's dangling antecedent is corrected. Verified on the changed file: `grep -nE "still declares \*\*seven\*\*|clause is looser|two-arm OR|copy is at seven|copy still declares seven"` → **0** lines.

## v0.59.10

- fix: **the pre-dispatch gate rejects a `--vault` that is not a vault root as a usage error (exit 2) instead of reporting a present subject page as missing.** Given a vault **name** the gate joined it as a relative path, both `os.path.exists` calls were False, and `resolve_subject` raised `no subject page for 'Manager Layer' in 24 Goals/ or 23 Topics/` — while `23 Topics/Manager Layer.md` sits on disk with `page_type: topic`. The message blamed the *subject* for an argument that was the wrong *kind*, and the reader went hunting for a page that was present. Measured 2026-09-27 at the load path: the name case and a genuinely absent subject returned **byte-identical** text (`fail-open: subject unresolvable (no subject page for …)`), so the two were indistinguishable. ⚠️ **A name is also cwd-dependent, and that is the worse half:** from the vault's own directory it names nothing, but from the vault's **parent** `--vault "my-vault"` resolved to a real vault and gated it silently — the same call meant different trees in different cwds, so a sibling `my-vault/` tree would have been gated without a word. The guard therefore rejects a **relative** path outright rather than merely a non-directory, then requires the root to hold `24 Goals` or `23 Topics`. Exit **2** is the code `manager-status` / `manager-drive` already document as *"usage error … fix the call; do not read it as changed"*, and it is the pre-existing, previously unused `EXIT_USAGE` constant. The fail-open rule is untouched: an absent subject under a real root still exits **10**. Negative control in `scripts/tests/test_manager_predispatch.py` (`TestVaultRootBoundary`) — the boundary guard must not swallow the fail-open path, or the fix trades one blind gate for another. Change set: `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_predispatch.py`.

- fix: **`manager-loop` persists the snapshot on its own tick.** The gate's `--save` call lived only in the `/manager-drive` *command*'s step 8, and `/manager-loop` dispatches the `manager-drive` **agent** — so on a running loop nothing wrote the store and `recorded_at` stayed frozen until someone ran a command by hand. Measured 2026-09-27: `~/.claude/state/manager-predispatch/manager-layer.json` read `recorded_at 2026-09-26T22:26:07+02:00`, 2½ h stale, against a loop that had ticked throughout. ⚠️ **A no-change tick writes nothing, and that is correct** — `main()` calls `save_stored()` only when the digest moved, so `recorded_at` marks when the tree last *changed*, not when the gate last ran; a wiring-only fix is not judged by a frozen timestamp on an unchanged tree. Change set: `commands/manager-loop.md`.

## v0.59.9

- docs: **`gate-owner-filter.py`'s header now states that a predecessor manager is not a peer manager, and that the resulting blindness is a documented limitation with a bounded, self-closing window.** After a handover the outgoing manager is still live and still owns the spawn records of every worker it started, so hops 3 and 4 read it as a live manager and its workers drop as `peer-manager` although they belong to the subject the watcher serves — measured 2026-09-26 on a Manager Layer handover at `gates: 6  emit: 1  dropped(peer-manager): 5`, four of them that topic's own workers (spawner `1217e759`). ⚠️ **The window closes itself:** hop 4 already keeps a dead manager's panes, so the blindness ends the moment the outgoing session exits — measured 2026-09-27, when those same four panes read `emit dead-manager` with no code change. **No predicate change ships, and the header says why:** a topic-scoped filter would need a subject source that does not exist for a manager that armed no loop (`worker-manager/<sid>.json` is absent for exactly that session) and would reintroduce the session-name convention hop 3 exists to avoid; inheriting the predecessor's spawn records either falsifies provenance or, while both managers are live, moves the blindness to the other session. The compensating control is the tick's direct feed read — read `who-needs-me.py` directly on the first tick after taking a topic over — now named in both the header and `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` § Cadence mechanics.

## v0.59.8

- fix: **the fleet manager no longer resolves a pane per `ESCALATION` row — `fleet-sweep-reader` renders the jump link and the drive agent copies it.** The per-row lookup (`python3 …/jump-link.py <pane-id>`, plus `who-needs-me.py --pane-for <sid8>` for rows the digest gave no pane) ran in the manager's own context, once per escalation row, in **both** `commands/fleet-loop.md` Step 3b and its keep-in-sync twin `commands/fleet-drive.md` step 5. It now runs once per round inside the sweep. Measured 2026-09-26 by attributing the contributing session's (`64b4a415`) `tool_result` payloads by source: the per-row pane read was **11 blocks / 63,612 B — 3.6% of that session's 1,783,972 B `fleet-loop` residue**, so this is a real but small win and is recorded as one rather than as a threshold. ⚠️ **The carrier is two surfaces, and getting this wrong strips links silently.** `ESCALATION` is the **drive agent's** output section (`agents/fleet-drive.md`), not the sweep digest's — `grep -c ESCALATION agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` returns **0** — and the drive agent holds no `Bash(python3:*)`, so it *cannot* render a link itself. The sweep therefore renders onto its `BLOCKED` / `CLOSERS` rows and the agent copies the string across. ⚠️ **The pane-less rows are the regression this had to cover:** the digest's `CLASSIFICATION` row carries **no pane** (only `BLOCKED` and `CLOSERS` do), yet the drive agent's candidates *are* `CLASSIFICATION` rows of class `parked`, which it previously handed to the caller as a bare `—`. Removing the caller's fallback without compensating would have dropped the jump link from every such row, so the sweep now **absorbs that lookup** — `--pane-for <sid8>` is attempted first, and `no pane — <reason>` is emitted **only when it genuinely fails**, with the failed probe quoted. The sweep reuses the `who-needs-me.py` read it already performs (`pane_for()` owns the session-registry + `wezterm cli list` join), so the plugin gains **no** second read path and **no** new tool grant; `jump-link.py` is never called with `--label`, which would add a per-row `wezterm cli list` and move the read rather than remove it. Change set: `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `commands/fleet-loop.md`, `commands/fleet-drive.md`. The digest's ≤ 40-line cap is unaffected — a link lengthens an existing row rather than adding one.

## v0.59.7

- fix: the manager's tracked set is **re-read on every sweep**, so a task filed under its own goal mid-run is reachable within the same session. The freeze — `commands/manager-loop.md` § Resolution's closing line, *"Print the resolved set once at manager start … and never widen it mid-run"* — exists to stop the manager being re-scoped by a **page edit** or by **inference**, but it also blocked the `goals:` declaration read from refreshing, and a worker filing follow-up work under its own goal is ordinary fleet behaviour. Measured 2026-09-26, goal manager session `c9f59bee` tick #3: a live worker filed **four** ready-to-start tasks under the manager's own goal at 18:02:13–18:03:23Z; a disk re-read of the declaration returned **38** against the run's resolved **34**; the sweep-reader held the frozen 34 and the drive leg reported *"membership is not mine to widen"* — so all four were unreachable for that session's entire lifetime, while the table rendered complete-looking and the operator had no signal anything was missing. **Step G now carries the distinction explicitly** — declaration read refreshes every firing · membership by inference never (no glob, no theme match, no content grep) · subject and branch detection once at manager start — and § Resolution's closing line was amended to match, with a delta line (`Tracked: 34 → 38 (+4): <added names>`) so movement is never silent. § Procedure item 6 now points at step G rather than restating the rule a third time. The goal branch previously inherited a section-level line whose only concrete referent (`Tracked (N): …`) is topic-shaped, and the declared-optional set (step 3) stays frozen on purpose — page *declarations* are written markings, not derived reads. The vault's `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` was amended in lockstep, including its relocated verbatim copy of § Procedure item 6, which a command-only fix would have left contradicting the command.

## v0.59.6

- fix: a `permission` gate is released only by an **arm** answer, never by a verdict alone. `attention-poll.mjs` settled a parked permission prompt as soon as an item read `answered` with a valid `decision` — a caller-declared field — so a direct API `POST` carrying `decision: allow` released a gate that executes commands. It now also requires `resolved_by`, which `attention-answer.py` sources from its own `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` rather than from a caller argument; a missing `resolved_by` is refused **and logged**, never silently waved through. Operator's ruling 2026-09-26, on the asymmetry: a permission approval executes a command, so it is the one class a script must not be able to take, while a scripted answer to a `message` card costs little and the board flow there is unchanged. ⚠️ **Scope, so the fix is not read as broader than it is:** the board renders no answer controls on a `permission` card at all and its JS never sends `decision`, so a board click could not release a permission gate before this guard either — **this hardens against a direct API POST, not against the board**, and it is *not* a fix for the falsified-probe threat on message cards (a real Playwright click stores `automation: false` and is indistinguishable from the operator's), which the operator accepted as residual. ⚠️ **Cost, named:** an arm run by a child stripped of `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` can no longer release a park through the store; `answer_permission` releases one directly and does not travel through the store, so no worker is stranded.

## v0.59.5

- docs: correct the boundary the attribution predicate states about itself. `v0.59.4`'s bullet and `answered-attribution.py`'s docstring both read as though a scripted browser click cannot release a gate. **It can.** A Playwright/CDP-driven Chrome reports `navigator.webdriver === false` — verified in-page on the deployed board 2026-09-26, not inferred from a record — so a scripted click stores `automation: false` and prints `ANSWERED:` exactly as the operator's own click does, with the same Chrome `user_agent`. What the predicate closes is narrower and still real: the **no-client** case (65 pre-field answers on the live store) and **`automation: true`**, both live-verified. The module docstring and `attention-ask.py`'s now say so, because the old wording invited a reader to treat `operator_answered()` as proof the operator acted — and it is not. ⚠️ Released history is deliberately not amended: the correction lands here rather than rewriting `v0.59.4`.

## v0.59.4

- fix: `attention-ask.py poll` now releases a gate only on an answer **attributable to the operator**. It read the stored `answer` alone, so a card answered by a scripted click read as `ANSWERED:` and a manager acted on a decision the operator never gave — the board's controls post a caller-declared `answered_by`, and the page's submit handler checks no `isTrusted`, no pointer-event provenance and no gesture token, so a Playwright/CDP `.click()` is indistinguishable from a human's. `poll` gains a third terminal, `NOT_OPERATOR_ANSWERED:`, for an item that moved on evidence the operator did not supply (no `answered_client` on the record, or a client reporting `automation: true`); callers treat it exactly as `OPEN`, and `commands/manager-loop.md` + `commands/fleet-loop.md` now say so where they read the poll. The rule lives in `scripts/answered-attribution.py` so the consumers share one predicate instead of restating it, and it fails **closed**: a re-asked gate costs the operator a glance, a silently dropped one costs a stuck worker. Pinned to the live store's own shapes, measured 2026-09-26 over 9,454 rows — 9,339 reaps (`closed` with no actor, which must not read as an act) and 65 answers recorded before `answered_client` landed (which must not read as operator-verified). The same pass fixed `describe_answer` reading only one of the two fields that carry the operator's content: `answer` holds a **single-question** item's and `answers` a **multi-question** item's, mutually exclusive by construction, so a multi-question item rendered as contentless. A contentless item is now named by its real state rather than by `closed`, which is a different transition.

## v0.59.3

- fix: pass the snapshot provenance to the drive leg from both dispatch templates. `agents/manager-drive.md` holds any row whose provenance the caller did not name, but the templates that *are* the caller — `commands/manager-drive.md` step 6 and `commands/manager-loop.md` — listed the prompt as *subject + tracked set + classification + orphan verdicts + roster + vault + timestamp*, with no `recorded_at` and no prior per-bucket sets. A manager following the command verbatim therefore **could not** satisfy the requirement: measured 2026-09-26, a live pass returned no `recorded_at` and no prior sets, and the agent named the omission itself. Both templates now carry the two fields and say why they are required.
- fix: enforce the provenance hold as clause (0) of `agents/manager-drive.md` step 4, not only as a `<constraints>` bullet. The same live pass read the constraint, quoted it — *"I could not independently verify `recorded_at`"* — and then **acted anyway**, deciding two `To open` rows and writing `mode: interactive` to disk. The clauses that actually decide rows had no provenance step, so nothing terminal fired. It is now a **batch-level** gate like (5)'s sweep-global cap, because provenance is a property of the list rather than of a row. A rule no clause enforces is a rule a run will narrate and then ignore.
- fix: `--vault` at the four `manager-predispatch.py` call sites now reads `<vault-root>`, and the `--save` step says what a wrong value costs. The script's own help calls it the *"vault root"*, but the call sites wrote `--vault "<vault>"` — and `<vault>` means the vault **name** everywhere else in those commands. Measured 2026-09-26: `--vault "my-vault"` returns `CHANGE fail-open: subject unresolvable`, while the absolute path returns a real verdict, so a manager filling the placeholder verbatim got a fail-open on **every** run and **no snapshot was ever saved**. The failure is silent in the worst way — it reports `CHANGE`, the run proceeds, and the prior record the next sweep diffs against never comes into existence. Same fix in `commands/manager-status.md` (steps 1 and 8).

## v0.59.2
- fix: resolve the pre-dispatch gate's script path through the `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT:-…}` fallback. Both commands invoked it as `python3 ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/manager-predispatch.py`, and `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` is **unset** in the shell a command's bash line runs in — so the path expanded to `/scripts/manager-predispatch.py`, which does not exist. Every other executable reference in this repo already uses the fallback form (40 occurrences); these four were the only bare ones. They appeared to work because the model resolved the path itself during the e2e run, which is exactly why a latent path defect survives a passing exercise. Same fix in `commands/manager-drive.md` (steps 2 and 8) and `commands/manager-status.md` (steps 1 and 8).

## v0.59.1

- fix: **record the attention store as the decider when its poll releases a parked permission.** A store-settled park wrote **no `permissions.jsonl` record at all** — the poll called the park's own `settle()` directly, while `logPermission(… decided_by:)` was written only on the `answer_permission` path — so a release the store made was indistinguishable from a policy allow. Measured 2026-09-25 on the delivery path's own e2e window: 2 records, both `decided_by: 'escalated'` with `decision: null`, and the settle that released the park produced neither. The poll now reports each release to an `onSettled` callback — fired **after** the settle, so a recorder that throws cannot withhold the decision — and `server/supervisor.mjs` writes the same record shape `answer_permission` writes, with **`decided_by: 'store'`**. That value is new: reusing `policy` / `escalated` / `manager` would attribute the release to an actor that did not make it, which is the false positive the field exists to prevent. `attention-poll.mjs` gained `STORE_DECIDER` and `storeDecisionRecord()`, built there rather than at the call site so the new value is unit-testable (`supervisor.mjs` cannot be imported in-process). Both directions are tested: a store-released park is recorded as the store's, and an open item, a missing verdict, a verdict outside the enum, and an ambiguous park are each asserted to record nothing — a one-directional check would pass on a recorder that fires unconditionally.

## v0.59.0
- feat: `/manager-drive` and `/manager-status` run a **pre-dispatch check** and replay the last snapshot when nothing changed, dispatching **no agent**. Until now both commands dispatched a full sweep agent on every run — measured 2026-09-25 on a 26-task tree with 25 done: 91,598 tokens for the sweep reader, 71,275 for the drive leg, 125,423 for a `manager-status` snapshot, all for zero new information. The gate is a plugin-owned `scripts/manager-predispatch.py` that ports the loop gate's **contract** verbatim — digest inputs (`status`, `phase`, `claude_session_id`, a hash of the task's `# Progress` section, the Session-column liveness word, the stuck verdict, plus the sorted member list), mtime deliberately excluded, the same three exit codes, and the fail-open rule. **Liveness is computed in the gate**, model-free, from the session registry plus the headless heartbeat store — never inside the dispatched `manager-sweep-reader` agent, which is forbidden to probe it — so a run whose only difference is a worker dying reports change and takes the full sweep instead of replaying the table over the death. Both **goal and topic** subjects resolve, so a goal-branch manager gets the same saving rather than fail-opening every run. The rendered table is stored with the digest and replayed under a `NO-CHANGE` marker, so the operator still sees the frame on every run. `manager-status` acquires its first write here — a **state file under `~/.claude/state/manager-predispatch/`, never vault content**, declared in the command body so its "no vault writes" contract stays true of everything it reports on.
## v0.58.1

- fix: give the drive leg a named read path and forbid the sweep-gate tick files. `agents/manager-drive.md` now names this sweep's snapshot — `~/.claude/state/sweep-gate-loop/<vault>/<subject>.snapshot.json`, with its append-only `.snapshot.history.jsonl` beside it — and forbids both tick files (the flat `~/.claude/state/sweep-gate/<topic>.tick.txt` and the per-vault `…/<subject>.tick.txt`). Rows handed over without snapshot provenance — the snapshot's `recorded_at` plus the prior record's per-bucket name sets, which a caller reading a hand-named stand-in such as `/tmp/<slug>-buckets.json` cannot produce — are **Held** with the omission named. Measured 2026-09-24/25: an act leg took its ready rows from a stale tick file, offered an already-completed task, and stopped after 2 rows with 4 audit attempts left.
- fix: require the walk's stopping bound to be **named and its value quoted** in the `Held` line. A walk legitimately ends on either the spawn cap or the audit budget (one `task-auditor` dispatch per ready row), and at a cap of 2, stopping at two is *correct* — so the count alone can never separate a correct stop from the measured defect, which stopped at two naming **no bound at all**. Attribution is the observable.
- fix: correct the flat-state-tree claim in `docs/fleet-surface.md`. It read *"the flat `~/.claude/state/sweep-gate/<topic>.*` tree is retired — nothing writes it any more (verified 2026-09-23)"*; re-measured **2026-09-26** the tree is **live** — `attention-routing.tick.txt` written 17:43, `attention-routing.{arms,cadence,json}` at 17:43–17:44. What survives is the *kind* of state: the flat tree carries loop-cadence records (`.cadence` / `.arms` / `.stopped`, per `docs/restart-worker.md:83`), not gate state, and all three gate scripts resolve the per-vault tree instead — so a reader resolving **gate** state there still finds nothing. The writer of the flat tree's cadence records is recorded as **open** rather than assumed to be the 900s job, and a tick-file *absence* test in the flat tree is explicitly not a gate-state check. ⚠️ **The same false claim lived in a second file and is corrected there too** — `scripts/stop-probe.py`'s module docstring read *"the flat `sweep-gate/<topic>.*` tree is retired — do not read it"* and cited the same 2026-09-23 verification. Its **operational** instruction survives (do not resolve *gate* state there); the "retired / nothing writes it" half did not, and it now carries the re-measurement and the open-writer note.
- docs: give the per-subject snapshot schema a single home. It had none — `grep` for `progress_hash` / `recorded_at` / `fail_open` across `docs/`, `agents/` and `commands/` returned **zero hits** — so `agents/manager-drive.md` was the schema's only carrier, which is how a restatement becomes the de-facto source. The schema now lives in `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Session end — the disarm contract, bullet *The model-free gate loop*, which also names the writer (`~/.claude/scripts/sweep-gate-classify.py`'s `save_snapshot()`) and records two things the schema does **not** carry: a task-file mtime (so a freshness reading cannot be taken from it) and any bucket concept (buckets are the caller's classification, per `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`).

## v0.58.0

- feat: add `scripts/transport-read-walk.py` — a call-graph walk that enumerates every in-repo consumer of a transport read, to closure, for the transport-read audit
- docs: add `docs/pane-reads.md`, the rule every transport read follows and the four severities a collapse takes
- fix: seven transport reads no longer report a broken transport as an empty fleet — `who-needs-me.py`'s lossy `panes()` wrapper is deleted, `pane_for()` and `fleet-board.py` call `wezterm_panes()` and test `is None`, and the `ps` reads in `fleet-colours.py`, `jump.py`, `fleet-sessions.py` and `stop-probe.py` return `None` instead of `{}` / `[]` / `(0, set())`, each with a named marker where the failure would otherwise have printed a confident count
- test: add `scripts/transport-read-check.py`, the three-state check that drives every enumerated transport read through broken / healthy-with-content / healthy-empty and fails on the pre-audit revision for each site this change fixes, while the sibling-repaired accessors pass
- test: wire the transport-read check into `make test`, so a site that re-collapses a failed read fails CI rather than waiting for someone to run the check by hand
- test: drive the transport-read check's third state — healthy-with-content — so a fix that returns `None` unconditionally, and breaks every working read, cannot pass on the broken and healthy-empty assertions alone

## v0.57.8

- fix: rename the drive report's `Not nudged` section to `Freshness / in-flight drops`, and require each row's leading token to name the check that **dropped** it. The old label read as a catch-all — *"anything we chose not to nudge"* — so parked and waiting-on-human rows were filed under a section whose membership rule is narrow (`agents/manager-drive.md` scopes it to *"every candidate a check dropped"*), and the false-nudge guard it exists to be became unreadable. The label now names the two pre-nudge checks, the `Drive:` count line follows it, and the prefix rule is explicit: a row that passed check 1 and was dropped by check 2 leads with `in flight:`, never with the passing check. Measured 2026-09-24 — a check-2 drop rendered prefixed `freshness:`. Behaviour is untouched: no candidate is dropped, kept, or ordered differently. The same wording landed in `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` § Step 5, which wins on disagreement.
## v0.57.7

- fix: **the necessity read's rows are the deliverable, and a count is not a row.** Measured against two released versions: the `v0.57.2` run emitted a prose summary with no rows, and the `v0.57.3` run — *after* the rows had been made mandatory — still emitted `"81 tasks needed · 43 are the goals' own output · 3 not needed"`, a **count for the `product` bucket rather than rows**. One behaviour, two starved criteria: a count cannot be reconciled against an independent hand-count (a one-task discrepancy is unlocalizable when no task is named — measured this run, 44 tracked named-subset tasks against the reported 43), and a counted `needed` bucket can never cite the criterion a given task advances. The section now states that the rows are the deliverable and the summary is a footer, carries both measured counter-examples, and adds the tell: a number written where a task name belongs means the section failed. Also repairs a duplicated `needed:` line and a lost indent in the template, both introduced by an earlier edit.

## v0.57.6
- fix: correct the falsified measurement in the resume-guard comments in `server/supervisor.mjs` and `server/decision-settle.mjs`. The run both comments cited carried no decision, so the guard was never observed to fire; the comments now name `renderResumePrompt` as the mechanism that stops the re-ask and scope the guard to what was observed. Comment-only — no behaviour change.

## v0.57.5
- chore: unfold seven bullets that sat under a release which does not contain them. Each was moved into the section for the tag that actually contains its merge — **not** into `## Unreleased`, since all seven shipped in a later release and moving them here would duplicate the entry in the next one. Found by the `Changelog Fold Guard` job on its first unattended run against this repo.

## v0.57.4
- chore: a post-merge **Changelog Fold Guard** job now runs on every push to master, calling the reusable guard that ships from `bborbe/coding`. It compares each released section against its own tag and fails when a bullet sits under a release that does not contain its merge. A pull-request check cannot see this: the fold happens in the merge, after the branch is already final, and the merge is clean — nothing warns. Wired after this repo's `## v0.57.2` was found claiming two fixes that no tag contains.

## v0.57.3
- fix: who-needs-me no longer lists a rendered closer whose session has started a new turn

- fix: **the necessity read must name the criterion a `needed` task advances, not merely count how many do.** The row was already mandatory, but a run can satisfy the letter with a prose summary — and one did: measured 2026-09-26 against the released `v0.57.2`, the output was *"75 tasks advance a goal's success criteria, 34 are the pipeline's own output, and 18 advance nothing"*, three correct buckets summing to 127 with a named `not needed` task, but **no `needed:` row at all**, so no criterion was ever named for the 75. A count of tasks that advance *some* criterion does not say which task advances which, and that citation is the reason this read exists rather than a tally. The rule now states it explicitly, with the measured run as the worked counter-example, and adds the consequence: a task whose criterion cannot be named is not `needed`.

- fix: **two more pane-read call sites no longer report a broken transport as an empty fleet.** `jump.py`'s `wezterm_panes()` and `fleet-colours.py`'s `pane_titles()` each returned `{}` for a failed `wezterm cli list` *and* for a reachable WezTerm holding no panes, so neither could tell the two apart — the same false-quiet class as the attention feed's, one layer out. Measured 2026-09-26 against `origin/master` with the mux socket unreachable: `fleet-colours.py` printed `27 live sessions · 0 in a wezterm pane · 27 headless`, emitted `"paned": 0` in `--json`, and **exited 0** — a broken read asserting every session is headless, byte-identical to the reachable-but-empty run in both the human and the machine output. `jump.py` was already loud but misattributed: `main()`'s `if not pmap:` gate printed `wezterm unreachable (no panes)` with byte-identical stderr and exit 1 for a broken read and for a healthy empty fleet alike. Both accessors now return `None` on a failed read — `{}` reserved for a real empty answer — and both gained the explicit `returncode != 0` check, matching `who-needs-me.py`'s convention. ⚠️ **`is None`, never `not pmap` / `not panes`**: `jump.py` refuses only on `None` and now names the read that failed rather than the fleet that is empty; `fleet-colours.py`'s `census()` returns `(rows, panes_read)`, and on a failed read it prints a named marker *in place of* the headless count and emits `"paned": null` with `"panes_read": false` — the count line is where the false claim lived, so a marker printed beside `27 headless` would still read as a measurement with a caveat. `fleet-board.py`, the second `census()` caller, unpacks the pair and is otherwise untouched. `fleet-colours.py --session ID --json` now emits the same `{"sessions": […], "panes_read": …}` document as the census form instead of a bare row array — a bare array carried `"pane": null` with no flag anywhere, the same ambiguity on the one output the census form does not cover. Both sites keep the healthy path byte-identical. Regression check: `JumpTransportTest` and `CensusTransportTest` drive `main()` across all three states (broken / healthy-with-panes / healthy-empty), and the broken cases fail against the pre-fix scripts while both healthy cases pass before and after, as regression guards should.

- fix: **the sweep reader's roster contract now matches the roster it is actually passed — the id join is sanctioned and the *"no id column"* claim is retired.** The definition asserted the roster was *"rows of `name [ref] · mode · status · started`"* with **"no id column"**, and step 5 read *"Join on the name, never on an id"*.
