The roster the real caller passes from the supervisor MCP is **JSON**, carrying `agent_id`, `label`, `status`, **`session_id`**, `session_status`, `awaiting_input` and `last_message_first_line` — so an id join was available all along, and it is the **only** key that resolves a renamed task. Measured twice in one manager session: `Add a Topic Phase Field With a Gated Planning → Execution Transition` against the file's `a sample task`, and `Evidence That worker-check…` against the file's `worker-verify` — both **unmatched** under a name join, both resolved by id. Reproduced live 2026-09-25 in both sweep modes: the agent joined on name and stated *"the roster you passed carries no id column"*. Both shapes are now documented and the key is conditioned on the shape — id for the JSON roster, name for a rendered snapshot — which is the file's own existing rule that *"the key follows the source's columns"* applied to the roster. ⚠️ **The retired rule's vacuous-clause warning is kept, scoped to the rendered snapshot only**, where it remains true; under the JSON shape the same clause is the correct one. ⚠️ **The caller's own task is now named as an artifact of the name join rather than reported as an orphan candidate** — a rendered snapshot omits the caller's own session by construction, so the caller's own task can never match by name.

## v0.57.2

- fix: **a skipped necessity read is now reported instead of silently absent.** Step 8 runs only when the caller passes the topic's member goals, and the rule for a missing input was to *"skip it, omit its report section, and say nothing"* — so a caller that failed to pass them disabled the whole read and left a report indistinguishable from one that judged every task and found nothing wrong. Measured 2026-09-25 against the released `v0.57.1`: a run returned a complete sweep — buckets, optional-list findings, a collision read, a count discrepancy it flagged itself — with **no Necessity section anywhere in it**, and nothing in the output said whether the read had run clean or not run at all. The skip now prints `Necessity: SKIPPED — the caller passed no member goals` and is reported as a caller bug, matching the declared-optional-set rule that already treats a caller-side omission as reportable rather than absorbable.

- fix: **the attention feed no longer reports a confident empty queue when the WezTerm mux socket is unreachable.** `main()` read the pane list through `panes()`, which folds a failed `wezterm cli list` into `{}` — and every record is filtered through `is_live()`, whose test is membership in that map, so an unreachable transport dropped *every* record and the feed printed `Needs you (0)` / `Nothing needs you.` and **exited 0**. A manager reading that had no signal it never reached the transport; the success code certified the lie. Measured 2026-09-25 against the installed `v0.57.0`, `WEZTERM_UNIX_SOCKET` pointed at a nonexistent path: 4 rendered panels and 16 idle rows vanished, exit 0, stderr empty — the healthy control run in the same second showed both. `main()` now calls `wezterm_panes()` and refuses on `None`, naming the transport on stderr and exiting 1. ⚠️ **`is None`, never `not pmap`**: a reachable WezTerm holding no panes is a real empty answer and must keep rendering, so the three states — broken, healthy-with-gates, healthy-empty — stay distinguishable, which is the whole property this feed is for. `panes()` keeps its `{}` contract for callers that only filter, and its docstring now names the conflation plus the two callers still reading a broken transport as "no panes" (`pane_for()` and `fleet-board.py`) — recorded as a follow-up rather than widened into this fix, since `fleet-board.py` is a second surface with the same false zero. Regression check: `FeedTransportTest` drives `main()` across all three states, and the broken-transport case fails against the pre-fix script (`AssertionError: 0 == 0`) while both healthy-path cases pass before and after, as regression guards should.

## v0.57.1

- feat: **`/supervisor:manager-loop` and `/supervisor:fleet-loop` take `--interval <N>m`, so a loop can be armed to tick rarely — hourly, say — instead of at a hardcoded cadence.** Both hardcoded their tick in prose (`manager-loop` ~5 min, `fleet-loop` ~15 min) with no way to slow it, and every tick is a full manager turn — measured ~320k cache-read tokens per empty sweep, growing with session age — so the dominant cost lever on an idle topic was unreachable. Default is now **15 min** for both. `fleet-loop` keeps its **15 min floor**; `manager-loop`'s floor is **5 min**, the value it hardcoded before the flag existed, so no previous cadence becomes unreachable. A below-floor value is **clamped up and announced** rather than rejected — a loop that refuses its own interval stops silently, which is the failure this flag exists to reduce. ⚠️ **Two caps bind outside the flag, and are now written down rather than left implicit:** while the session owns a live headless worker the effective interval is `min(<interval>, 900)`, because headless permission parks auto-deny after 15 minutes and a longer tick would resume a worker with a denial it did not earn; and the attention watcher is **not** governed by `--interval` at all — it keeps its own 30-min-bounded arm, so a long tick behind a live doorbell still wakes the manager once per watcher event, which is the intended shape (the watcher is the push channel, the tick is the poll). Both command bodies also record that the value handed to `manager-liveness.py --arm --interval` is the **fallback** basis only: `observed_interval()` prefers the measured median arm-to-arm period once ≥4 arms exist, and the declared value alone once produced five false lapsed-manager pushes in 32 minutes. Runbooks `Manager Session` § Cadence mechanics and its `Fleet Manager Session` twin are updated in lockstep, since they are the single home the commands point at for the tick.

- fix: **the necessity read now emits a row per task, so it answers *which* and not only *how many*.** Step 8's report listed rows only for `not needed` and `product`, so a run that judged every task necessary printed a bare summary line — and a reader could not tell which task had been checked against which criterion. Measured 2026-09-25 against the released `v0.57.0`: the read fired correctly and separated 34 per-alert tasks, but the whole output was `93 needed · 34 product · 0 not needed`, naming no task at all. A `needed:` row is now mandatory, naming the task and the criterion it advances; every `not needed` and `product` row is still required in full, and sampling is permitted on `needed` alone. A verdict that names no task is a count, not a finding.

- feat: **a headless worker's liveness is now readable by a process that did not spawn it.** A headless worker is an in-process SDK `query()` owned by the supervisor server that spawned it — no process of its own, no registry entry (it holds no socket), no argv — so every probe a third party could run returned the same decisive "not live" for a worker genuinely mid-turn and for one that finished an hour ago. The guard acts on "not live" by ALLOWING a resume, so a manager that did not spawn the worker could put a second writer on one conversation; the spawning server's in-process `agents` Map answers correctly and only for its own workers, which is precisely the caller that cannot use it. The owning server now re-stamps a file per live worker under `stateDir/live` (`server/heartbeat.mjs`), `checkLiveness` gained the heartbeat probe alongside the registry, and `scripts/live-workers.py` lets any process — the sweep gate included — decide from the stamp's age alone. ⚠️ **Age, not existence, and that is the whole design**: a marker written at spawn and unlinked on a graceful exit answers only the graceful case, and `kill -9` leaves it behind forever — which is exactly the defect being replaced, since the spawn ledger's `status: running` is the absence of a close. A stamp refreshed every 30s and read as live for 60s goes stale on its own, so the ungraceful case needs no second mechanism. ⚠️ Deliberately a **sibling** of the spawn ledger, never a field inside it: `stateDir/sessions` is append-only, and stamping liveness into it would make an append-only log a live-state source. An unreadable store returns `live: null` ("could not tell") rather than a confident `false`, because folding an I/O error into a negative is how it becomes permission to resume. The two characterisation tests in `liveness.test.mjs` that pinned the blind spot AS the defect are updated to assert the fix, as their own comment instructed rather than deleted, and `heartbeat.test.mjs` proves the write and the read across a real process boundary instead of a mock.

## v0.57.0

- feat: add `scripts/attention-ask.py`, the asking half of the board-answers pair — `post` declares a `message` question with its options and prints the item id, `poll ITEM_ID` prints the stored answer or `OPEN`. A manager uses it to post a question and end its turn instead of blocking on `AskUserQuestion`; the answer arrives on its next loop tick, because a browser button cannot send a cross-session message. A post with no producer id is refused rather than sent: an item with no producer can never be polled back by anyone, so it would be a question asked into a void. Empty `context`/`options`/`expires_at` are omitted rather than sent blank, since the schema reads an absent value as optional and a present `""` as a value. A failed `poll` names the item and carries the store's own explanation — it runs unattended on a loop tick, so `FAILED: store returned 500` naming nothing is not something a manager can act on.

- feat: **a manager posts a decision to the attention board instead of blocking its turn on `AskUserQuestion`.** `commands/manager-loop.md` and `commands/fleet-loop.md` § Needs-input now post through `scripts/attention-ask.py` and read the answer on a later tick, with `AskUserQuestion` demoted to the **fallback for an unreachable store**; `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` and its `Fleet Manager Session` twin are updated in lockstep, so all four surfaces agree. ⚠️ Permission and irreversible gates never become board items — they stay `jump-link.py` handovers, because a board button cannot release a gate and posting one there invites a relay that would be permission laundering. ⚠️ A `message` item awaiting a board answer is never auto-closed by the `CLEARED` path, which the loop commands already state.

- feat: `manager-sweep-reader` gains a topic-level **necessity read** (step 8) — enumerates by each task's `goals:` frontmatter rather than a goal's curated `# Tasks` section (measured 42% coverage), reports the size of the set it inverted, and returns exactly three verdicts: `needed`, `product`, `not needed`. Adds the **product clause** that neither `verify-topic` checks 5–6 nor the two vault-cli anchors carry, so a per-alert output task is reportable as the goal's product instead of silently clean. Carries an explicit `<constraints>` carve-out and a matching `<error_handling>` rule so the reporting path never widens the swept set, and a necessity verdict never changes a bucket, an icon, a count or a section placement. Wires the three callers — `manager-loop`, `manager-status` and `manager-drive` — to pass the topic's member goals, without which step 8 is gated off and never fires.

## v0.56.5

- fix: **`gate-relay-read` triaged the same closer pane as `question` on one run and `no-gate` on the next.** A finished turn whose `👤 You:` line asks the operator something (`pick —`, `approve:`, `you run:`, `review:`) sits at an idle prompt, and the triage called an idle prompt `no-gate` without saying which reading wins. Measured 2026-09-25 on 0.56.4: panes 1925 and 1942 read `question` at 17:02:58Z and `no-gate` at 17:04:45Z. Plain-text closers are the only class the send leg may relay, so the flip made the relay path unreliable exactly where it applies. An asking closer line is now a plain question, and the rule is checked before `no-gate`. `👤 You: nothing` and `later (on …):` stay `no-gate`.

- fix: **the drive agent can no longer spawn a worker, so it cannot open one without a mode.** `agents/manager-drive.md` declared `mcp__supervisor__spawn_agent` and used it to open rows directly, so the mode classification was skipped and the ledger recorded `mode_source=config` — a defect no prose check can read, because it is defined by a *grant*. The tool is gone from its `tools:`; the agent keeps **every decision** (readiness scoring, the prose and collision holds, the spawn cap, the pre-open disk re-read, the mode classification) and now **returns** `To open` / `To resume` rows for the caller — `commands/manager-loop.md` and `commands/manager-drive.md` — which holds the tool, executes them, verifies against the session registry, and writes `last_auto_resume`. ⚠️ **Routing the opens through a `Skill`-invoked `/supervisor:open` instead was measured and rejected:** that command requires `mcp__supervisor__*` **in the invoking session** (`commands/open.md:400`) and otherwise falls back to wezterm, which the same file states *"cannot produce a headless worker"* (`:404`) — so every open would have come back a **tab**, and a wezterm spawn writes **no ledger row**, which would make the very measurement this change serves pass vacuously. The resume half moved with it for the same reason (`resume=` rides the same tool), and the caller's path A preserves today's **headless** resume where the wezterm route would return a tab. `scripts/check-spawn-mode.py` now lists `commands/manager-drive.md` as a spawn site — the spawn moved out of the agent and into its caller — while keeping `agents/manager-drive.md` listed for the decision half it still owns. ⚠️ **An agent audit then found a second grant of the same class, removed with it:** the file *also* carried `Bash(wezterm cli spawn:*)` — a raw terminal spawn, i.e. the same bypass one surface further out, and one `check-spawn-mode.py` could not see because its grant dimension named only the supervisor tool. Both routes are now enumerated in `GRANT_TOOLS`, with a test proving the second one is caught. `Skill` was dropped from `tools:` too: its only two mentions were negative, and an unused grant is the same capability risk.

## v0.56.4

- fix: **an unroutable attention row was a dead end, and a nameless one wore its spawner's pane title as its identity.** `who-needs-me.py` rendered `unroutable — pane N does not resolve to this session` and stopped: the gate was real, open and answerable, but the row never named how. Measured 2026-09-25 by deliberately parking a headless worker (session `50193c14`, `spawn_agent(interactive=false)`): it inherited its spawner's `WEZTERM_PANE` (pane 0, the Fleet Manager's own tab), so the row was correctly refused a jump by the 2026-09-22 ownership check — and then handed the operator nothing. An unroutable row now also renders the command that answers it, `attention-answer.py answer <STORE item_id> --decision allow|deny`, and `normalize_store_item()` now carries the **store's** `item_id` for it: it had kept only the hook log's `dedup_key`, so a handover built from the row's own `item_id` would have been unresolvable by construction. The same measurement exposed a second hole behind the first — `is_routable()` proves ownership by comparing the pane title to the session's *name*, so a nameless session reached `return True` by design and kept a confident jump to a pane it did not own; a pane provably belonging to another registry session is now refused before that test, which cannot see the case at all. `name_of()` refuses the same borrow, so a nameless row renders its own session id rather than the spawner's name. A routable row is untouched: one jump line, no answer line.

- fix: **the gate relay dispatched once per pane, which costs more than relaying by hand.** `commands/fleet-loop.md` said *"For each entry … read the live question through the `gate-relay-read` agent"*, and `manager-loop.md` dispatched the send leg per pane, so a round with N blocked panes paid N dispatches. Each dispatch carries **~2.2 KB of fixed main-context overhead** before any content — the async launch stub (~1.1 KB) plus the hand-back's harness wrapper (~1 KB). Measured 2026-09-25 on 0.56.0 over 4 real gates: a single-gate read+send relay cost **5,527 B** against the **3,944 B** hand-rolled baseline, while one read covering two panes cost **1,760 B per gate**; overall 3,154 B/gate, a ~20% saving instead of the ≥50% the change was for. Both commands and both agents now require **one read dispatch and one send dispatch per round**, carrying every pane and every answer; the fleet-loop check that confirms a gate is open folds into the same read rather than dispatching per report. No behaviour change inside either agent — both already processed a pane list.

- fix: **both gate-relay agents printed a placeholder timestamp in their header.** The template read `<$(date -u +%FT%TZ)>`, which the agents copied as text and filled with `00:00:00Z` (read leg) and `13:xx:xxZ` (send leg), measured 2026-09-25, although both hold a `Bash(date:*)` grant. The header now says `<timestamp>`, with an explicit instruction to run `date -u +%FT%TZ` this run and paste its output, so a header can be ordered against the pane reads it reports.

## v0.56.3

- fix: **the gate owner filter dropped its own pane→session hop when the gate had already closed, and woke a manager for another manager's pane.** `session_for_pane()` folded the event log's open/close lines and took the newest **open** item, so a gate the feed reported but whose items had all closed by the time the filter read the log resolved to no session — and the filter failed open to `unowned`, emitting exactly the pane it exists to drop. Measured 2026-09-25 in Fleet Manager session `64b4a415`: `emit unowned pane 1908 session=None spawner=None` after 20 consecutive polls dropping the same pane as `peer-manager session=82bf6c3a spawner=56021d2e` — and **all 29** of that pane's items were closed at read time. The hop now resolves pane→session from the newest item **regardless of open state**: whether the gate is still open was already decided upstream by `who-needs-me.py`, which is slow (every session's log, the attention store, transcripts) and can take seconds, so re-deriving it here raced a log that had moved on. ⚠️ Deliberately **not** fixed by failing closed on an unresolved session — that would silently drop real gates, the broken-watcher-looks-quiet failure this module exists to avoid. Two alternative sources were ruled out by measurement rather than assumption: no `.needs.json` carries the affected panes, and the attention store held no item for that producer.

- fix: **a manager now re-arms its attention watcher and acts on a `CLEARED` itself, instead of asking the operator to confirm the clear.** `manager-loop.md` step 6 armed the watcher *once at manager start* and `fleet-loop.md` armed it *at loop start*, but a `Monitor` is bounded at 30 min — so the fleet's only push channel died silently mid-run, and nothing announced that it had. Both now re-arm on expiry/death. And on `CLEARED tab <N>` both confirm with `mcp__supervisor__agent_status` (`awaiting_input: false` = cleared; `true` / `waiting` = still standing; `null` = unregistered, falls back to the pane and is never read as "no gate"), close the corresponding ledger/attention entry on that evidence, and continue — never asking the operator to reply "ok" to a clear the manager can observe itself. ⚠️ Never auto-closes a `message` item awaiting a board answer. Runbooks `Manager Session` § Cadence mechanics and its `Fleet Manager Session` twin are updated in lockstep, and the confirmation mechanism now has a single home — the twin points at it rather than restating the field mapping. ⚠️ That mechanism is also **scoped correctly for the first time**: `mcp__supervisor__agent_status` resolves only workers the calling session spawned (`agents.set()` fires only inside `spawn_agent`), so a peer's worker returns `unknown agent` rather than a status — the fleet manager's ordinary case. For every other session the mechanism takes that session's id from the ledger/attention entry the firing is about (the pane→session hop is `session_for_pane()` in `scripts/gate-owner-filter.py` — ⚠️ `who-needs-me.py --pane-for` is the *opposite* direction, session id in and pane out) and reads the session registry, with the pane as the fallback. The commands also no longer key the trigger on one literal line shape (`CLEARED tab <N>`, which no recommended watcher emits): § Cadence mechanics now owns the shape and says to treat any drop as a clear *candidate*. Both commands now also **grant** `mcp__supervisor__agent_status`: the clause previously prescribed a call neither command's `allowed-tools` permitted.

- fix: **`/supervisor:fleet-verify` check 3 applies a stated stamp rule instead of counting "2+ files".** New `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Session stamps: a session may carry many `claude_session_id` stamps but **at most one on an open task**, task creation never stamps, and a manager never stamps a worker's task. New `scripts/stamp-check.py` applies it deterministically. It fails on a stamp behind 2+ open tasks, lists a *suspect* (a stamp on an open task whose `metrics_sessions` names other workers), exits 2 rather than reporting a missing or empty dir clean, and treats an empty `claude_session_id:` as no stamp. Check 3 had been LLM prose counting files per stamp. Measured 2026-09-25 it flagged 33 stamps: 0 were manager stamps, 30 were an ended session's finished tasks in turn (history that records who did the work), and 3 were a creator's stamp left on an open task. One false "stamp" was `goals:`, read across the newline after three empty fields.
- fix: **`fleet-board.py` ties every `needs-input` row to its own pane.** The `--json` `details` map read the same literal `"waiting on an open gate"` on every row (measured 2026-09-23: 4 of 4), which attributes nothing, so `/supervisor:fleet-verify` check 4 reported UNKNOWN for want of a pane. The pane was already known — every gate record passes `is_live()`, which requires its pane in `wezterm cli list` — and `collect_signals()` discarded it. It now returns it, and each row reads `pane <id> — open <kind> gate` (the `pane <id>` form `who-needs-me.py`'s `_PANE_REF` parses), or a reason naming that session when a gate carries no pane. Only a session's gate records attribute it: its `idle` record is not a gate, and the first cut rendered `open idle, question gates` on the live fleet.
- fix: **`context-usage.py` no longer prints a pane id it has not confirmed.** Every recorded pane id is resolved against `wezterm cli list` before it reaches the table, and a session without a confirmed pane is never offered as a compaction candidate — the state files are written at event time and never rewritten, so a pane id outlived its pane and was printed with the confidence of a live one (measured 2026-09-20: `--compactable` offered pane 109 for a 72h-old session whose pane did not exist, and `get-text` exited 1). Compaction is a no-ask action, so a phantom candidate is a run that reports success and does nothing. A **failed** `wezterm cli list` is its own answer, distinct from an empty one: ids are withheld and the run says so, because an unreadable query cannot prove a pane is gone any more than it can prove one is live. `scripts/tests/test_context_usage.py` is new — the script had no coverage at all.

- feat: **`/supervisor:attention-next` can now answer a `permission`-class item, and delivers it without a Claude-session relay.** `attention-answer.py` refused every non-`message` item, so a parked headless worker's gate could only be settled from the session that spawned it — the arm that exists to answer remotely was the one arm that could not answer a gate. The script now takes an explicit `--decision allow|deny` for a `permission` item, posts it beside `answered_by`, and prints a `DELIVERY: supervisor poll` line **instead of** a `TARGET`. That substitution is the whole change: the wrapping command branches on the line, so a `TARGET` here would have made it `SendMessage` a session — the relay this path exists to remove, and a session cannot release another session's parked gate. **An omitted decision is refused, never defaulted** — neither an implicit allow nor an implicit deny is safe, and that refusal is the guard keeping an irreversible prompt from being settled without an operator-supplied verdict. `ack` items stay refused, since they ask nothing. ⚠️ **Requires attention-controller `v0.6.0`**, which adds the `decision` field this posts; against an older store the answer is accepted and the verdict silently dropped, so this half must not ship ahead of it.

- fix: **`gate-owner-filter.py`'s docstring promised a `--check` flag the script never defined.** The module docstring described its anti-silence guard as *"`--check` reports the drop count"*, but `main()` defines no such flag — the count is printed to stderr on every run, and `--explain` / `--json` are the only output switches that exist. The docstring now names that stderr line, so the guard it advertises is the guard that exists. Caught by the runbook-reconcile pass, which checked each advertised flag against `main()` rather than trusting the docstring. Also drops a duplicate gate-owner-filter entry this merge re-introduced into the unreleased section: it already sits under v0.56.0, and the copy came back because #214 was branched before that release cut and merged after it — the release-plus-open-PR interaction, not a second change.

## v0.56.2

- fix: **a decision carried by a resume now answers the gate, instead of re-asking it.** Measured 2026-09-25: a worker parked on a permission, its spawner was killed, and it was resumed carrying a `deny`. It came back and **re-raised the identical tool call**, parking a second time — the decision had reached the transcript and not the gate. The framing that misleads here is "settle the original promise": that promise died with the spawner's process, so for a spawner-gone worker there is nothing left to settle. The gate worth answering is the one the resumed worker raises in the server that now hosts it, and `server/decision-settle.mjs` decides which carried decision may answer it. Deliberately narrow: a different tool never matches, and a park that named a blocked path requires that path — because applying a decision to a gate it never answered is the replay failure the resume validation already refuses one level up. **Consumed once**, so a decision answers one park and not the worker's whole future, and a carried **`allow` is not auto-applied** — honouring one without the live policy check would make this a laundering path with a new spelling. A carried `deny` only ever withholds, so it always applies.

## v0.56.1

- fix: **a direct open from the drive agent must carry an explicit mode, and the open gate now re-checks the row it is about to open.** `agents/manager-drive.md` holds `mcp__supervisor__spawn_agent` for its auto-resume path and, nothing at the tool boundary stopping it, used it to *open* rows directly — measured 2026-09-25: `agent_163`/`agent_164` reported `mode_source=config`, the value a site produces when it never passed the argument, on a plugin that already carried #202's prose fix. The repair is not a prohibition: the capability stays, and clause (6) now requires a direct call to classify the row (read its `mode:`, classify per item 6 and write it back when absent) and pass `interactive=` explicitly. It also gains a pre-open disk re-read of `status` / `phase` / open-box count — the ready-to-start bucket comes from the caller's sweep, so a row that went terminal or parked since then was opened anyway. ⚠️ **The enforcement is the ledger, not the prose:** `scripts/check-spawn-ledger.py` fails when a new-worker record reads `mode_source=config`, which is precisely what a text scan cannot see. It runs against the live ledger by hand or from a sweep and never in `make precommit`, because a tree carries no spawn history. ⚠️ **It judges a 24h window, not the ledger's history** — the wiring landed incrementally, so a whole-history run reported **241** offenders on 2026-09-25 and could never pass, measuring nothing; the window is what makes the verdict mean *still happening now* rather than *once happened*. Run against the live ledger the same day it reports **16** in the last 24h, so the defect it exists to catch is live rather than historical. Resume rows are excluded — they answer to the auto-resume gate.

## v0.56.0

- feat: **the manager's gate relay moves out of the manager's own context.** The Fleet Manager relays operator answers into tab workers by hand — read the pane, batch the question, re-read it, type the answer, read back to verify — and every one of those steps lands in the manager's context. Measured on Fleet Manager session `64b4a415` against a frozen snapshot (md5 `b78ef2dc23fd59fcce6dc5440bc17a80`, 21,545 lines; ⚠️ the live transcript keeps growing, so the hash is what pins the number): **234 pane-call Bash invocations / 418,706 bytes** of tool output, of which **140 calls / 205,113 bytes are relay-attributable** across 52 relayed questions — **3,944 bytes per relayed question**, and **65 of those 140 calls are read-only** (120,464 B), i.e. the read-back is the larger half of the cost. Two sub-agents take it: **`gate-relay-read`** (pane id in, per-pane summary out, `4N+2` lines; holds **no `send-text` grant**) and **`gate-relay-send`** (operator's answer + provenance in, one line out). ⚠️ The split is a **permission boundary, not a capability guarantee** — a `Bash` grant is a prefix glob over a command string, so the read leg is *not permitted* to type into a pane rather than *unable* to; the files say so in those terms and must not be read as stronger. The send leg re-reads the pane immediately before typing — the read that proved the gate open at *surfacing* time does not prove it at *answer* time — repeats a bare `\r` until the composer clears, and reports `not sent` with no pane mutation when the gate cleared meanwhile. **It never drives a selection modal.** `commands/fleet-loop.md` mandated arrow-key navigation into an `Enter to select` pane while `commands/manager-loop.md` and `docs/fleet-surface.md` forbade it, and the latter two were last touched by the *same* commit (`a6b5d0d`) without being reconciled; the operator ruled on 2026-09-25 that a modal pane is a `jump-link.py` handover, and `fleet-loop.md` is corrected to that reading so the three agree. Both commands now **dispatch the agents** instead of carrying the mechanics inline: `commands/fleet-loop.md` § Needs-input and `commands/manager-loop.md` § Path-B relay rules point at `gate-relay-read` / `gate-relay-send`, and the raw `Bash(wezterm cli get-text:*)` / `Bash(wezterm cli send-text:*)` grants are removed from both — the agents hold them, and the commands keep only `list` (liveness) and `spawn` (resume). Verified by `grep -c gate-relay` returning ≥1 in each command and `grep -cE 'wezterm cli (get-text|send-text)'` returning 0 in both.
- feat: **the attention watcher can drop gates a manager does not own, so its model wakes only for its own.** The watcher a manager arms is fleet-wide — `who-needs-me.py` reports every open gate on the machine — and every emitted line costs a full turn, a re-sent session context each. Measured 2026-09-25 on Fleet Manager session `64b4a415`: ~12 wakes in one session for panes owned by other live managers, at a measured median of 345,344 cache-read tokens per turn — roughly 4.1M tokens in a single session, recurring every loop. `scripts/gate-owner-filter.py` resolves ownership through four hops and drops **only** on a live peer manager: pane→session from the event log (`~/.claude/state/attention/*.events.jsonl` — **not** the retired `*.needs.json` store, which carried nothing for the live feed: 0 of 9 feed panes had a record, and **not** the HTTP attention store, which is authoritative for *which* items are open but carries no `pane`), session→spawner from the ledger's `parent_session`, manager-ness by the spawner having **no ledger record of its own** (managers are operator-started, so unlike a worker-spawner they were never themselves spawned; measured 600 records, 15 parents, 11 managers against 4 worker-spawners), and liveness from the session registry. ⚠️ **The keep-set is wide on purpose** — panes this session spawned, panes whose spawner is a worker, panes whose spawner is dead, and unowned panes all emit, because a naive `spawner in live_managers` test includes the watcher's own session and would silence it for exactly the panes it exists to report. An unreadable ledger or registry is **unknown, not empty**, and fails open: reading "the ledger holds 0 records" as "the probe could not be read" is the reasoning error that produced the wrong conclusion at `Manager Session.md:517`. Run live against the fleet: 6 gates, 4 emit, 2 dropped as peer-manager-owned.
- feat: **a resume can now carry the operator's decision into a parked worker.** `resume` took a session id only, so a worker whose spawner died mid-park could only be resumed with its prompt unchanged — which is not an answer. It now accepts `decision: { item_id, behavior, message? }`, validated against the park on that session's ledger record: a bare `allow` with no `item_id` is refused, and so is a decision naming a *different* park, because that is the replay case where one gate's answer is applied to another. The worker is **told** the decision; the tool is not re-run, since the gate was already answered once. Validation and prompt rendering are pure (`server/resume-decision.mjs`), so the rule is testable without a store, a server or a clock. ⚠️ Deliberately **not** in this change: checking that the referenced item was actually *answered* — that needs an attention-store client, which this server does not have, and the ledger record proves a park happened rather than that it was answered.

## v0.55.1

- refactor: **`manager-verify` extracted into a command+agent pair, and the four-command subject-resolution chain moved into one shared doc.** `commands/manager-verify.md` was **265 lines** against the guide's ~100-line ceiling, and its bulk was two things with no home: the seven-step goal/topic fork, and the ~99-line resolution chain copied verbatim into all four manager commands. The fork moves to `agents/manager-verify.md` — the matrix rule is explicit, *"the command has no business knowing the matrix — it just dispatches and prints what the agent returns"* — and the chain moves to `docs/subject-resolution.md`, which the four commands now **point at** instead of each carrying a copy. The command is **64 lines**; `manager-loop` 236 → 203, `manager-status` 134 → 101, `manager-drive` 111 → 78. ⚠️ **Two things deliberately stay in the command**, because an agent can do neither: source 3 of the resolution chain reads the parent conversation (a subagent runs in a fresh context), and `Skill` dispatch exists only in the REPL — so branch detection and the instrument call stay on the caller's side, and the agent receives `{subject, branch, instrument output}` as inputs. The move also corrects a claim the 2026-09-24 relocation left false: the file still read *"the siblings are plugin commands and this one is vault-local, so there is no common file to reference"* — all four ship in the plugin now, and the shared doc **is** the common file. Read-only is unchanged: no write tool, no mutating mode, no `--dry-run`.

## v0.55.0

- feat: **a parked permission now lands on disk, so it outlives the process that parked it.** `pending` is an in-memory Map: the parked tool, its full input and its reason existed only there, and they died with the server — which is exactly the case that matters, since a spawner exiting is what strands a headless worker. The park now rides the per-worker ledger record (`server/park-record.mjs`), which is already keyed on `session_id` and already outlives the process: written at park time, cleared on **every** settle (answer, deny and timeout alike). The attention-store item is deliberately not the carrier — it is swept when its producer exits, so it cannot be the durable half. The input is **redacted** before it lands — secret-named keys and bare bearer tokens, mirroring `redact()` in `~/.claude/hooks/attention-log.py`, the sibling path that already persists tool detail — because *full* and *unredacted* are different properties and only the first is needed to resume. Also corrects the resume guard's comment, which claimed a headless worker *"has no pid for the registry and no argv for any process probe"* and had the guard's scope reasoned from it; measured 2026-09-25, it has both — the SDK binary is its pid and it registers in `~/.claude/sessions/` while alive.

## v0.54.6

- fix: **a resume could land on a worker parked on a permission, putting two writers on one conversation.** The in-process resume guard matched `status === 'running'` only, but a worker blocked inside a tool call carries `blocked-on-permission` — so a parked worker slipped the guard, fell through to `checkLiveness`, which answers `live: false` for a headless worker (no pid, no registry entry, and the registry holds none of them), and was resumable while still parked. The test is now finished-vs-not, extracted to `server/resume-guard.mjs` with tests, so a new status is a deliberate edit there rather than a silent pass-through. Surfaced while planning a decision-carrying resume, which would have widened the same hole.

## v0.54.5

- fix: **a jump handover named a command and an actor instead of handing over the link.** `commands/manager-loop.md` told a manager to *"batch a `you run: /supervisor:jump <pane-id>` line"*, while the same file's `**A relay never releases a gate**` clause and `commands/fleet-loop.md` hand over the one-line output of `scripts/jump-link.py` — verb-free, as `manager-status` / `fleet-status` render their `jump:` rows. The command form is the one that failed: measured 2026-09-21, an operator answered a bare `y` against `you run: /supervisor:jump 1121` and released nothing, twice, the second time with the gate genuinely open. `/supervisor:jump` is the **executor** and these commands **emit links without running it** (`commands/jump.md:2`, `docs/session-tiers.md:97-98`), so the handover now takes the verb-free link form at all three sites, with the gate release stated as the operator's **own keystroke in that pane**. The passage also gains a **one-action-per-line** rule, so a second action (e.g. `/reload-plugins`) can never be bundled onto the handover and make the operator's reply ambiguous between them. Not a namespace change — `/jump` → `/supervisor:jump` was fixed separately. ⚠️ This does **not** settle the standing ask *"jump me to a raised gate without asking when exactly one is open"* — that describes the manager running the jump, a separate behaviour with no command home yet.

## v0.54.4

- fix: **`session_transcript_age()`'s docstring claimed a reuse the body does not make.** It read *"Reuses `fleet-sessions.py`'s own `last_message_ts()` rather than re-deriving the read, so the two cannot drift on what 'last written' means — it reads the tail of the jsonl and falls back to mtime."* The body does neither: `scripts/who-needs-me.py:461` computes `age = time.time() - max(os.path.getmtime(h) for h in hits)` — the transcript file's own mtime, with no tail read and no parse. The two functions genuinely disagree on what "last written" means (`last_message_ts()` at `scripts/fleet-sessions.py:56-76` takes the last line's embedded `timestamp` and reaches `jsonl.stat().st_mtime` only after the whole loop falls through), so the docstring was worse than silent: it misled in the one place a reader goes to settle that exact question, which is the trap *"`touch -t` moves mtime, but a consumer that parses the last JSON line's embedded `timestamp` never reads it"* names. **Which side was wrong is settled by the consumer, not by preference:** `agents/manager-drive.md:85` drops a candidate *"when its mtime is inside `scripts/who-needs-me.py`'s `LIVE_WINDOW` (5 min)"* — it reads mtime — so the body is correct and the docstring is the defect. The docstring now states the reading and names the contrast; the `inf`-on-absent semantics and the memoization note are preserved, except that the note no longer says "tail-read", because there is no tail read. ⚠️ **No behaviour change**: the diff touches the docstring block only — no hunk lands on `LIVE_WINDOW`, on `reclassify_idle()`, or on a threshold, and `scripts/fleet-board.py:741` still reuses the function, so the *"one definition, two renderings"* contract `agents/manager-drive.md:87` states is intact.

## v0.54.3

- fix: **an `asked-of-you` held in a worker's pane is refused rather than recorded — the entry could never close.** This kind's only close path is `answer`, and `answer` needs the manager session to RECEIVE the operator's words; for a gate in a tab worker's pane the operator releases it with their own keystroke *there*, and the manager is forbidden from receiving it (*"a relay never releases a gate"*), so the entry sat open forever — indistinguishable from a question genuinely still outstanding, and the one surface built to answer "what is still asked of me" answering it wrongly and permanently. Measured 2026-09-21 in the `the topic phase model` manager session: entries `7fbcec86`, `d1161bc4` and `4f79f16e` were created for pane gates and not one could close on its own — all three were eventually cleared by a manual batch pick that stamped them `operator answered in session: y`, an attribution a later reader cannot tell from a genuine answer — and `f9c512c1` is still open over a gate whose pane no longer exists (36 `asked-of-you` entries open fleet-wide against 71 closed, counted 2026-09-25). `add` now takes `--held-in-pane <pane-id>`: **refused** on `asked-of-you`, naming the hand-over instead (`jump-link.py <pane-id>`, never a relay, and a direct go is needed), and recorded as provenance on `asked-of-me` / `pushed`, which close on their task file. Nothing is lost by not recording a pane gate — a tab worker's gate is a *blocked session*, so `notify-gate.py` still publishes it to the phone and the sweep still renders it `waiting-on-human`. The rule ships in `skills/open-items/SKILL.md` (the ledger rules' single home) and in both vault runbooks, and the regression test fails against the pre-change script on the assertion that separates the rule's refusal from argparse's `unrecognized arguments` — an exit-code-only assertion would pass pre-change and pin nothing. Chosen over a `released-in-pane` close path, whose evidence is identical to `asked-of-me` / `pushed`'s close condition and would have recorded a false release on the very entry it was meant to fix (`f9c512c1`'s gate was **superseded**, not released), and over a batch-confirmation round, which had already been run once and produced the forged attribution above.

## v0.54.2

- fix: **a spawn site can be defined by a tool grant rather than by its text — and the mode rule now reaches those too.** `agents/manager-drive.md` holds `mcp__supervisor__spawn_agent` for its auto-resume path, and nothing at the tool boundary stopped it calling the tool to *open* a row instead. Measured 2026-09-24: it opened two directly (`agent_143`, `agent_144`, 22:19Z, parent `1217e759`), each carrying `mode: interactive` **on disk** yet reporting `mode_source=config` — the field written and the spawn ignoring it, which is precisely the defect the mode rule exists to remove. Its own prose said *"Open with `/supervisor:open` via `Skill`. Do not derive or duplicate the headless/interactive decision"*, and it bypassed that anyway: **the grant permitted what the prose forbade.** The file now states that `spawn_agent` is for `resume=` only, and that a direct open must classify and pass the argument because it has no Step 0.6 behind it; `commands/fleet-loop.md` carries the same note for the same reason. `scripts/check-spawn-mode.py` gains a **grant dimension** — any file whose frontmatter grants the tool must reference the rule's home — because a text scan reads what a file *says* and cannot see a site defined by what it is *permitted*. `agents/manager-drive.md` joins `SPAWN_SITES` for the same reason, and two regression tests pin the grant case. ⚠️ Recorded as a limit of the enforcement, not only a bug in one file: the check that shipped in #178 verified prose, and this site was never prose.

## v0.54.1

- fix: **the resume recipe passes the colour chip unquoted, so a restarted worker comes up pink instead of default.** `resume_command()` wrote `"/color 'pink'"` — the single quotes are *shell* syntax in `commands/open.md` Step 3.1, where they break out of the outer `bash -lc '…'` string so `$CHIP` expands. This string is built in Python, where there is no outer quoting to break out of, so the copied escape became a **literal** quote and Claude Code rejected the argument — `Invalid color "'pink'"`. Found by the first live run of `/supervisor:worker-restart`: both restarts (one mid-work, one gate-held) came up with the rejected chip and kept the default colour. It matters because the colour is the fleet's only role cue — `open.md` calls it *"the only fleet-wide cue for which role a session plays"* — and a restarted worker that should be pink is exactly the mis-coloured session that nearly got a manager relaunched as a worker on 2026-09-18. The quotes are dropped, deliberately, and the copy's divergence from the recipe is now documented in the function's own docstring so a future re-copy does not reintroduce them.

## v0.54.0

- feat: **`/supervisor:worker-restart` — kill, resume and re-orient ONE named worker in a single verb.** The Fleet Manager did this by hand on 2026-09-20 and forgot the third step: a resumed session replays its own pre-kill transcript, so it cannot tell on its own that the thing it was waiting for already happened, and the replayed panel also read to the operator as a live gate. The command is the **orchestrator**, not a second kill mechanism — it calls `scripts/restart-worker.py` for the kill+resume leg rather than reimplementing it, so exactly one such mechanism exists, and a new read-only sibling `scripts/restart-precheck.py` owns the two things the script does not. **(1) The pre-kill worktree probe.** It refuses on tracked changes **only when the target is the sole live session in that worktree**, and the discriminator is sharing rather than dirt — measured against the live fleet: **19 of 25** interactive sessions sat in a dirty worktree, and all **17** primary-vault sessions were dirty by the *same three* tracked files, none of them the target's own. A refusal on dirt alone would have blocked 76% of the fleet on somebody else's work. Untracked paths never refuse. **(2) The cause-of-death branch**, per `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker: the transcript tail's last `👤 You:` line classifies (absent, `nothing`, or a parked `later (on …)` → **mid-work**; a real gate → **gate-held**), the pane text then **vetoes** (a selection modal or a non-empty composer means type nothing), and the veto wins. ⚠️ **The restart-request death needs no third branch**: a harness `restart Claude Code` gate is not a `👤 You:` line, so the 2026-09-20 case already lands in mid-work by construction — no heuristic added. The classifier is imported from `who-needs-me.py` rather than re-derived, so the two cannot drift on what a transcript tail says. ⚠️ **`docs/session-tiers.md` records the one exception to its own prefix rule**: `worker-restart`'s prefix names its **target**, not its invoker, because the `worker-*` tier is the one tier whose own rule forbids resuming or spawning a session and a worker cannot restart itself — `restart-worker.py` refuses a `busy-target`, and a session running the command is by definition mid-turn. Two bugs the first live runs found and the tests now pin: a stale registry entry must not buy a refusal exemption (`pid_is_alive`), and an absent transcript (`int(inf)`) crashed the report before the classifier could return `undetermined`.

## v0.53.0

- feat: **`fleet-board.py` emits a `saturation` value — the fleet working ratio, `(busy + shell) / live sessions` — as the fleet's saturation signal in place of router tok/s.** The numerator counts the **raw registry status**, deliberately not `counts["running"]`: the buckets apply a precedence (`problem` → `needs-input` → `running` → `idle`), so a `busy` session holding an open gate is bucketed `needs-input` and a bucket-based numerator would drop it — undercounting precisely the sessions that *are* working. On the board's own fixtures the two readings disagree, 3 of 6 against 1 of 6, and that gap is what `TestSaturation` pins. The raw status is also what `ListAgents` reports, which is what makes the printed numerator/denominator cross-checkable by hand against the same moment. `ratio` is `None`, never `0.0`, for an empty fleet, so "no live sessions" and "nothing working" cannot render alike. Offline by construction: the tok/s figure that sits beside the ratio stays the caller's job (`commands/fleet-loop.md` reads it from `claude-metrics.sh`), because resolving it here would make a script that runs every sweep tick depend on six credentialed Prometheus sources and break the pure-fixture tests the board is built on.

## v0.52.3

- fix: **the resume recipe and every call site of it now pass `--cwd`, so a resumed session lands in its own directory instead of wezterm's.** PR #195 fixed this inside `restart-worker.py` only; the recipe itself (`docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker, path B) and its call site (`commands/open.md` § 3.1) still spawned without the flag, so **every other caller** kept inheriting *wezterm's* working directory — `$HOME` for a server started from a home directory. Claude Code then stops on *"Accessing workspace /Users/&lt;user&gt; — do you trust this folder?"* and registers **no pid at all**, so a resume that worked reads as a no-op in both directions: a caller checking the registry finds nothing and reads "did not take", a caller checking the spawn's exit code reads "took", and neither reading names the trust dialog. The value now comes from the session registry — `~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json` carries a `cwd` field (verified 2026-09-24: 26 of 26 records on this host) — and is resolved **before** the spawn, never guessed; a session whose record carries no `cwd` is refused rather than resumed into the wrong tree. The sweep is fleet-wide and its inventory is recorded in the PR body: `--cwd` was measured at **0** occurrences in both files before the change, and `commands/manager-loop.md` / `agents/manager-drive.md` were scanned and needed no edit — neither carries a resume argv, both defer to the recipe by name, and `manager-loop.md` already passes `cwd` explicitly on path A. `restart-worker.py`'s docstring, which asserted that `--cwd` was *absent* from the copied recipe, is corrected to match.

## v0.52.2

- fix: **`restart-worker.py` resumes a worker into its own working directory, not wezterm's.** The resume recipe copied from `commands/open.md` Step 3.1 carries no `--cwd`, and `wezterm cli spawn` otherwise inherits wezterm's own working directory — for a server started from a home directory, `$HOME`. Claude Code then stops on *"Accessing workspace /Users/&lt;user&gt; — do you trust this folder?"* and registers **no pid at all**, so a kill+resume that worked perfectly looked like a no-op. Measured 2026-09-24: a session whose real cwd was `~/Documents/Obsidian/my-vault` resumed into `$HOME`, stalled on that dialog, and produced no registry entry. ⚠️ **Only the real end-to-end restart could have found this** — every unit test passed, the mechanical funnel was clean, and a dry run reported success, because none of them spawn a tab. The cwd now comes from the registry record and is passed as `--cwd`; it is read **before** the kill, so a session whose record carries no cwd is refused rather than killed and left stranded. The docs' allow-rule section and the resume-recipe note are unchanged in shape — the recipe simply now carries the one argument it was missing.

## v0.52.1

- fix: **`restart-worker.py` can now actually reach its precondition-4 check — two bugs made it refuse every restart.** Both were found by running the deployed `0.52.0` copy against live sessions, which is precisely what the end-to-end step exists for; neither is visible from the unit tests, and the first is invisible from a synthetic `startedAt`. **(1) `startedAt` is milliseconds.** The live registry writes `1790269714645`, but `parse_started()` read a bare number as *seconds*, placing the session's start some 57,000 years out — so `load_mtime > started` was false for every session and `stale-load-path` refused unconditionally. That is the check failing in the direction that hides real fixes: it never once permitted a restart, and every refusal it produced read as correct. The unit is now detected by magnitude (past `1e11` is milliseconds). **(2) The newest load path was chosen by directory mtime.** Two versions installed in one operation get near-identical mtimes and the tie falls either way — measured: `0.51.2` and `0.52.0` differed by 3ms with the *older* one later, so an mtime-max selected `0.51.2` and precondition 4 then compared the session against the wrong copy. Selection is now by version, ordered numerically (`0.10.0` > `0.9.0`), with that directory's own mtime still answering "was it installed after this session started". Verified against live sessions: an idle worker now clears (`role: worker · status: idle · load path: 0.52.0`) while a busy target still refuses `busy-target`.

## v0.52.0

- feat: **`scripts/restart-worker.py` restarts one stale idle worker session under a narrow allow rule.** A manager's standing mandate (`65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` § Gate triage class A) already covers killing and restarting a live worker whose loaded code is stale, but on 2026-09-24 the auto-mode classifier denied even a read-only wait-for-idle loop on the registry as "Interfere With Workloads" — so the mandate could not be exercised without a permission rule, and a rule on bare `kill` would be far too broad. This script is the narrow, allowlistable way to exercise it: one session id in, one registry pid killed, the same session resumed in a new tab. Seven refusals each exit 1 with a stable reason token — `unknown-session-id`, `ambiguous-pid`, `busy-target`, `headless-target`, `manager-target`, `role-undetermined`, `stale-load-path` — and every one fires *before* anything is signalled, so no code path reaches `os.kill` on a target it has not cleared. The role predicate is not reimplemented: it calls `fleet-board.py`'s own `vault_index()`, `loop_slugs()`, `manager_subject()` and `colour_census()` through the repo's existing importlib sibling-import, honouring all four of `build_grouping()` Rule 1's signals (the root name, `manager_subject()`'s two sources, and the orange colour) so the script and the board can never disagree about who is a manager. Runbook precondition 4 ("something must actually load differently") is enforced rather than remembered: the newest load-path copy is compared against the session's own start, and a load path no newer than the session refuses as `stale-load-path`. The resume recipe is a deliberate copy of `commands/open.md` Step 3.1's — a markdown command is not executable, which is why `commands/worker-restart.md` is scoped to call this script rather than reimplement kill+resume.

## v0.51.2

- fix: **a headless worker is no longer reported `done` on a mid-turn SDK `result`.** The message loop settled a worker on *any* `message.type === 'result'`, so a `result` arriving mid-turn — measured 2026-09-22, a resumed worker read `done` with `num_turns: 0` two minutes after spawn while its transcript gained 113 records — reported a live worker as finished and filed its ledger record as terminal (`ended_at` set) while it was still writing. It matters because the failure runs in the dangerous direction: a manager reading `done` right after a spawn concludes the spawn failed, and the retry it invites is a **second writer on one conversation** — the exact condition the server's own resume guard exists to prevent, on a path where the guard is not consulted. The loop now **captures** each `result` and settles the terminal `status` plus the ledger's terminal patch **once, after the stream ends**; a stream that ended with no `result` at all still settles `done` via the no-result path. The trigger was a degenerate `result` from the bundled CLI, which stopped recurring on its own at 2.1.280 — so a fix proven only by "it stopped happening" would be proven by absence, and the guarantee is pinned by unit tests instead. To make that possible the loop moved to `server/agent-loop.mjs`: `supervisor.mjs` exports nothing and connects a stdio server at load, so it cannot be imported by a test, and the loop previously ran as a fire-and-forget IIFE that nothing could await. The tested loop and the shipped loop are now the same code, with `writeLedger` injected so the ledger's write timing is directly observable.

## v0.51.1

- fix: **`worker-drive`'s producer check now names where a manager's subagent legs write, so a healthy loop can no longer be reported as a silent producer.** §1's carve-out told a session to *"Verify the producer can still fire it"* and §0b prescribed mechanical wait verification (`pgrep` / `docker ps` / `gh`), but neither covered an upstream that **is** a subagent: a manager's sweep and drive legs run as subagents, so their work never lands in the manager session's own transcript and a `grep` over it returns nothing on a round that completed. Step 4 now requires reading a leg's own transcript — `~/.claude/projects/<project>/<manager-session-id>/subagents/agent-*.jsonl`, where each leg's final assistant turn is its handback — and states that the obvious glob (`~/.claude/projects/*/agent-*.jsonl`) is two levels too shallow and returns zero files, which reads exactly like a producer that never fired. Only after reading a leg's handback may the producer be called silent. Measured 2026-09-24: a `/supervisor:worker-drive` run read the manager transcript alone, found no `asked-ledger` executions and no `AskUserQuestion`, and wrote *"the producer is not firing — measured this run, and it is the load-bearing finding"* into a task file; the next run read a completed `supervisor:fleet-drive` leg's handback reporting *"Of the 12 parked sessions, 5 are waiting on you"* and naming five panes. The producer had been alive the whole time — the verdict was produced by an instrument that cannot see the producer's work, which is the failure §1's own closing line names.

## v0.51.0

- fix: **a non-task-anchored spawn now carries a mode decision instead of silently falling through to the fleet config.** `commands/manager-spawn.md` handles a task-anchored brief by reading the task's `mode:` field, but for the bare case it said only *"reach for this command when the brief is not task-anchored"* — and never what to pass. The shared rule omits the argument only when `mode:` is absent, and a bare brief has no field, so the spawn omitted it and reported `mode_source=config`: **indistinguishable in the ledger from a site that never decided at all.** That matters beyond tidiness — PR #178's own SC3 requires **0** `config`-sourced new-worker spawns over a working day, so a single bare spawn would have failed the measurement for a reason that was a path-mix artefact rather than a wiring defect. The bare path now classifies the brief and passes the argument explicitly — `interactive=false` only on positive evidence the work never needs a human mid-flight, `interactive=true` otherwise — since with no field to store it in, the argument *is* the only record of the decision. Omission stays reserved for a *task* whose `mode:` is absent, which Step 0.6 resolves upstream by writing the field before the spawn. § Spawn a worker item 6 carries the same distinction.

## v0.50.6

- fix: **`manager-drive`'s open gate can reach the auditor again — the audit binding was a skill name that has never existed.** Clause (1) scored each ready-to-start row with `Skill("vault-cli:task-auditor")`, but `task-auditor` is an **agent** (subagent type `vault-cli:task-auditor`) and the drive agent's `tools:` line carried no `Task`, so no invocation could reach it: `Skill` answered `Unknown skill: vault-cli:task-auditor` and every row was held with no score. Measured 2026-09-24 on the drive E2E fixture run (Set A sweep 1 against the fixture goal): 4 of 4 rows held, `opened 0` — the prose-blocker and file-collision holds fired correctly on their own clauses, so the audit clause was the only broken one and the shipped open step was inert. The name it resembles, `vault-cli:audit-task`, is the *command* that dispatches this same agent, so routing through `Skill` added a hop without changing the target. `Task` joins `tools:` and clause (1) dispatches the agent directly, with the readiness prompt it must use — the five hard gates and the terminal `READINESS:` line — **referenced** to its single home, `commands/open.md` § Step 1.5, rather than restated. ⚠️ **The grant is deliberately narrow, and the file now says so:** `Task` is a generic dispatch primitive and `vault-cli:task-auditor` is its only permitted target, because an open dispatched through `Task` would bypass `commands/open.md` § Step 0.6 and leave the row's `mode` undecided — the same defect the `spawn_agent` fallback is already forbidden for. The introducing commit (`30b16cf`) widened the grant with `Skill` on the reasoning that *"both acts are reachable as skills"*; that held for `/supervisor:open` and not for the auditor, so the two are now granted separately. ⚠️ **What this does not claim:** the readiness prompt is a *contract* the drive needs as much as the address — a bare auditor report carries a score but no hard-gate list, so swapping the name alone would have fixed the address and dropped the semantics clause (1) is built on. The fixture evidence run itself stays with *a separate task*, in the manager session.

## v0.50.5

- fix: **`mode: interactive` is now honoured, not only `mode: headless` — the field was inert in one direction.** PR #178 wired the mode decision to the spawn argument, but only for `headless`: a task carrying `mode: interactive` on disk spawned with the argument **omitted**, so the fleet config decided. The outcome looked correct because the config reads `interactive` — which is exactly why it went unnoticed: right for the wrong reason. Move `spawn.mode` to `headless` and every task that had *explicitly* declared itself interactive flips with it. The omitted argument also reports `mode_source=config`, **indistinguishable from a spawn site that never decided at all**, so no ledger measurement can separate a wired spawn from an unwired one — and #178's own SC3, which requires **0** `config`-sourced new-worker spawns, could not have passed by construction, since the same design says `headless` should be rare. `commands/open.md` § Step 3, `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-spawn.md` and § Spawn a worker item 6 now pass the argument whenever `mode:` is present — `false` for `headless`, `true` for `interactive` — omitting it only when the field is absent. `scripts/check-spawn-mode.py` asserts **both** directions, so a one-directional site fails `make precommit`, and a regression test pins that case.

## v0.50.4

- fix: **`worker-drive`'s report template counted four hard stops where the contract now names five.** v0.50.3 added the **Other-owned** row and updated both prose references, but the §8 report shape still read `STOPPED: <one of the four hard stops, named>` — the exact line a session had to improvise against when every remaining row belonged to another session, so the one place the new row was most needed still excluded it. ⚠️ **Recorded as a partial sweep, not a fresh defect:** the same change fixed the count in two places and missed the third — the failure mode this command exists to catch, committed by the fix for it. Found by grepping the **deployed load path** for the old wording rather than trusting the merge, which is also why it was found at all.

## v0.50.3

- fix: **`worker-drive` stops contradicting the two artifacts that own its contracts.** Two rows in `commands/worker-drive.md` disagreed with the documents they defer to, both found in one run on 2026-09-24. **(1) Voice mode.** The speak-the-verdict row listed `interview` alongside `on` and `narrate`, while the tts-mcp playbook says in as many words *"⚠️ `interview` speaks questions, not answers"* and marks its own **Answers** column ❌ for that mode — so a session following this command spoke verdicts in a mode whose contract keeps answers on screen. The row now separates `interview` out and names the playbook as the authority. **(2) The hard stops.** The contract read *"Continue until one of exactly **four hard stops**. Nothing else stops you"*, but §0b's owner table already permits stopping when every remaining row belongs to another session — *"Another session / person … **Nothing.** Preempting it sabotages their run. Say so explicitly."* With no row to name that case, a session that had driven everything improvised a stop and had to flag its own improvisation in the report. A fifth row, **Other-owned**, makes it a stop the report shape can carry.

## v0.50.2

- fix: **the `close-me` bucket's liveness verdict moves to the caller, where it can actually be made.** The bucket is defined as *"the task is terminal (`completed`/`aborted`, all SCs `[x]`) but its worker session still runs"* — a disk half the reader can evaluate and a liveness half it may not (`agents/manager-sweep-reader.md:31`: no `pgrep`/`ps`, no transcript recency). Its only liveness-adjacent input is the roster, which the runbook itself calls empty-not-absence and which cannot see a headless worker at all, so **roster presence is not liveness**: a roster row matching the task title is a *join*, not a probe. Measured 2026-09-22: ten such rows resolved to **ten ids absent from the session registry**, and the runnable `✅ DONE — CLOSE:` line was printed from that classification alone — twice in one day, byte-identical. The reader now classifies the disk half and states liveness **UNVERIFIED**, emitting `🔧 CLOSE-ME CANDIDATE: <task> [<sid8>]` **carrying no command**; **`/supervisor:manager-loop` and `/supervisor:manager-status` each confirm against the session registry** (`grep -l "<id>" ~/.claude/sessions/*.json` over the task's whole id set — frontmatter `claude_session_id` **and** every `metrics_sessions` id, unanchored — then `ps -p <pid>`) and print the runnable line **only** for a confirmed-live session. This is the shape the sibling bucket already had (*"Flag orphan candidates — never verdicts"*) and the one `sweep-gate.py` already ships, where the disk-only bucket and the registry-gated action line were split from the start. The defect is **mode-dependent** — `tick` and `snapshot` are separate call sites — so both commands carry the clause; a fix in one says nothing about the other. The runbook's § Step 4 Close-me bullet and its § Sweep output action-line contract carry the split in the vault, and its icon legend now states that `🔧 close-me` is a **post-verdict overlay the caller applies**, not a bucket the sweep derives — so a renderer showing `✅ done` for a terminal task whose session is still live is not diverging, it is showing the pre-verdict cell. ⚠️ **Two things this does not fix, recorded rather than claimed:** a finished **headless** worker whose session is still live still emits no close line, because the registry cannot see headless workers (diagnosis settled by *a separate task*; the recording change is owned by *a separate task*); and the gate's own `action_lines()` keeps emitting its registry-gated line, which is correct — its verdict is genuinely made, not guessed.

## v0.50.1

- fix: **`/supervisor:fleet-loop` routes the needs-input / BLOCKED set into its batch from the numbered flow, not only from the Cadence checklist.** The command described its escalation twice with different scopes: Step 5 and the Output shape scoped it to the sweep's `stalled`/`parked`/`orphan` buckets and to a grouped *report*, while the `AskUserQuestion` batch over the BLOCKED bucket and the `asked-ledger` `claim` that gates it lived only in § Cadence's **Needs-input** bullet list. A round could therefore walk Steps 0–6 and its Output shape to the letter, render the BLOCKED section, report the blocked set by name — and batch nothing, claim nothing. Measured 2026-09-24 on the Fleet Manager session (`64b4a415`), round digest `2026-09-24T16:21:23Z`: the digest emitted a `BLOCKED (feed, raised — not verified open)` heading with 6 rows and the manager's report carried a "Still waiting on you (5)" section, while **zero** `AskUserQuestion` / `asked-ledger` `tool_use` invocations occurred across the 16 span transcripts and `~/.claude/state/asked-ledger.json` was never written. Step 5 is now **an ask and a report** — the blocked set as an ask (pointing at § Cadence's **Needs-input**, which owns the protocol and is not restated), the sweep's own findings as the grouped report — and the Output shape gains a **Needs-input** item that is **never omitted**, so `:70`'s reference to "the needs-input section" resolves instead of naming a section that did not exist. The asymmetry was the tell: `commands/manager-loop.md:199` already carries the same batch inline in its Procedure step 3 (Act), so the worker layer wired it and the fleet layer did not.

## v0.50.0

- fix: **`manager-sweep-reader` carries no bucket count, and no longer readies a blocked task — the bucket set is now vault-relative.** The agent is vault-agnostic (`/supervisor:manager-loop` and `/supervisor:manager-status` both dispatch to it in either vault), yet it hard-coded a bucket count in **five** places (`:3` *"canonical 7-bucket"*, `:82` *"Step 4's canonical seven"*, `:92` *"not an eighth bucket"*, `:94` *"exactly one of the seven"*, `:269` *"the canonical seven"*) while the another vault runbook had moved to **eight** (`blocked-upstream`, added 2026-09-23) and the primary vault's runbook was still at seven and says *"this is the canonical set; there is no second one."* A fixed number is wrong in one vault whichever number is chosen, so the count is **deleted rather than renumbered**: the file now names no number and defers to the runbook, which its own `<constraints>` already make canonical. That is the rule `agent-command-development-guide.md` § `agent-cmd/single-source-of-truth` requires, and the one the file already stated at `:82` (*"read the bucket set … in runbook § Step 4; do not work from a list here"*) while `:94`/`:269` contradicted it — so the repair is a deletion of the copy, not a substitution. `blocked-upstream` joins the classification, marked **one-vault-only until the reverse-port lands**; an unconditional bullet would assert in the primary vault a bucket its runbook does not define. Separately, and independently of the frame: **the ready-to-start predicate never checked whether a blocker had shipped.** It read *"`blocked_by` shipped, or the task was `next` with a free slot"*, and the second arm admitted any `next` task without reading its blocker's status — under `manager-drive`'s standing spawn mandate such a row is **opened**, not merely listed. Measured 2026-09-24: `BRO-22019` bucketed ready-to-start while its blocker `BRO-22018` was still `next`, as did the out-of-set control `a sample task`, whose blocker carries `goals: []`. The bare free-slot arm is gone from the agent; the predicate now requires **every declared `blocked_by` to read `completed`**, and a task declaring none satisfies that **vacuously**, so blocker-less work stays startable. ⚠️ **The two runbooks differ on this clause, and the agent reports rather than papers over it.** another vault states the check verbatim (`70 Runbooks/Manager Session.md:155`); the primary vault's `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md:131` still carries the loose two-arm wording (*"…or it was `next` with a free slot"*), so in that vault **the runbook wins** — the agent classifies such a row `ready-to-start` and reports the disagreement, because silently applying the tightened reading there would be the agent overriding its own canonical source. Three primary-vault tasks are in exactly that state today. **Closing it is a primary-vault runbook edit and is deliberately not made here** — this change's scope is the plugin text, and the reverse-port owns the runbook; the entry records the divergence rather than claiming a fix it does not deliver. ⚠️ **Read the blocker's `status`, never merely whether the list is present** — a presence test over-blocks in the other direction and quietly empties the bucket. The same *"seven-bucket"* phrase is corrected in `agents/manager-drive.md`, `commands/manager-drive.md` and `commands/manager-loop.md`. `commands/manager-verify.md`'s *"not one of the seven"* is deliberately untouched: it counts that command's **steps**, not buckets.

## v0.49.0

- feat: **the headless/interactive decision now reaches every new-worker spawn site, and the rule gets exactly one home.** The classifier shipped in `commands/open.md` § Step 0.6, but it ran **only** on the `--flagged` batch: a bare `/supervisor:open "<task>"` reached Step 3 with no classification at all, omitted the `interactive` argument, and let the fleet config pick the tab. Measured 2026-09-23 — **63 new-worker spawns in one day and 0 of them headless**, 57 of them sourced from `config` rather than from any decision. The rule now lives once, as § Spawn a worker **item 6** in `docs/fleet-surface.md`, joining the readiness bar and the spawn cap as that block's third constant; `commands/open.md` keeps the storage and the write and points at it rather than restating it, `commands/manager-loop.md` gains a mode check at its gap-7 spawn site, and `commands/manager-spawn.md` routes through the same rule. The block's § A now carves out `mode: headless` explicitly instead of forbidding `interactive` on every fresh spawn, so the single home no longer contradicts itself two paragraphs apart. `scripts/check-spawn-mode.py`, wired into `make precommit`, fails when a new-worker spawn site loses either half — the reference to the home or the `mode: headless` → `interactive=false` binding — and when a new spawn site appears unwired; resumes are excluded by keying on `resume=` rather than `prompt=`, because `commands/fleet-loop.md` continues an exited headless worker with **both** keys and a check keyed on `prompt=` alone would demand the mode decision of a resume.

## v0.48.5

- fix: **the spawn-confirmation prescription drops `last_message` — it read as a liveness signal and is not one.** v0.48.4's correction replaced `.status` with "`agent_status.last_message` and the transcript file's mtime advancing", and a live exercise on 0.48.4 falsified half of it: `last_message` read `null` at 95 and at 106 transcript lines of a running turn. **The reported mechanism was wrong and the conclusion right** — it is not a completion artifact (assistant text arrived at record 107, roughly 40s *before* the turn ended), it is null *until the worker writes assistant text*, and a tool-heavy worker writes none for most of a turn: **11 of the 15 assistant records** in that transcript were `tool_use`-only. So it reads exactly like the idle case, which is the failure this whole defect is about. The prescription now names **the transcript file's mtime advancing** alone, and says plainly why `.status` and `last_message` are each wrong. Found by evidence rather than by audit — the third self-caught error on this defect, and the only one no amount of re-reading could have found.

## v0.48.4

- fix: **the spawn-confirmation prescription now names a signal that actually works — on all four surfaces that carried the wrong one.** `commands/manager-loop.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md` and two manager runbooks told a manager that a headless worker's liveness "comes from `agent_status` (`.status`, `.session_id`)". `.status` is set to `done` on **any** SDK `result` message (`server/supervisor.mjs`), so a resumed worker measured reporting `done` with `num_turns: 0` was read as finished while its transcript kept growing — and the obvious next act, a retry, puts two writers on one conversation, the corruption the resume guard exists to prevent. The prescription now names the **transcript** — `agent_status.last_message` and the transcript file's mtime advancing — and states that `.status` is terminal only once the turn has ended. ⚠️ **The registry is not the alternative**: a same-server headless worker holds no registry entry at all (`server/liveness.mjs` header), so pointing readers there would trade one wrong instrument for another. ⚠️ **`current_tool_call` is not a liveness signal either** — it is non-null only while a `tool_use` is in flight, so a live worker thinking between calls reads `null`; it stays named only for its real job, which call and how long held. § Spawn a worker gains a one-line cross-reference rather than a fifth copy, because a restated sentence is how one wrong field reached four surfaces.
- fix: **the drive leg re-checks every nudge candidate before it sends, so a worker that moved or is mid-tool-call is no longer nudged.** The sweep reads the task files, the caller probes orphan liveness, and only then is `agents/manager-drive.md` dispatched — so a worker that advanced inside that window was classified `stuck` and nudged anyway. Measured 2026-09-23: 7 rows bucketed `stuck`, 4 nudged, and **two of the four replied that the nudge was wrong** — one had updated its task file inside the same minute the sweep read it, and the rule's own authoring session recorded the near-miss (*"branch (b) can nudge a worker that is actively progressing, whenever the sweep's snapshot predates the act pass"*). Two checks now run in the act pass, and either one dropping the candidate means no message. **Freshness** — `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` records each task file's mtime in its existing single read pass and returns it for every `stuck` row as report **section 6**; the drive leg re-reads it (`PATH`-independently, the form clause 7 already mandates, never a bare `stat` flag) and drops any candidate whose file is **newer** than the sweep's reading. **In flight** — the roster's `idle` word is a point-in-time pane status that says nothing about the tool loop inside the session, so the drive leg reads `~/.claude/state/attention/<session_id>.tool.json` and drops a candidate whose call is `open` for under ~20 min, falling back to `scripts/who-needs-me.py`'s `LIVE_WINDOW` transcript reading when no marker exists — reusing that shipped definition and `scripts/fleet-board.py`'s mirror of it rather than adding a fourth one. Both readings **fail closed**: a `stuck` row arriving with no mtime is reported as un-checkable rather than nudged, and an unreadable mtime is never read as fresh. Output gains a `Not nudged` section and a `not nudged` count on the `Drive:` line, so a dropped candidate is visible instead of reading exactly like one the sweep never classified. `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` § Step 5 carries the same rule, because that runbook wins over the agent file on disagreement.

## v0.48.3
